# OpenFlyover — architecture

Phase 1 goal: import a GPX / FIT file, show the 3D terrain (Mapterhorn or AWS Terrarium elevation) draped with
orthophotos, the track draped on the terrain, and an orbit camera. 100% local, no API key.

Phase 2 (camera flyover, timeline, profile): "Flyover" section at the end of this document. Later phases: Takram atmosphere,
full customization through a single project document, WebCodecs video export, Tauri and offline packs (see README).

## Stack

- Vite 8 + React 19 + TypeScript (strict), oxlint.
- three + @react-three/fiber + @react-three/drei, zustand.
- Tests: vitest (node environment; jsdom in the files that need the DOM, `// @vitest-environment jsdom`). `*.test.ts` / `*.test.tsx` files sit next to their modules (about 2,701 tests in 164 files). End-to-end: `npm run e2e` (`e2e/run.mjs`, see "Development rules").
- Texts: in-house runtime in five languages (French, the reference, English, Spanish, Italian, German), no dependency (`src/i18n`, see "Internationalisation").
- Tailwind CSS 4 (build-time Vite plugin, Preflight on) over the CSS variables of the design system in `src/ui/theme.css` (`@theme` block, `dark` variant on `[data-theme='dark']`). The hand-written CSS sits in `@layer base` / `@layer components`, so utilities win; shared `@utility` classes and the type, spacing and control-height scales in `src/ui/utilities.css` and `theme.css` (see "Styling").
- HeroUI 3 (MIT, React Aria inside) for the interactive components, wrapped one by one in `src/ui/primitives/` (see "Primitives").

## Coordinate conventions (see `src/core/types.ts`)

- WGS84 lon/lat in degrees. Scene heights in meters **above mean sea level** (elevation tiles,
  GPX / FIT), placed by the local frame as ellipsoidal heights: the whole scene sits N (geoid undulation)
  too low, which only matters for what reads it in true ECEF. The atmosphere and the clouds therefore take
  `mslLocalToEcef(frame)` (frame raised by N at its origin) and cloud altitudes + N; everything else (terrain, track,
  labels, camera) stays in MSL, consistent with itself. N varies by ~0.4 m / 10 km around Mont Blanc: a constant offset.
- Three.js scene in a **local tangent frame** centered on the trip centroid: +X east, +Y up, +Z south.
  ECEF → local conversions run in JS doubles, never in the shader.
- Web Mercator tiles, XYZ scheme (y = 0 at the north).

### Development rules

- Import types from `src/core/types.ts` with `import type`.
- No React dependency in `geo/`, `import/`, `terrain/`.
- Picking a file, saving a file, reading a drop, keeping a preference: go through `getPlatform()`
  (`src/platform`), never through an `<input type="file">`, a fresh download link or `localStorage`, nor through Tauri
  directly.
- Node 24 may not be in the global PATH. Under WSL it comes from nvm: `source ~/.nvm/nvm.sh && nvm use 24` before `npm`. On
  Windows it is installed with fnm: run `fnm use 24` (or prepend the fnm installation folder of Node 24 to PATH) before `npm`.
- Scripts (`package.json`): `npm run dev` (port 5173), `npm run build` (`tsc -b`, then Vite), `npm run preview`, `npm test` (vitest run; `npx vitest run --maxWorkers=1` on a busy machine), `npm run coverage` (same with coverage), `npm run typecheck` (`tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json`), `npm run lint` (oxlint), `npm run e2e`, `npm run tauri:dev` / `tauri:build`, `npm run notices` (regenerates `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` and the installers' licence pages), `npm run installer-assets` (installer images).
- `npm run e2e` (`e2e/run.mjs`): starts its own Vite server and drives the app in a headless Chromium (puppeteer-core, software GPU) through 21 scenarios run one after the other in the same page (`accueil`, `onglets`, `langue`, `diagnostic`, `couleur`, `reglage-repli`, `poids-de-rendu`, `qualite-export`, `image-cle`, `timeline`, `inspecteur`, `menus`, `heure`, `cartes`, `cles-etiquettes`, `reperes`, `region`, `projet`, `export`, `historique`, `mobile`); any console or page error that is not network noise fails the scenario. Environment: `OPENFLYOVER_CHROME` (Chromium / Chrome executable), `OPENFLYOVER_E2E_SKIP_EXPORT=1` (skips `export`, the slowest, which needs the tile servers), `OPENFLYOVER_E2E_ONLY=a,b` (only those scenario ids). The scenarios read the French interface; they find controls by role, label or `data-testid`, never by styling class.
- CI (`.github/workflows/`): `ci.yml` (pull requests and master: `lint`, `test`, `build` in parallel, `rust` only when `src-tauri/` or the workflow changed, `badge` on master for the README coverage badge), `pages.yml` (website on GitHub Pages), `desktop.yml` (installers; a `v*` tag also publishes a release, see "Desktop application"). No end-to-end tests in CI.

## Loading (bundle splitting)

The first screen (shell, panels, welcome card) loads the app, React and the HeroUI / React Aria primitives, without three.js:
about 1.5 MB, 475 kB gzip over 20 files (the app 328 kB, `primitives` 290 kB, React 219 kB, the React Aria code they share in chunks of their own), instead of a single 3.1 MB file, 930 kB gzip.
Nothing in the first screen may import `three` or `@react-three/fiber` statically: the status bar reads the renderer through
`scene/perfProbe.ts` (a plain module the scene fills when its canvas is created), and the build prints no chunk-size warning.
The rest arrives through dynamic `import()` calls, each in its own file:

- right after the first paint: the 3D scene (`FlyoverCanvas`, three.js + fiber, `React.lazy` in `Stage`) and the
  "Hors ligne" (offline) panel (`React.lazy` in `App`). They stay mounted;
- with the first track: the atmosphere (Takram, clouds, post-processing, geoid grid: `AtmosphereLayer`, `React.lazy`
  in `FlyoverCanvas`, with its own `Suspense` so the terrain shows in the meantime);
- when the export drawer is first opened: the drawer (video, batch, poster: `ExportPanel`, kept mounted afterwards)
  and, through its codec check, mediabunny (~700 kB, 175 kB gzip). Mounting it at startup used to download both
  before the user had done anything;
- on demand: the FIT decoder (`import/fit.ts`, on the first .fit, `import/index.ts`), mediabunny (reading a video,
  export: `film/video.ts`, `export/encoder.ts`), the segments and calendar of the day and time fields (`DateFieldPicker`,
  `TimeFieldSegments`: ~66 kB, 19 kB gzip, `lazy` in `DateField` / `TimeField`, when first shown with a mouse; the browser's own
  input stands in until then and on a touch screen).

To keep three.js out of the first screen, the app imports lon/lat distances and boxes from `geo/lonLat.ts`, not from
`geo/ellipsoid.ts`. React has its own file (`codeSplitting.groups` in `vite.config.ts`), kept in cache from one version to
the next. Lazy files over 500 kB (three + fiber, Takram, mediabunny) are expected: the warning threshold is 800 kB, which no file exceeds since 2.1.0 (the first screen is about 1.5 MB in all, 470 kB compressed).

## On-demand rendering

The scene is drawn only when something changes (`frameloop="demand"`, `src/scene/renderOnDemand.ts`): when
idle, no more frames. A frame is requested on every frame while the film plays, while tiles load
(`engine.stats.pendingTiles`) or while a reframing animates (`CameraRig`); then `WAKE_FRAMES` = 30 frames after any
change in the stores read by the scene (application except `terrainStats` and `loading`, weather, highlighted region,
labels, export), after
a drape (track, water, labels), the loading of the label font or the marker image, and for each
texture from the three loaders (sky, clouds): the time for the temporal upsampling of the clouds to fill
(~16 frames). The clouds ask for frames themselves until a still view has averaged `CLOUD_SETTLE_FRAMES` = 32
frames (16 on a phone or tablet, see "Device budget") (`previewCloudPass`, below). OrbitControls (drei) requests its own frames, damping included. The time step of a frame is
capped at `MAX_FRAME_DELTA_S` = 0.25 s (`frameDelta`), and `wakeScene` resets the clock when the scene was asleep
(`clock.getDelta()`): playback and reframing do not jump after a pause.
The export keeps `frameloop 'never'` and draws its own frames. Measured with software rendering: no more frame requests
once the margin has elapsed (~30 s there, one frame per second; ~0.5 s on a real graphics card). The engine's delays
(retry of a failed tile, unloading) are times, checked at the next frame.

Cloud preview: lightened "bas" (low) preset (`PREVIEW_MARCH`: 120 steps of at least 150 m, 15 for shadows, instead of
200, 100 m and 25), at full resolution, temporally upsampled while the view changes, averaged with the "bas" marches
once it is still (see "Volumetric clouds", Quality); the export goes back to the chosen quality.

## Terrain engine — design

1. **Sources** (`sources.ts`): Mapterhorn elevation `https://tiles.mapterhorn.com/{z}/{x}/{y}.webp` (Terrarium, webp),
   AWS Terrain Tiles `https://s3.amazonaws.com/elevation-tiles-prod/terrarium/{z}/{x}/{y}.png` (Terrarium, z ≤ 15).
   Imagery: IGN BD ORTHO (Géoplateforme WMTS, PM), ArcGIS World Imagery (the default outside France and Switzerland), swisstopo SWISSIMAGE, and the national aerial photos `basemap-at` (Austria, CC BY 4.0, z1–19), `gsi-photo` (Japan, z2–18) and `usgs-imagery` (United States, z0–16), picked by hand only.
   The catalog also holds non-photographic base maps, never picked automatically (`AUTO_IMAGERY_IDS` =
   `ign-ortho`, `swisstopo`): topographic maps `ign-plan` (Plan IGN, France, z19), `swisstopo-carte` (Swiss national
   map, z19; blank outside Switzerland beyond z15) and `opentopomap` (worldwide, z17, CC BY-SA, volunteer server: offline
   packs allowed for personal use only, capped at 2,000 tiles per day, see "Offline packs"). The EOX Sentinel-2 and the three
   dated IGN orthophotos were removed in 2.1.2 (`REMOVED_IMAGERY_SOURCES` of `project/document.ts` opens a saved project that used one
   with Esri World Imagery, resp. IGN BD ORTHO). Rejected sources (key required: Stadia,
   Thunderforest, SCAN 25): `docs/sources.md`.
   « Date des photos » (`settings.imageryDate`, `imageryDates.ts`): '' = the latest, `ign:<year>` = the IGN layer
   `ORTHOIMAGERY.ORTHOPHOTOS<year>` (2000–2024, each only the departments flown that year, white elsewhere at every zoom:
   the dated source's `fallback`, the latest photos, is drawn instead in `loadImageryTexture`), `esri:<release>` = the
   World Imagery Wayback release (`wayback.maptiles.arcgis.com`). `datedImagerySource` gives the engine a source with its own
   id (`ign-ortho@ign:2020`), so a date change reloads the imagery. The dates offered are found on demand at the centre of the
   first track: for IGN the mosaic graphs (WFS `ORTHOIMAGERY.ORTHOPHOTOS.GRAPHE.<period>:graphe_bdortho` for 2000–2023 and
   `…GRAPHE-MOSAIQUAGE:graphe_bdortho` for the latest, 6 requests in a 20 m box: `pva` year and `date_vol` day of the
   flight; « YYYY-01-01 » = year only; a dated layer may also answer 404 where the year did not fly: the fallback too), and for Esri the tilemap of the newest release, whose `select` names the
   release the tile last changed in, walked back (about 20 requests of 116 B in the Alps) plus one metadata query per
   version for the day the photo was taken (`SRC_DATE2`, layer « 1.2m Resolution Metadata »). Kept for the session. The
   poster's flat map and the offline packs keep the latest photos.
   The picker groups them (`groupImagerySources`): photos, then maps (`map: true`), the sources covering the centre of the first
   track first (regional before worldwide), then « Ne couvrent pas la trace ». Covering = inside the `coverage` box and, for IGN
   and swisstopo, inside their country's `outline` (`geo/outlines.ts`, Natural Earth 1:50m simplified to 1.5–2 km, 1.5 km of
   margin): the boxes overlap (Chamonix is in swisstopo's, Geneva in IGN's). The automatic choice (`pickRegionalImagery`) still
   uses the boxes.
   Exact URLs, max zooms, encoding and CORS support are checked empirically (`docs/sources.md`); `sources.ts` is the single source of truth.
   A source without CORS goes through the Vite proxy (`/tiles/<id>/...`, see `vite.config.ts`).
2. **Fetch** (`fetch.ts`): `fetch()` + `createImageBitmap`, priority queue, concurrency ~12, deduplication of in-flight requests,
   LRU ~600 bitmaps (200 on a phone or tablet, "Device budget"), `AbortSignal` support, 3 retries (250 ms, 1 s, 4 s, bypassing the HTTP cache) on network error, 5xx, 429
   and 400 (the IGN Géoplateforme returned bursts of "Layer … unknown" as 400 for valid tiles, with a
   `max-age` of 21 days): a sub-tile that still fails makes its terrain tile's imagery incomplete (next item). Before the
   network, the copy from an offline pack when a pack contains the URL's source (see "Offline packs").
   **One fetcher for the session** (`TerrainLayer`, `sharedFetcher`): the engine is rebuilt when the tracks or the
   « Depuis la région » shot change, and the decoded tiles outlive it; emptied when no track is left. The export uses
   the preview's engine, so it starts from the tiles the preview loaded (only the finer levels of a film taller than the
   preview canvas are new). Below it, the browser's HTTP cache under each server's rules (Mapterhorn and OpenTopoMap
   7 days, IGN 21 days, Esri 1 day, swisstopo 1 h then an ETag check): no tile store of our own outside the offline packs,
   which the providers' terms govern (`docs/sources.md`).
3. **DEM** (`dem.ts`): Terrarium `h = (R*256 + G + B/256) - 32768`; Mapbox `h = -10000 + (R*65536 + G*256 + B) * 0.1`.
   Decoding through `OffscreenCanvas` (fallback `HTMLCanvasElement`). Bilinear sampling.
4. **Imagery** (`imagery.ts`): for the terrain tile (z,x,y) and `zoomOffset = k`, fetch the `2^k × 2^k` sub-tiles
   at z+k and draw them into a `tileSize·2^k` canvas → `CanvasTexture` (sRGB, mipmaps, max anisotropy).
   If z+k > the source's maxZoom: take the deepest available tile and crop it.
   Each sub-tile is drawn as soon as it arrives (the fetcher's LRU closes evicted bitmaps: one that waited for its
   siblings could no longer be drawn; a closed one is asked for once more). A **plain white sub-tile** of a regional source
   (`source.coverage`: IGN answers one, HTTP 200, outside France from z13, `docs/sources.md`; 8 × 8 samples of the
   decoded bitmap all ≥ 250) is replaced by the same ground from the nearest coarser tile that is not white (up to 6
   levels, z12 mosaic for IGN): the terrain no longer turns white, and no longer flips to the coarse colours and back when
   the level of detail changes. If a sub-tile fails: the area is gray and
   the texture carries `userData.incomplete`; the engine tries the tile again (below). Rejects only if all fail.
5. **Mesh** (`mesh.ts`): `(segments+1)²` grid; for each vertex, fractional Mercator position → lon/lat →
   bilinear height (DEM grid, height × exaggeration) → ECEF → local through `frame.toLocal`. UV (0,0) = NW corner... careful:
   in Three.js `uv.y = 1` is the top of the image; row 0 of the image tile is the north → `uv.y = 1 - v`.
   Normals computed from the grid (before adding the skirts). Skirts: a copy of the outline lowered by `skirtDepthM`.
6. **Quadtree / LOD** (`quadtree.ts`, `engine.ts`):
   - Roots: all tiles of the root zoom covering `area` (root zoom chosen for ≤ 16 tiles, ≥ terrain.minZoom).
   - Geometric error of a tile ≈ `tileGroundSizeM / segments`.
     Screen error `sse = geometricError * viewportH / (2 * distance * tan(fov/2))`, distance = camera → bounding sphere.
   - Refinement by **replacement**: the parent stays displayed until its 4 children are ready (no holes).
     A child outside the frustum counts as ready for replacement (but is loaded at low priority).
   - Loading priority: decreasing sse. A tile is "ready" when DEM + texture are loaded: it is never drawn before its
     imagery is composed. Imagery that failed (or `incomplete`) fails the node like a DEM failure, so the parent keeps
     its own imagery meanwhile; the last attempt keeps what arrived (gray material or patches, `node.imageryMissing`).
   - Transient DEM failure (network, 5xx, corrupt tile): 3 attempts 5 s apart (`retryDelayMs`, engine clock `deps.now`),
     counted in `failedTiles`. In the preview a retry is fetched after every first attempt and prefetch, and the view does
     not wait for it (`pendingVisibleTiles`): unreachable tile hosts would otherwise hold every frame. During a video
     export (`engine.setExporting(true)`) the view waits for every retry: up to `exportMaxLoadAttempts` (8) attempts,
     `exportRetryDelayMs` (1 s) apart, doubling up to `exportMaxRetryDelayMs` (30 s) (1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 30, 30 s: ~1.5 min
     of waits plus the fetcher's own retries, ~2.2 min per tile), fetched at once, counted in `pendingVisibleTiles` until
     the last attempt; tiles that had given up are tried again at the start. Each failed node keeps why (`node.failure`:
     host, layer élévation / imagerie, cause such as « HTTP 503 », « erreur réseau (…) », « imagerie incomplète »); the
     selection lists the visible nodes given up on (`missing`) and those still retried (`retrying`), read by
     `viewTileFailures(engine)` for the export's error. A visible tile given up on (or drawn with imagery missing) is
     counted in `stats.missingVisibleTiles`.
     **Optional cleaning** (`demClean` engine option, setting `demClean { enabled, seaToZero }`, off by default): the source
     DEMs (Mapterhorn and AWS alike) contain isolated spikes over the sea (seen off Corsica), and the 3D track follows the
     DEM. A pixel is replaced by the median of its 8 neighbours when it differs from it by more than 150 m, the median is
     below 20 m and at most one neighbour is within 150 m of it (isolated: capes, cliffs and mountains are never touched).
     `seaToZero` then brings heights below 0 m to 0 m (flattens real depressions, hence a separate option). A change
     rebuilds the tiles like a source change. The elevation computed for a track (`terrain/elevation.ts`) is not cleaned.
     DEM **4xx or outside coverage = "no data" leaf** (Mapterhorn stops at z12 outside high-resolution areas):
     never retried, not counted as an error, the parent stays displayed at its resolution.
   - Unloading: nodes not visited for > 2 s (`unloadAfterMs`; 30 s after a prefetch, `prefetchKeepMs`, so the export's
     tiles for the next frames outlast a slow frame; the export waits up to 5 min per frame) and not ancestors of a visible node → dispose geometry/texture; the `HeightField`
     keeps ~400 grids at 256 px equivalent (LRU, i.e. ~100 MB; a 512 px grid counts as four; 160 on a phone or tablet).
     The texture's retained 2D canvas is released with it (`width = height = 0`: WebKit keeps a canvas's backing store until a GC).
   - **Tile retention during an export on a phone or tablet.** Wall time is the wrong clock there: an export renders a few
     frames a second, so 30 s keep hundreds of film frames of tiles (measured on an iPhone 12: +1.2 tiles per frame,
     68 → 244 loaded tiles in 100 frames, no plateau, tab killed). The export (`ExportController`) calls
     `engine.setRetention({ frame, keepFrames, prefetchKeepFrames })` before every film frame when
     `budget.exportTileKeepFrames > 0` and `setRetention(null)` at the end: `keepUntil` is then a film frame (views
     `exportTileKeepFrames` = 6, prefetches the farthest prefetched frame + 2 × 5 = 30; `keepUntil` is reset when the clock
     changes), so the export's tile waits (updates without a frame) neither hurry nor stretch it. A phone prefetches the
     frames +5, +10, +20 (`exportPrefetchAhead`) instead of +5…+40. Behind it a **hard cap**, `maxResidentTiles`
     (`DEFAULT_TUNING`, from the device budget: 120 phone, 160 tablet, 90 with ≤ 2 GB, unbounded on a computer; also in the
     preview): past it `enforceCap` (every update) unloads the ready tiles the current selection did not visit, least
     recently visited first (a prefetch counts half a frame after the update, so a tile ahead outlasts one behind; deeper first on
     a tie; roots stay), and `prefetch` starts nothing. The tiles of the current selection (drawn, shadow casters, their
     ancestors) are never evicted. A computer sets no retention and no cap: nothing changes there. Estimate behind 120:
     ~2.6 MB a tile (imagery texture 512² with mipmaps ~1.4 MB, its canvas ~1 MB, DEM grid, geometry) = ~310 MB at the cap.
   - **The cap is hard: the level of detail gives way** (phone, tablet). A low view looking at the horizon in a portrait
     film needs 220–390 tiles by itself (iPhone 12: 147 → 224 loaded during the « descente », tab killed), so the
     selection is made to fit: `solveLodLevel` multiplies `errorTargetPx` by `LOD_STEP` (1.1) per level, 0 to
     `MAX_LOD_LEVEL` (28, ×14.4), and keeps the finest level whose selection fits `maxResidentTiles` (bisection: a
     selection only gets smaller with the level). The step is small because the level hovers around the budget (the count
     moves with the tiles loaded, whose bounding spheres are tight, and with the camera): at ×1.25 it flipped between two
     levels every few frames of a zoom and each flip redrew a third of the view (simulated dive of 150 frames: 8 frames redraw
     8 tiles or more, up to 43 % of those in view), at ×1.1 a few tiles at a time (2 frames, 29 % at most). The count is
     `countSelection` (`quadtree.ts`): the nodes the selection visits once everything it asks for is loaded (drawn
     tiles, their ancestors, the culled children of refined nodes, i.e. the shadow casters), readiness ignored, stopped
     past the budget; a few passes over at most ~120 nodes per update. A larger error target coarsens the distant tiles
     first: the ground under the camera stays at the deepest zoom. The cap counts the tiles **loaded or loading**
     (pending loads hold fetches and decode copies): `enforceCap` runs before the loads start and makes room for them,
     cancelling unvisited loads too, and a phone loads 6 tiles at a time (`maxTileLoads`, 12 tablet, 24 computer).
     The preview keeps its level and goes one level finer only when that selection holds within `LOD_RELAX_SHARE`
     (90 %) of the budget, one step per update: no back and forth on a still or slow view. An export (frame retention
     set) solves the level from the view at every update, without memory: a film frame does not depend on how fast the
     frames before it went (tested: the same view after another path gives the same tiles in view). The level still
     reads the tiles' bounding spheres, tight once loaded, like the selection itself. `stats.lodFactor` gives the
     multiplier (1 = full detail). Simulated (fake tiles, 720 px portrait, 300 m, 2° below the horizon, z15): 376–393
     tiles without the cap, 120 at most with it (loaded + loading, every update), level 5–6 once settled (×3.1–3.8),
     about ten one-step changes over 150 frames; the nearest tile stays z15, the farthest drops from z11 to z9, and
     ~40 tiles are drawn in view instead of ~137. Computer: budget unbounded, level 0, selections bit-identical.
     Not done: a lower imagery zoom offset for far tiles (the offset is per engine and chosen at load time; a tile
     coming closer would keep its coarse texture), not loading the culled children on a phone (~45 % of the budget in
     that view goes to tiles out of the frustum, but they are the shadow casters).
   - Bounding sphere: from the geometry if loaded, otherwise from the tile bounds with heights [-500, 9000] m.
   - Material: `MeshStandardMaterial({ map, roughness: 1, metalness: 0 })`, `side: FrontSide`.
   - `sampleHeight` = the terrain **as drawn**: the triangles of the tile drawn at the point (`sampleTileMesh`), else
     (off the view) the deepest loaded tile there, else the `HeightField` (tree rebuilt after a change of imagery).
     The track, the marker and the labels therefore lie on the visible mesh: never on a finer grid the mesh does not
     show yet (or no longer), never on a coarser one or at sea level because the LRU evicted the grid of a drawn tile.
     Reading only the LRU `HeightField` (100 grids of 512 px on a computer, for up to ~390 drawn tiles in a chase view)
     left a 540 km track without elevation with thousands of points on screen at 0 m in most re-drapes and jumps of
     200 to 500 m (p95) between them (simulated flight in Corsica, 0.1.0 included).
   - `onChange` fired (coalesced per frame) when a tile becomes ready or is removed, or the drawn tiles change (a
     refinement from loaded children, a return to the parent) → the track re-drapes.
7. **Scene** (`scene/`):
   - `FlyoverCanvas`: `<Canvas gl={{ antialias: true, logarithmicDepthBuffer: true, alpha: true }} camera={{ fov: 50, near: 1, far: 5e6 }} flat>`
     (no tone mapping: orthophotos are already displayable images), `HemisphereLight` 1.2 + `DirectionalLight` 2.0 (fixed SE sun;
     with three's 1/π shading a flat ground renders ~0.88 of the albedo), glacier sky `#A9CCD9` → paper `#F5F2EA` background as a CSS gradient behind the transparent canvas.
   - `TerrainLayer`: creates the engine in a `useEffect` and disposes it in the cleanup (StrictMode-safe; never in a useMemo), recreates it
     only if the frame changes or if the tracks leave the current `area` (area = `expandBounds(bounds, 25 km, min 40 km)`, sticky; with a region view, that area becomes the
     `detailArea` of a 500 km wider one, where tiles stop at `OUTER_MAX_ZOOM` = 11: `engineAreaFor`),
     adds `engine.group` to the scene through `<primitive dispose={null}>`, `useFrame` → `engine.update(camera, size.height)`,
     pushes a copy of `stats` to the store at ~4 Hz (only if changed), `setOptions` with only the changed keys when the settings change.
     Exposes `{ engine, frame }` through a context to `TrackLines` and `CameraRig` (which must be rendered inside it).
   - `TrackLines` (component; the line objects, draping, colours and styles in `trackLineSet.ts`, no React, so the
     component file keeps React fast refresh): for each track, `Line2` (three/addons/lines) 4 px wide, color `track.color`, densified points (step ≤ 10 m),
     height = `(engine.sampleHeight(lon,lat) ?? pt.ele ?? 0) * exaggeration + 3` (the drawn surface, see above); on a
     flight (`track.flights`) `max(terrain, pt.ele − offsetM) * exaggeration + 3` (`trackHeightM`, `src/flyover/flights.ts`,
     the one rule of the line, the marker, the camera aim, the ghost racers and the track picking). Re-draped on
     `engine.onChange` (150 ms debounce, 600 ms at most while the drawn tiles keep changing).
     Depth-tested only: the relief hides the parts behind it. (A translucent pass without the depth test, meant as a
     faint hint, was dropped: the round caps of the 10 m pieces overlap by several pixels and its alpha piled up into
     an opaque line from a few km away.)
     No start / finish mesh: `Labels` pins them (see "Climbs and labels").
   - `CameraRig`: `OrbitControls` (drei) with damping, `maxPolarAngle = 85°`, `minDistance = 30`, `maxDistance = 400 km`;
     `computeFitView(bounds, frame, groundHeightM)` (`scene/CameraRig.tsx`): target = center, camera to the south-east, pitch 40°, distance = 1.4 × box diagonal (min 2 km).
     On each `change` of the controls (orbit, pan, zoom, damping, fit; not while playing or exporting), `liftAboveGround` raises the
     camera to `FREE_CAMERA_CLEARANCE_M` = 30 m above the exaggerated terrain under it (`engine.sampleHeight`): the tile mesh cuts the
     corners of the elevation grid by a few tens of metres on steep relief; below the flyover's 80 m, so a flyover view is never moved.
     Also when the ground changes under a camera sitting still: on `engine.onChange` (finer tiles, at most once per frame) and on
     a change of the exaggeration, one sample, `wakeScene` only if the camera was lifted (same conditions).
8. **State** (`store.ts`): `tracks: Track[]`, `addTracks`, `removeTrack`, `clearTracks`, `settings { terrainSourceId, imagerySourceId,
   imageryZoomOffset, exaggeration, … }` (the terrain keys; the other groups are described in their own sections), `setSetting`, `terrainStats`, `bounds` (union of the tracks) and `frameOrigin` (centroid of the
   first batch rounded to 0.01°, fixed as long as a track remains); the engine `area` is derived in the scene (`TerrainLayer`).
   `fitRequest` (counter incremented to request a reframing), `loading` (import failures go through the messages, see "Interface"). On import, the imagery switches automatically to
   IGN then swisstopo if the track lies entirely within their footprint, unless the user has already picked a source by hand.
9. **UI** (`ui/`): see "Interface" (shell, tabs, top bar, export drawer, status bar). Labels in
   French or English (see "Internationalisation"). Design system: `src/ui/theme.css`.

## "Carte alpine" (alpine map) design system (CSS variables, `src/ui/theme.css`)

Token set **"Refuge"**: the neutrals are tinted, never grey (warm map paper, blue-black ink), so the shell reads as a
map sheet and not as a generic dark app; one trail-marker red carries every primary action and selected state, with its
full scale (hover, tint, on-ink); glacier and moss, drawn from the subject, are used sparingly for meaning only.
Contrast measured on the computed colours: text ≥ 4.5:1 on every pair, controls ≥ 3:1.

| Role | Variable | Value | Contrast |
|---|---|---|---|
| Panel background | `--color-paper` | `#F6F3EC` | ink 13.6:1 |
| Cards, segmented wells | `--color-card` | `#ECE7DA` | ink 12.2:1 |
| Text, shell | `--color-ink` | `#1B2832` | |
| Menus, toasts, tooltips on the shell | `--color-ink-raised` | `#263540` | white 12.6:1 |
| Secondary text | `--color-ink-soft` | `#4E5C65` | paper 6.2:1, card 5.6:1 |
| Separators | `--color-line` | `#DDD6C6` | decorative |
| Field and control borders | `--color-line-strong` | `#8E8777` | white 3.0:1, paper 3.2:1 |
| Primary action, selection (trail-marker red) | `--color-accent` / `--color-accent-strong` (hover) | `#B9391F` / `#962C15` | white on it 5.7:1 / 7.9:1 |
| Selected tile, chip, current project | `--color-accent-tint` | `#F7DDD3` | ink 11.6:1 |
| Accent on ink (focus, playhead, stops) | `--color-accent-light` | `#FF9B73` | ink 7.3:1 |
| Secondary text on ink | `--color-glacier` | `#A9CCD9` | ink 8.8:1 |
| Information badge on paper | `--color-glacier-deep` | `#2F5D70` | paper 6.5:1 |
| Start, success | `--color-moss` / `--color-moss-light` | `#3A6446` / `#8FC79B` | paper 6.1:1 / ink 7.7:1 |
| Attention (unsaved, tiles in error) | `--color-amber` / `--color-amber-light` | `#7A5200` / `#F0C35E` | paper 6.2:1 / ink 9.1:1 |

Semantic roles over the palette (component CSS uses these): `--surface-chrome`, `--surface-chrome-raised`,
`--surface-panel`, `--surface-card`, `--surface-control`, `--text-1`, `--text-2`, `--text-on-dark`,
`--text-on-dark-2`, `--line`, `--line-strong`, `--accent`, `--accent-hover`, `--accent-tint`, `--accent-on-dark`,
`--status-attention`, `--status-ok`, `--status-error` (each with an `-on-dark` variant), `--shadow-card`,
`--shadow-pop`. Selected states (tiles, chips, switches, sliders, checkboxes) are in the accent; segmented controls
are a card well with a white pill; the only filled accent buttons are the primary action of a view.

Headings: Fraunces; body and buttons: IBM Plex Sans; "diffusion" (broadcast) overlay style: IBM Plex Sans Condensed. Fonts
**bundled** (no network access): WOFF2 in `public/fonts/` (Fraunces variable opsz + wght, IBM Plex Sans variable wght,
IBM Plex Sans Condensed 500/600/700), `latin` and `latin-ext` subsets declared through `unicode-range` in
`src/ui/fonts.css` (imported by `src/main.tsx`); the browser downloads a subset only if a character requires it.
Sources, versions and licenses: `public/fonts/README.md`. `src/ui/fonts.test.ts` checks that every referenced file exists and
that every drawn font (UI, overlay, 3D labels) has its face. Text contrast ≥ 4.5:1.
Track colors (`TRACK_COLORS`) are chosen for legibility over orthophotos, not from the design system.

## Interface

Dark shell (ink) around light panels (paper), in the "Carte alpine" tokens. Styling: Tailwind utilities in the components, the shared `@utility` classes of
`src/ui/utilities.css` and a little hand-written CSS, see « Styling » below. Tokens in `theme.css`: the semantic roles listed above (aliases of the palette; the dark theme
only remaps them), sizes (`--topbar-h` 48, `--rail-w` 64, `--panel-w` 320, `--dock-w` 300,
`--statusbar-h` 24, `--control-h` 32, `--space-6`), `--shadow-card`, `--shadow-pop`, `--focus-ring-on-dark` (accent on
ink, 7.3:1). Dark theme: `:root[data-theme='dark']` in `theme.css` remaps the panel roles (`--surface-panel/card/control`,
`--text-1/2`, `--text-info`, `--line`, `--line-strong`, status and shadows); the bar, rail and status bar are dark in both
themes. Roles added for it: `--accent-fill` / `--accent-fill-hover` (fill under white text), `--text-info`, `--swatch-border`;
`--status-error*` are fixed reds (they no longer follow the accent).
**Primitives** (`src/ui/primitives/`, one file per component, exported from `index.ts`, with a `<name>.css` skin only where the component
needs one; screens never import `@heroui/react`): `Button` (`variant` primary / secondary / icon, `size="sm"`, `block`; it keeps the `.btn` / `.icon-btn`
classes the CSS of the screens keys on), `Tip` (tooltip, see "Tooltips"), `Menu` (list of actions: opened by a trigger, or at a
`point` for the right-click menu of the 3D view; `sheet` makes it a bottom sheet on a phone) and `Popover` (panel with its own
controls, same two modes). Both are portalled out of their container (into the open `Modal`, if any) and carry
`data-local-escape`, so an open one closes before the app's own Escape; their skin is utilities (`overlay-frame`, `overlay-sheet`, `overlay-item` in
`utilities.css`), HeroUI's CSS for them is not imported. `Tabs` / `TabList` / `Tab` / `TabPanel` (the rail: panels kept mounted, the shown one the only `tabpanel`, the others inert and hidden by the skin; the arrows follow `orientation`; a press on the shown tab calls `onReselect`; the DOM ids of tabs and panels are the library's, the screens and tests key on `data-testid`), `Toast` (the messages, see "Messages") and `Disclosure` (the button and folded body of a `SettingRow`). Tabs and Disclosure import no HeroUI CSS: their look is the screen's. The choice controls: `Switch` (a whole feature on
or off), `Checkbox`, `Chip` (one of several choices, a pill ticked when chosen), `RadioGroup` with `Radio` (`look` `segmented`,
`chips`, or `plain` for options the screen draws: the 3 × 3 `AnchorPicker`, the output-format buttons, the proportion and camera-preset tiles; the arrows move and choose, the screens' CSS keys on
`.option__label`; a `Radio` forwards its `ref` for a `Tip`) and `ToggleButtonGroup` (the segmented look for buttons that switch what the screen shows). Their `checked` /
`value` / `onChange` take the values, not events; a `title` is a native tooltip over the whole control; a `<fieldset disabled>`
around them dims them. `Select` (`options` of `{ value, label, disabled? }` or groups of them, `value` / `onChange` on the value, `look` `field` or `bar`) is the browser's own `<select>` on a touch screen (`pointer: coarse`, for its native picker) and a button that opens a list (typed letters, arrows, own Escape, portalled like the menus) elsewhere; one list of options feeds both, and a `<label htmlFor={id}>` names either. The fields: `Slider` (a track and thumb with the value written out in an `<output>`; the range input inside keeps
the arrows, Page Up / Down, Home and End and the screen-reader name, taken from `aria-labelledby` / `aria-label`; a drag on the thumb is
one undo step through `[data-slider]` in `installSliderGestures`, a vertical swipe from the rail scrolls the panel, and `onChange` is
never called twice with one value), `NumberField` (React Aria's, so the entry is settled on Enter or leaving the field, within `min` / `max`
and on the `step`; `live` also applies each number typed in range; Escape puts the stored value back) and `TextField` (`label` above, or
`aria-label`; `type` `password` / `search`, `inputMode`, `labelClassName` for a label on a dark panel); `NumberField` and `TextField` take the project's `.input` look and no HeroUI CSS. `DateField` (a day as `'YYYY-MM-DD'`, `''` for none) and `TimeField` (a time as `'HH:mm'`) are the browser's own `<input type="date">` / `<input type="time">` on a touch screen (`pointer: coarse`, `useCoarsePointer`: the wheel and calendar of the system are made for a finger, as for `Select`) and, elsewhere, React Aria's segments in HeroUI's date input (typed or changed with the arrows, in the language of the application) with, for the day, a calendar over the page (`DatePicker`, portalled into the open `Modal` like the other overlays, `date-panel` skin in `primitives/date-field.css`); `onChange` is called only with a complete value or `''` once every segment is cleared, and the segments (`DateFieldPicker.tsx`, `TimeFieldSegments.tsx`) are loaded when first shown (`lazy`, the browser's input stands in meanwhile), so the date code stays out of the first screen. `Modal` (the dialogs: shortcuts, sources, Strava, diagnostic report; `open` / `onClose` / `title`, the heading is the dialog's name; modal, focus kept inside and given back, Escape and a click on the backdrop close it, it scrolls, full screen on a phone) marks its dialog `data-modal`: `openDialog()` / `isModalOpen()` (`overlay.ts`) find it, so menus, popovers and tooltips opened from inside are portalled into it and the app's own shortcuts wait. `ColorField` (the colour: a button showing it that opens a panel, area / hue slider / hex field, for any colour, bottom sheet on a phone, plus optional `presets` to pick in one tap; the value is a lowercase `#rrggbb`, `onChange` is called while dragging like the native input it replaces; `dot` is the button alone, `children` follow it). Modal and ColorField, like the overlays, import no HeroUI CSS. HeroUI's styles are imported in `theme.css`
piece by piece (base, theme, utilities, then `components/<name>.css` of each primitive in use, then `primitives/<name>.css`, the
app's skin of it), and its theme variables are set there from the roles above (`--background` = `--surface-panel`, `--surface` =
`--surface-card`, `--overlay` = the raised ink of the bar and tooltips, `--default` = `--surface-card`, `--border` = `--line`,
`--field-*` = the control surface and `--line-strong`, `--radius` = `--radius-md`; `--accent` and `--accent-hover` are the
app's own, so a filled accent surface uses `--accent-fill`); `--color-accent` and the radii stay the app's (second `@theme`
block). A Tailwind `@theme` token of HeroUI that the app also defines must keep the app's value.
**Styling** (read this paragraph before touching a screen). Tailwind CSS 4 runs at build time only
(`@tailwindcss/vite`, nothing at run time). `theme.css` imports it and declares the palette, fonts, type and spacing scales, radii and
shadows as `@theme` tokens (`bg-ink`, `text-accent`, `font-heading`, `text-body`, `h-control`…), the semantic roles above, the `dark`
variant (the theme attribute, never the system scheme) and the named variants `touch`, `drawer`, `phone`, `phone-any`,
`phone-landscape` and `bar-condensed` (the breakpoints of the shell, named once). `utilities.css` holds the `@utility` classes
several components share (`type-label`, `type-hint`, `panel-head`, `rail-tab`, `btn-chrome`, `overlay-frame`…), the frames of the
shell (`shell-frame`, `panel-frame`, `dock-frame`, `tabpanel-frame`, `stage-frame`, `dock-section`: a frame that sets defaults for its
children does it with `:where(&) > *`, so the children's own utilities win), the keyframes `tip-in` and `sheet-in`, and the
`timeline-sized` variants. A component carries its layout, spacing and type as utilities in its `className` (a string too long for the
JSX is a `const` above it with a comment) and reads the state attributes of the components (`aria-selected`, `data-selected`,
`data-focus-visible`…), never extra state classes; tests and the end-to-end scenarios find controls by role, label or `data-testid`,
never by a styling class. What stays hand-written (about 2,100 lines with `utilities.css`, in `@layer base` / `@layer components` so that a utility wins):
`src/index.css` (reset and page base), `theme.css` (focus ring, form controls, the dark theme, and at its end what the breakpoints
change in the `:root` variables and in the form controls of a touch screen), `appearance.css` (the page background), `fonts.css` and the skins
`primitives/<name>.css`. `shell.css`, `app.css` and `exportPanel.css` are gone: the shell layout, the panels, the dock, the stage, the
drop veil and the point-of-interest form are utilities in the components that use them. Hooks the code looks for are data attributes
(`data-stage` on the 3D view for the thumbnail, `data-sheet-handle`, `data-collapsed` on the shell body, `data-rail`).
**Appearance** (« Réglages » › « Apparence », `PreferencesPanel`): theme Clair / Sombre / Système, accent colour, bar and rail
colour, reset. Device preferences in `Preferences.appearance` (`openflyover.preferences.v1`, `parseAppearance` in
`appearance.ts`). `applyAppearance(root, appearance, systemDark)` sets `data-theme` on `<html>` and the
properties `--color-accent*`, `--surface-chrome*` (nothing for the default colours). `installAppearance()` (`preferences.ts`,
called first in `main.tsx`) applies it at start-up and on changes of the preferences or of `prefers-color-scheme`; `index.html`
has a tiny inline script for the theme before the first paint. Free colours are clamped: the accent so that white text
keeps 4.5:1, the bar for its secondary text (hint in the panel). Compact: spacing scale ×0.85, `--control-h` 28 px with a fine
pointer (`appearance.css`).

- **Top bar** (`TopBar.tsx`): logo, project name editable in place (`projectName` in the store, empty = name of the
  first track, `effectiveProjectName`) and the saved state, told by the save icon (a dot while modified, the accent colour for 3 s once saved: `data-save-state` `unsaved` /
  `saved` on the button; `savedProject`: settings, tracks and name at the
  last save or the last open, compared by reference, `isProjectDirty`; a project with a track is saved
  automatically in « Mes projets », see "Project (phase 4)"), undo / redo, "Ouvrir" (open, icon only; a single picker: `.opnflvr` / `.json` → project, anything else → track import,
  `routeOpenedFiles`; opening a project clears the history), "Enregistrer" (save); in the center the **output format** ("Libre" (free),
  16:9, 9:16, 1:1, 4:5, 21:9); on the right "?" (shortcuts) and "Exporter" (export), the only primary button of the shell, which opens
  the export drawer and becomes "42 % · Annuler" (cancel) during an export.
- **Output format**: a format writes `settings.video.aspect` (saved, undoable); "Libre" is a preview
  flag (`freeFraming` in the store, neither saved nor undoable). Outside "Libre", `Stage.tsx` frames the 3D view to the format
  (`frameRect`, ink bars): the stage (`stage-frame`, `data-stage`), which holds the 3D canvas, the overlay and the legend, takes the framed
  rectangle; the overlay therefore follows the exported area with no change to `src/overlay`. The export renders into this visible canvas.
  Without a track, no framing: the home screen (`EmptyState.tsx`), a paper page over the whole view (no sky gradient),
  one centred column: what the app does (one sentence and three points: free, no account, everything stays on the device),
  "Choisir un fichier" (choose a file), "Importer depuis Strava", "Essayer avec l'exemple" (try the
  sample), "Ouvrir un projet…" (open a project; a single picker whose `accept` changes), a « Nouveau en <version> » card
  (the first titles of `WHATS_NEW[0]`, opens « Nouveautés » and marks them seen like the « i » button), then "Récents": the three latest
  entries of « Mes projets » but the open one (`useLibraryStore`, kept fresh by the always-mounted Projet tab), and links:
  « Premiers pas » (the user guide, published with the site by `pages.yml` under `docs/`), the desktop installers of the
  latest release (website only) and GitHub.
- **Rail and panel**: vertical rail of icon + label tabs (64 px; `Tabs` primitive: `tablist`, one tab stop, arrows (up / down in the rail, left / right in the phone's bar) and Home / End, a press on the open tab folds the panel) and a
  320 px panel, one tab at a time, nine tabs in montage order (`SHELL_TABS`), one question each: **Trace** (track; track list with "+ Ajouter" (add; a menu: « Fichiers GPX ou FIT… », « Activités Strava… »), waiting message without a track, then the « Trace » line settings, « Marqueur » and « Étiquettes », hidden without a track),
  **Carte** (map; base map and water, relief, OSM landmarks and points of interest),
  **Météo** (weather; weather of the outing, clouds, weather in the image), **Lumière** (light; atmosphere, shadows,
  exposure, sun, colour grading), **Survol** (flyover; camera, pacing), **Objectif** (lens effects),
  **Habillage** (overlay), **Projet** (project; the open project's actions, « Mes projets », presets, offline packs), **Réglages** (preferences of the app on this
  device, not of the project: `src/ui/preferences.ts`, `openflyover.preferences.v1` in `getPlatform().storage`, parsing
  `parsePreferences` in `shell.ts`; not shown on the home screen). All tabs stay mounted (`hidden`): weather, landmarks and export have
  side effects. `App` builds their contents, the stage and the timeline once per track state (`useMemo`, the timeline
  also per `sheetOpen` and the phone layout): a tab switch re-renders the shell (tab bar, top bar, status bar), not the nine panels nor the
  3D scene (see "Phone and tablet"). Flat sections separated by a rule, sticky section header (title + the ↺ "modifié" reset button, `ModifiedMarker`). A click
  on the open tab, the button at the bottom of the rail or `[` collapses the panel. Tab and collapsed state are remembered by the browser
  (`getPlatform().storage` `openflyover.shell.v1`, `parseShellPrefs`: an unknown tab id falls back to Trace), not by the project.
  **Home screen** (no track): the rail holds « Projet » alone, folded, and « Exporter » is hidden (`ShellState.home`,
  `shownTabs`, `tracks` event sent by `App` when the first track arrives or the last one goes): the other tabs, the
  export drawer (Ctrl+E) and the keyboard moves cannot reach a hidden tab. The first track brings the tabs back as last
  left (« Trace » in place of « Projet »); the home screen's state is never remembered.
  The tab list scrolls when it does not fit (short window; phone bar), and `App` scrolls the open tab into view.
- **Right dock** (300 px): non-modal export drawer (`ExportPanel`: formats as tiles, resolution, estimate, "Exporter
  la vidéo" (export the video), sections « Format », « Encodage », « Rendu »; summary duration · frames · codec ·
  size on one line; modes "Vidéo" (video), "Plusieurs formats" (several formats): resolution chips per format, still image, poster,
  batch estimate, "Tout exporter" (export all), progress "2 / 4 · 16:9 1080p · 42 %", file list, and "Affiche" (poster));
  otherwise the **inspector** of the block
  selected on the timeline (`FilmInspector`, two `<aside class="dock">` of which only one is visible: selecting a block brings its inspector over the drawer (the
  `inspect` event closes it, `exporting` keeps it while an export runs), opening the drawer puts it over the inspector,
  which comes back when the drawer closes if the block is still selected). Below 1360 px wide, only one side is open at a
  time: whatever fills the dock (drawer or inspector) collapses the panel, which comes back when the dock empties; expanding the
  panel empties the dock and deselects the block (`shellReducer`: `inspecting`, `inspect` event, which `App` sends when
  `filmSelection` changes, after a press is released: the timeline does not change scale during a drag).
- **Timeline size** (`TimelineResizer.tsx`, pure and tested `layoutSize.ts`: `TIMELINE_HEIGHT_KEY`,
  `parseTimelineHeight`, `timelineHeightRange`, `clampTimelineHeight`): on wide layouts (not the phone layouts), a thin
  separator (`role="separator"`, « Taille de la timeline ») between the 3D view and the timeline. Pointer drag (capture),
  Up / Down (Shift: larger), Home / End (smallest / largest), double-click = default layout. The height is clamped
  (transport row + ruler + « Plans » lane at least; the view keeps at least 35 % of the shared height) and re-clamped on
  window resize. Remembered per device (`getPlatform().storage`, `openflyover.layout.v1`, `{"timelineH": px}`), not in the
  project nor in `preferences.ts`; absent = the timeline takes the height of its content. The handle sets
  `data-timeline-sized` and `--timeline-h` on the view (`<main>`), which the timeline reads through the `timeline-sized` variants (`utilities.css`); the stage resizes through its `ResizeObserver` and R3F invalidates on
  size changes; the export renders its own size. Disabled during an export.
- **During an export**: tabs, panel collapse, format, "Ouvrir", "Recadrer" (reframe), undo / redo (buttons) and
  closing the drawer are disabled (resizing the scene would resize the canvas).
- **View**: floating "Recadrer la vue" (reframe the view) button (F); click on the track and right-click menu (`TrackPicker`, see "Film and
  timeline").
- **Touch gestures**: 3D view, the controls' own touch handling (`OrbitControls`: one finger orbits, two pinch to
  zoom and pan; the controls set `touch-action: none`); a double tap (two one-finger taps within 300 ms and 30 px,
  `useDoubleTapFit` in `CameraRig`, touch only) fits like F; a tap on the track moves the playhead, a long press
  (500 ms, one finger still within 10 px, `TrackPicker`) opens the right-click menu, a second finger cancels both.
  Timeline: blocks, grips and playhead drag with the same pointer events as the mouse (pointer capture, content in
  `touch-action: none`); a second finger is not a press (`isPrimary`) but a pinch, which zooms around the fingers'
  middle and scrolls as they slide (touch listeners on the scroller, `zoomAt`).
- **Safe zones** (`src/ui/SafeZones.tsx`, pure and tested geometry in `src/ui/safeZoneLayout.ts`): floating button below
  "Recadrer" (outside "Libre") or G; preview state (small `useSafeZonesStore` store, neither saved nor undoable, off at
  startup). DOM layer in the framed stage (`data-stage`), above the overlay, `pointer-events: none`: the export (canvas)
  never sees it. 16:9, 1:1, 21:9: action safe 93% and title safe 90% (EBU R 95), dotted. 9:16 and 4:5:
  areas hidden by the interface of vertical feeds (union of Instagram Reels / TikTok / YouTube Shorts measured on a
  1080 × 1920 screen, rounded to the safest value: top bar 220 px, buttons on the right 160 × 720 px, caption at the bottom 440 px),
  hatched; a 4:5 is placed full width in the middle of this screen and only the parts that cover it remain
  (`phoneInterfaceOver`).
- **Messages** (`toast.ts`, `Toaster.tsx`, `primitives/Toast.tsx`): small zustand store (`showToast`, `dismissToast`, pure
  `pushToast`: the same message replaces the old one, 4 at most, the oldest error leaves last), the list of which the
  `Toast` primitive (HeroUI Toast, its queue kept in step with the list) shows at the bottom center of the view,
  above the timeline, in a single always-mounted `aria-live` region. Success and info disappear after
  `toastDuration` (5 s, up to 12 s for a long text), timers paused while the pointer or the focus is on the messages;
  errors as `role="alert"`, until closed; optional action button. Sources: import (`importFiles` from `importFlow.ts`, bound
  to the store by `projectActions.ts`: "Trace « … » importée", failures), project opened / saved / file
  warnings, presets (saved, "Préréglage appliqué : …"), export ("Vidéo prête · Télécharger à nouveau", "Image
  prête", failure, "Export annulé"), "Réglages remis par
  défaut · Annuler" (`resetSettings` returns a guarded undo: it only acts if the settings are still those it
  returned, so never on a later or earlier step; the message disappears on the next settings change).
  Also photos added to the timeline ("Placer sur le parcours" (place on the route) as the action).
- **Drop anywhere**: `dragover` / `drop` on `window` (in `App`): `.gpx` / `.fit` files imported, `.opnflvr` (or an older `.json`) opened
  as a project (`openFiles`, routing `routeOpenedFiles`), full-screen veil "Déposez vos traces GPX ou FIT" (drop your GPX or FIT tracks)
  (`pointer-events: none`) during the drag (`isFileDrag`). A drop already handled (`defaultPrevented`: the timeline
  takes photos) is left as is, and the veil fades out over the timeline; nothing is accepted during an export.
- **Timeline bar**: timecode `formatTimecode` (minutes on two digits) and fixed-width readout values: the bar never
  wraps differently while the film plays, so the view never resizes. The recorded clock time only with the « Réglages »
  preference. Tool buttons are icons only (tooltip and `aria-label`), « Options » included. During an export the
  timeline keeps the playback it had (it does not follow the export's playhead, so it does not re-render per frame).
- **Status bar** (24 px, below the timeline): map state of the current view (`ui/mapStatus.ts`, pure): a ring and
  "Carte · 72 %" (drawn tiles over drawn + awaited ones, `pendingVisibleTiles`; never back during one load, at most
  99 % until complete; a load under 0.5 s does not show), then "Carte prête" (map ready) with a check, "N tuiles en
  erreur" (N tiles in error) in yellow with a tip; nothing before the first tile; not a live region, the ring is a
  `progressbar`. Import in progress; chips for the other fetches (Météo / Repères / Région / Eau from `useWeatherStore`,
  `useLandmarkStore`, `useRegionStore`, `useWaterStatusStore` in `scene/waterStatus.ts`; `fetchChip` in `mapStatus.ts`;
  spinner / check / attention with the store message / muted); a performance readout when the bar is at least 900 px
  wide (`perfParts`: frames per second counted with R3F `addAfterEffect`, textures and geometries of the renderer
  through R3F `_roots`, loaded tiles, JS heap where `performance.memory` exists, desktop RAM and CPU through the Tauri
  command `system_stats`), each part chosen in « Réglages »; then the short credits of the active sources, always
  visible (`compactCredits`, `SOURCE_CREDITS`; OpenStreetMap first and linked to its copyright page; OpenTopoMap and
  Esri never collapsed, Esri's providers behind « … » below 560 px: OSMF, OpenTopoMap and Esri ask for a credit visible
  without interaction), and ⓘ, which opens "Sources et licences" (sources and licenses) (`Modal`) with the full lines
  (`overlayCredits`, same strings as in the film), licence links, and for IGN layers the update date of the data read
  from the tiles' `Last-Modified` (`terrain/sourceDates.ts`, Licence Ouverte Etalab 2.0) or « date de mise à jour non
  communiquée par le service ». The window also holds the link to the GitHub repository and the « Voir les nouveautés »
  button, which opens « Nouveautés » (`WhatsNewDialog`): what changed, in the words of the users and by version, newest
  first (`ui/whatsNew.ts`: `WHATS_NEW`, an icon, a title and a sentence per item, the texts in the `news` domain of the five
  languages; a minor version stands for its patches). A dot on the ⓘ button shows until the newest version was opened
  (`Preferences.whatsNewSeen`, compared with `LATEST_NEWS`): adding a version means its items first in `WHATS_NEW` and
  its keys in the five `news.ts` files.
- **Shortcuts** (`src/ui/shortcuts.ts`): `SHORTCUTS` registry (id, keys, label, group Lecture / Montage / Projet /
  Vue (playback / editing / project / view)) shown by the "Raccourcis clavier" (keyboard shortcuts) box (`HelpDialog.tsx`, `Modal`, "?" or the bar button)
  and reused in tooltips (`withShortcut`). `matchShortcut(key, keyFocus(target))`, pure: Ctrl/Cmd+S save,
  Ctrl/Cmd+O open, Ctrl/Cmd+E export drawer, F reframe, G safe zones, S stop at the marker, T text at the playhead, `[` collapse
  and `?` help (AltGr / Shift accepted), Esc, ← / →
  (Shift: 5 s, `seekTime`), Home / End, Alt+← / Alt+→ previous / next keyframe (`seekKeyframe`, default prevented: it is
  also the browser's « back »); nothing in a text field or a list (`isTextEntry`), and no arrows or Home / End
  on a control that uses them (slider, radio, tab, timeline ruler). No digit shortcut (AZERTY). No Ctrl+N (browsers keep it for a new window). `App`
  listens in the capture phase (no shortcut while a modal box is open); Esc closes, in order, the (native)
  box, the export drawer, then the timeline selection (also from an inspector field); an open menu
  (timeline "Options", right-click on the track: `data-local-escape`) closes itself first. Moving
  the playhead and S / T live in `SeekShortcuts` (component with no render output, film clock, bubble phase: a selected
  block keeps its arrows).
  `installHistoryShortcuts` (Ctrl/Cmd+Z, Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+Z, Ctrl+Y) and Space (timeline) stay where they are.
- **Tooltips**: the `Tip` primitive (`src/ui/primitives/Tip.tsx`, HeroUI Tooltip), after 450 ms, on hover and keyboard focus,
  on every icon-only button; it wraps no element (its listeners sit on the child, the text is portalled, into the `Modal` that holds
  the child). `side` (`top`, `left`, `right`) and `align` (`start`, `end`) near the edges of the window; any other tip is
  shifted inside the window or flipped by React Aria's positioning. A long press shows it on a touch screen (`tap`: a tap too, for
  an ⓘ), any key (Escape included, which the app still gets) or a press closes it, no tooltip on the phone's tab bar. Timeline blocks
  keep their `title` (full label).
- **Widths** (the variants of `theme.css` in the `@utility` frames of `utilities.css`, and the media block at the end of `theme.css`; the desktop layout from 1100 px up is unchanged by the touch
  rules, which only match a coarse pointer): at 1280 px the panel and dock shrink to 280 px and "Ouvrir / Enregistrer"
  become icon-only; below 1024 px (tablet) the panel, the export drawer and the inspector become side sheets over the view
  (rail and timeline kept).
- **Phone and tablet** (`BOTTOM_SHEET_QUERY` and `COMPACT_QUERY` in `shell.ts`, same strings as the CSS):
  - *Compact* (`max-width: 699px` or `max-height: 499px`, a phone either way up): top bar reduced to the logo, the
    project name, « Plus d'actions » (⋯, `MoreMenu` in `TopBar.tsx`: undo, redo, open, save) and « Exporter » with its
    label; no format picker (the drawer tiles) nor « ? »; in landscape the timeline starts folded (`Timeline`: strip
    with play and time) and unfolds as a sheet over the view; panel and drawer sheets fold it (`sheetOpen`), its
    unfolding folds them (`onUnfold` → shell event `fold`): one sheet at a time. Dialogs (`Modal`) are full screen. Safe areas
    (`viewport-fit=cover` in `index.html`, `env(safe-area-inset-*)` on the shell rows and sides), `100dvh`, no page
    overscroll. A touch tablet in portrait (`max-width: 1023px` and `pointer: coarse`) also takes the « Plus d'actions »
    menu.
  - *Portrait phone* (`max-width: 699px`, after CapCut; `App` reads it with `usePhone`): from top to bottom the top
    bar, the 3D preview (`--phone-preview-h` 42dvh, `Stage.tsx`, only with a track), the timeline's bar as a
    slim transport row (play and time, then the « Arrêt », « Vitesse », « Texte », « Média », speed and « Options » icons in a row
    that scrolls sideways, `data-testid="timeline-tools"`; no ■, readout, zoom nor fold), the timeline (`Timeline` `centred`, below),
    the bottom tab bar (all nine tabs, 72 px wide at least so the next one peeks past the edge, scrolling sideways),
    the status bar. Panel, export drawer and inspector are half sheets from under the transport row to the status bar,
    over the timeline and the tab bar, never over the preview (sliding in, not under `prefers-reduced-motion`). Their
    head (`SheetHandle.tsx`: grip, tab name, « Terminé » ✓) closes them on ✓ or when dragged down past a third of
    their height, a flick included (`dismissSheet`); the ✕ of the drawer and the inspector is hidden. Sheet content
    scrolls inside (`overscroll-behavior: contain`); a press on the view does not close a sheet (it does on a tablet
    side sheet). A selected block (`inspecting`) replaces the tab bar with its actions (`BlockTools` in `App`:
    « Terminé » deselects, « Modifier » opens `FilmInspector` in the half sheet (`editing`, `edit` event; ✓ closes it
    and keeps the selection), « Supprimer », « Sans plan » for the opening and the closing, `removeFilmItem`).
    Landscape phone: the rail stays on the side, without its fold button; the timeline strip as above.
  - *Centred timeline* (`Timeline` `centred`, portrait phone): the lanes carry half their width of blank at both
    ends, the playhead is a line fixed in the middle (drawn on the body of the lanes), so the horizontal scroll is the film
    time (`centredScrollLeft`, `centredTime` in `film/timeline.ts`): a layout effect after every render scrolls to the
    playhead (and remembers it), any other scroll (finger, momentum, wheel) seeks. The content takes `touch-action:
    pan-x pan-y`, so the browser scrolls under the finger; a press is a tap if released within 8 px (a tap on a block
    selects it, elsewhere it seeks to the tapped time); the selected block takes `touch-action: none` and drags and
    stretches as before. A touch pauses the playback. A pinch zooms around the playhead (×4 at first, so a 4 s stop
    is ~80 px wide); lane names hidden, lanes 36 px.
  - *Tab switch cost* (October 2026, headless Chromium, 390 × 844 at DPR 3, touch, CPU throttled 4×, sample loaded,
    scene idle): the sheets lie over the view, so a tab switch, an opening or a fold of the sheet never resizes the 3D
    canvas nor draws a frame. Before the `useMemo` in `App`: 437 components re-rendered and the R3F scene reconciled
    (two commits), 45 to 70 ms of click handling (one long task); after: 62 components (the tab bar, top and status
    bars), 18 to 37 ms, no long task. Left: style and layout of the shown panel (« Carte » is the largest, 10 to 15 ms
    at 4×). No `backdrop-filter` or CSS filter on the sheets or the tab bar; the sheet's `--shadow-pop` is the one
    large blur. Not measurable here: WebKit's own paint and compositing costs (iPhone).
  - *Touch* (`pointer: coarse`, any width): `--topbar-h` 56 px, `--control-h` 44 px and 44 px minimum on buttons, fields, chips, segmented
    options, tiles, menu items and timeline buttons; lanes 40 px, grips 16 px; a larger invisible hit area around ⓘ;
    16 px text in fields (iOS zooms into smaller ones); no callout or selection on the 3D canvas.
  - *No hover* (`hover: none`): tooltips do not stick after a tap (`Tip` ignores touch hover); an ⓘ shows its sentence when tapped
    (`tap`), a long press shows any other tip. Every other `:hover` style sits in `@media (hover: hover)`,
    in place (same cascade on a computer): no grey background or border left on a button after a tap.
- **Sections of the tabs** (`PanelSection.tsx`): `PanelSection` = flat collapsible section (`fold-head` utility,
  sticky header: title, the ↺ "modifié" reset button of its keys, chevron), open initially, then as last left: open / folded
  state remembered by title in the platform storage (`FOLDS_KEY` `openflyover.folds.v1`, `parseFoldPrefs` in `shell.ts`,
  read once on mount, written on `toggle`; without storage or with an unreadable value every section opens; a title no longer used is ignored). Carte: "Fond de carte" (base map)
  (imagery, water), "Relief" (exaggeration), "Repères et points d'intérêt" (`LandmarkPanel`, absent without a track).
  Trace: « Trace » (line settings, track colouring included; `TrackStyleSection`), « Marqueur » (`MarkerSection`). The 3D labels are all in « Habillage » › « Étiquettes 3D » (`LabelSettings`, `LabelColors`, the `labels` keys). Réglages: « Apparence », « Barre d'état », « Timeline » (`PreferencesPanel`).
  Météo: "Météo de la sortie" (`WeatherPanel`), "Nuages" (clouds), "Pluie et brume" (weather in the scene and its intensity;
  `WeatherSettings`). Lumière: "Atmosphère" (atmosphere, shadows, exposure), "Soleil" (sun time and date; `LightSettings`), « Jours et nuits » (`NightSettings`: « Montrer les nuits », « Crépuscule » / « Nuit » / « Aube » in seconds, « Cartes de jour »: switch, informations, titre, position, taille, durée, « Vue haute pendant la nuit »; `film.dayCards`),
  "Couleurs" (`GradingPanel`). Survol: "Caméra" (camera; preset tiles, each implying a style, north up),
  "Durée et rythme" (duration and pacing; duration with « − » / « + » buttons (one slider notch) around the slider and the acceleration over the recorded time (`formatSpeedUp`: elapsed time start to end, nights included, over the duration; hidden for an untimed track), film duration, slow-downs on / off, « Plan de situation » switches for the opening and the closing). Objectif: "Effets d'objectif" (`LensPanel`).
  The two or three essential settings of a section stay open; every other one is a `SettingRow`: one button row with
  its name and current value (iOS Settings style, `aria-expanded`), whose control stays hidden (mounted, `hidden`)
  until the row is pressed, then unfolds under it and takes the focus (the checked radio, else the first field); the
  value turns bold when one of its `paths` departs from the default (`modifiedPaths(settings, paths)` from
  `project/apply.ts`, paths `'key'` or `'key.field'`); the section's ↺ button resets it. A lone field in a row hides its
  plain label (the row names it). Rows: imagery detail, terrain source, water strength, track colouring, smoothing, width,
  dash, marker size, km markers, label size and range, landmark kinds and distance, sea render, low cloud base, cloud quality, weather intensity, exposure, sun date,
  the four grading sliders, flare, bloom radius, camera style, distance / tilt / aim / smoothing, pacing parameters, transitions; export frame rate, quality, still type. Setting
  keys and behavior unchanged. `InfoTip` (ⓘ, one-sentence `Tip`, focusable, `aria-label`) only
  on jargon: exaggeration, exposure, highlights.
- **Render weight** (`src/ui/renderCost.ts`, pure, tested; `RenderWeight` and `TabWeightDot` in `PanelSection.tsx`): which settings lengthen
  the export, as a pictogram (three bars like a signal icon: filled navy, the rest periwinkle; the heaviest tier in chartreuse on a navy
  chip; `--color-navy` / `--color-periwinkle` / `--color-chartreuse` in `theme.css`) after the name of the setting (`weight` prop of
  `SettingRow` and `RangeField`, export drawer included), and a chartreuse dot on a navy ring on a tab (rail and phone bar) while one
  of its settings is at tier 3 (`tabHasHeavySetting`). Both read the store with their own selector (primitives), so the memoised panels
  and the 3D scene do not render again; `Tip` tooltip with the factor, visually hidden « Poids de rendu : léger / moyen / lourd »
  (the dot adds « réglage lourd actif » to the tab's name). Tiers come from the factors of the code; « ≈ ×N » only where N is one:

  | Setting | 1 bar | 2 bars | 3 bars |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | Clouds (atmosphere on, not « Aucun ») | « Nappe » (one draw call), « Rapide » (16 slices) | « Moyenne » (24 slices) | « Fine » (32 slices) |
  | Motion blur (« Flou de bougé ») | | « Standard » export quality: up to 4 renders per frame (≈ ×4) | any amount: up to `SHUTTER_SUBFRAMES` = 8 renders per frame (≈ ×8) |
  | Depth of field | on | on with the shutter (repeated at each of its sub-frames) | |
  | Resolution (export drawer; short side, the phone cap applied) | 720p | 1080p (≈ ×2,25 pixels) | 1440p (≈ ×4), 4K (≈ ×9) |
  | Frame rate (export drawer) | 24, 30 i/s | 60 i/s (≈ ×2 frames) | |

  The weights are those of the effective export: the clouds' quality and the sub-frame cap are the ones the export quality picks
  (« Qualité d'export » below), and the tooltip says « imposé par la qualité … ». The shutter's amount does not change its weight
  (the radial streak alone exists only in the preview).
  The default project (« Moyenne », no lens effect, 1080p 30) has no tier 3, so no dot. Bloom, flare and shadows are not shown (one
  cheap pass per render). These weights are estimates derived from the code, to recalibrate on a real GPU (never measure the export
  speed on SwiftShader).
- **Lumière**: « Source de la lumière »: « Heure de la trace » (follow the track; = `sunFromTrack`, disabled with an explanation
  without timestamps; « À la suite »: every stage at its own recorded hour, the light blending across the fade junctions, see
  "Several tracks") / « Heure fixe » (one fixed time and day for the whole film, every stage included). The film track
  (`useFilmTrack`: the sequence for « À la suite ») gives the day bar and the start time. « Jours et nuits » is one switch
  (the seconds stay here in a sequence too, where they apply to the `nuit` junctions, whose inspector shows them as well; « Cartes de jour » is hidden there: the stage cards do it, with the same figure chips, `JunctionCard.fields`). In fixed time: solar-hour slider above a night / dawn / day / dusk bar with the
  sunrise and sunset marks (`solarDay` at the origin of the local frame and on the UTC day of the start of the first track, like the
  scene; standard 6 am – 6 pm day without a track); the text under the bar gives sunrise and sunset in local time when the
  first track knows its UTC offset (`Track.utcOffsetMin`, `clockHourOfSolar`), with the solar hour in the ⓘ,
  otherwise in "heure solaire" (solar time) only (no time zone database), and six shortcuts "Lever" (sunrise; first quarter hour after sunrise), "Matin" (morning)
  (halfway to noon), "Midi" (noon), "Heure dorée" (golden hour; 1 h before sunset), "Coucher" (sunset; last quarter hour before),
  "Nuit" (night; solar midnight), grayed out when the moment does not exist (polar day or night). Each shortcut is one undo
  step (`transaction`); a slider drag is one too (see "History").
- **Panel clarity**: `Switch` to turn on a whole feature (overlay and each of its elements, atmosphere, weather, landmarks, ghost
  race, slow-downs, credits); checkable chips (`Chip`: check mark and tinted background once
  selected, dotted if unavailable) for multiple choices (landmark types, counters); option text at weight 500,
  labels at 600. Météo: weather on two lines (outing; marker instant), tables collapsed under
  "Détails" (details); ghost race synchronization and ranking only once it is enabled. Habillage (`OverlayPanel`)
  in `PanelSection`: "Habillage" (switch, style, "Couleurs et polices" (colors and fonts) collapsed; the only ↺ "modifié" button,
  for all of `overlay`), "Titres" (titles), "Compteurs" (counters; and "Classement (course fantôme)" (ghost race ranking) from two tracks), "Profil et mini-carte" (profile and mini-map), "Météo, logo et texte" (weather, logo and text; absent when the overlay is off), "Crédits des
  sources" (source credits; always). Each element (`WidgetGroup`) has its switch, then its position (`WidgetPosition`: 3 × 3
  grid `AnchorPicker`, then "Position libre" with two offsets in pixels of the exported film, labelled from the anchor; `OffsetField` keeps a draft
  while focused, one undo step per edit),
  then up to three tabs, only those it has: "Contenu" (content: `children`), "Style" (`style`: size, the logo's opacity,
  then its own colors and fonts) and
  "Visibilité" (visibility: `visibility`, e.g. the display time of the opening card). `PanelSection` accepts sections
  without `keys` (no marker). Projet: without presets, one
  line instead of an empty list. Lumière: sunrise / sunset on one line (local time if the track gives its offset,
  otherwise solar time), the matching solar time in the ⓘ of "Heure solaire". ⓘ also on the fine camera
  settings and the flyover duration. Setting keys and behavior unchanged.
- **Icons**: Lucide paths (ISC, notice in the header of `src/ui/icons.tsx`), inline SVG, only the ones used;
  format frames drawn from the ratio (`AspectIcon`).

## Internationalisation

The interface and the texts drawn into the films and posters exist in **French** (the reference, and the default),
**English**, **Spanish**, **Italian** and **German**; the code, the comments and the documentation are in English. In-house runtime, no dependency (`src/i18n`).

- **Files.** One object per domain and language: `src/i18n/<language>/<domain>.ts` (`topbar`,
  `timeline`, `inspector`, `export`, `overlay`, `poster`…), gathered by `fr/index.ts` and `en/index.ts`. A message is
  identified by `domain.key` (`t('timeline.bar.play')`). French defines the shape: `Messages = Widen<typeof fr>`
  (`messages.ts`), English is typed `Messages`, so a missing or an extra key is a compile error, and `MessageKey` is the union
  of the keys, so a typo in a key is one too. `languages.ts` is the one list of the languages (`code`, native `name`,
  `locale`), with `detectLanguage` (first browser language the app has, else French).
- **Runtime** (`index.ts`).
  - `t(key, vars?)`: the message of the current language with its `{name}` placeholders filled. A message written
    `{ one, other }` (any `Intl.PluralRules` category) is a plural: `vars.count` (a number) picks the form. `t` never formats a
    number or a date: the caller passes them formatted.
  - `useT()`: `t` for a component. It renders again when the language changes and returns a new function then, so a `useMemo`
    or `useEffect` that lists `t` among its dependencies follows the language (but a toast raised from an effect keyed on
    something else must not list `t`: it calls the module-level `t`, which reads the language at that moment).
  - `useLanguage()`, `getLanguage()`, `setLanguage()`, `subscribeLanguage()`, `currentLocale()` (for `Intl` and `localeCompare`);
    `template(key)` (the message with its placeholders unfilled); `withLanguage(code, fn)` words something in another language
    without telling the components (the diagnostic report stays French); `tRich(key, { link: <a/> })` (`rich.tsx`) for a message
    with React nodes in it.
  - Non-React code (film overlay, poster, export, import errors, film model) calls `t` directly: the current language lives in
    this module, set from the preferences.
- **Numbers, dates, lists** (`format.ts`, `Intl` formats cached per language): `formatNumberIntl(value, fractionDigits, minFractionDigits)` (« 1 234,5 »
  / « 1,234.5 », no sign on a zero), `decimalSeparator()`, `formatDate(value, options)` (any `Intl.DateTimeFormat` options),
  `formatList(items)` (« a, b et c » / « a, b and c »). `src/ui/format.ts` (`formatNumber`, `formatDistance`, `formatDuration`,
  `formatBytes`, `formatClock`…) builds on them and on the `format.*` messages (units: « Mo » / « MB », « D+ » / « Gain »).
- **The language** is a device preference, `Preferences.language` (`ui/shell.ts`, stored with `Preferences.appearance`). The
  top bar has the selector (`LanguageMenu`, between « ? » and « Exporter »: flag and code, a menu of native names; the flags are
  inline SVG, `ui/Flag.tsx`, because Windows draws the flag emoji as two letters;
  `role="menuitemradio"`, arrows, Escape, focus back to the button). `installLanguage()` (`ui/preferences.ts`, called by `main.tsx`
  only, so the tests stay French) applies the preference now and on every change, sets `<html lang>`, and on a first run with nothing
  stored takes the first browser language the app offers (it is remembered only once the person picks one).
- **Adding a string.** Pick the domain (a new domain: a new file in every language folder, added to each `index.ts`), add the key to
  `fr/<domain>.ts` then to the other languages (the compiler lists what is missing), call `t('domain.key', { name })`. The parity test
  (`i18n.test.ts`) also checks that every language uses the same `{placeholders}` for every key and that every plural has `other`.
- **Adding a language.** Copy `fr/` to a new folder, translate every file (the compiler lists the keys left), register it in the
  `catalogs` of `index.ts` and in `LANGUAGES` (`languages.ts`: code, native name, locale) and draw its flag in `ui/Flag.tsx`.
  Nothing else: the menu, the
  preference, the plurals and the dates follow.
- **Rules.**
  - No displayed text in the code: labels, `aria-label`s, tooltips (`title`, `Tip`), placeholders, hints, toasts, errors the user
    sees, dialogs, the shortcuts help, empty states, and the texts drawn into the film (counter labels, units, card wording, credits,
    weather words, dates) all go through `t`.
  - Never build a sentence by concatenation or by a plural suffix (`+ 's'`): one message per sentence, `{count}` plurals, and
    whole variants (« photo » / « vidéo » × singular / plural) rather than a word plugged into a sentence; word order and agreement
    belong to the language.
  - Dates and numbers go through `format.ts` / `ui/format.ts`, never `toLocaleString('fr-FR')`, `toFixed(…).replace('.', ',')` or a
    hand-written month list.
  - A module-level table of labels (`Record<Id, string>`, a list of `{ id, label }`) uses getters (`get label() { return t('…') }`)
    so it follows the language without every consumer changing. Text computed once and kept (a landmark label, a default name, a
    toast) is worded when it is made; a `useMemo` that computes labels lists `t` (or `useLanguage()`) among its dependencies.
  - Not translated: the user's own data (track and project names, captions, point-of-interest names), OpenStreetMap names (the
    `name:fr` tag is preferred), file formats and units that read the same (`km`, `m`, `bpm`), proper names, and what is for the
    developer: `console` messages, internal assertion errors, the breadcrumbs and the **diagnostic report** (kept in French on purpose,
    `withLanguage('fr', …)`: it is pasted into bug reports, the dialog around it is translated), the command-line output of the
    batch render, the sample GPX, `public/manifest.webmanifest` and the service worker.
  - The documentation (README, this file, `docs/`, the user guide included) is in English and quotes the French labels of the interface (the reference language); the interface itself is available in the five languages.
- **Terminology** (English): Shot (« Plan »), Fade to black / white, Cut (« Coupe »), Hold (« Arrêt »), Keyframe (« Image clé »),
  Timeline, Lane (« Piste / ligne »), Opening / Closing card, Export, Render; Elevation gain (« D+ »), Pass (« Col »), Peak / Summit
  (« Sommet »), Hut (« Refuge »), Stage (« Étape »), Route (« Parcours »). The French wording is the reference and does not change.
- **Tests.** `i18n/i18n.test.ts` (parity of keys and placeholders, `t`, plurals, `withLanguage`, `detectLanguage`),
  `i18n/format.test.ts` (numbers, dates, lists in both languages), `ui/preferences.test.ts` (`installLanguage`), `ui/LanguageMenu.test.tsx`
  (keyboard, Escape, the choice), and `e2e/run.mjs` (« langue » scenario; the scenarios start in French through the browser language).
  The other tests run in French (`installLanguage` is not called), so they assert the same texts as before.

## Flyover (phase 2)

- **Playback** (`store.playback { playing, progress, timeS, speed }`): `progress` ∈ [0, 1] along the **first** track, at constant ground
  speed; duration `settings.flyoverDurationS` at ×1 (60 s by default) whatever the length. `timeS` = film
