  time (s at ×1, pacing included) when playback or the export sets it, `null` after a move from outside (slider,
  climbs, landmarks: it is then derived from the progress through the pacing). Reaching 1 without a film time pauses, restarting
  from the end rewinds; `requestFit` pauses, removing a track resets to 0.
- **`FlyoverRig`** (in `TerrainLayer`): advances `progress` in `useFrame`, places the marker (unlit white sphere,
  `depthTest: false`, constant screen size) and drives the camera during playback or when `progress` changes while paused
  (scrub); otherwise the orbit stays free around the marker.
- **Camera** (`src/flyover/camera.ts`, `computeCameraView`): pure function of (progress, film time, settings,
  terrain sampler), with no state from one frame to the next, so the video export can render any frame in isolation.
  Orbit and cinematic (`movesWithTime`) follow the film time (`timeS`, or progress × duration when missing) and therefore keep
  turning during pacing pauses; the other styles depend only on progress. Heading = chord
  [d − w, d + w] (2w = « Lissage des virages » `turnSmoothingM` in metres; 0 = « Auto »: w = 2% of the track,
  150 m–1.5 km, × the `smoothing` multiplier of the presets and older projects), automatic distance 4% of the track (600 m–4 km, × distance).
  **Scaled with the film's ground speed** v = track length / flyover duration (2 km per film second for 540 km in
  4 min 30 s, 280 m for the sample): the heading is that chord averaged along the track over ± 1.5 × « Lissage de la
  caméra » × v (`smoothHeadingAt`; none at 0 s): chord directions tabulated once per path and step, unwrapped along it
  (each against the last chord at least w / 2 long) and weighted by the chord length, so a loop, an out-and-back or a
  GPS star at a rest turns the camera through instead of flipping it (the bare chord swung by up to 180° in a frame
  there: 3,000 °/s); 'sway' measures its turn as the averaged direction w ahead minus w behind. The aim point and the
  camera's point of the track are the track averaged over at least 0.5 s × v (`POSITION_SMOOTHING_S`): the corners of a
  sparse recording (a point every 60 m–2 km) are rounded off instead of jolting the view at each one. With « Lissage de
  la caméra » the camera stands on the track smoothed over a deviation σ = 0.2 × « Lissage de la caméra » × v
  (`cameraLine`: exact mean of the polyline over ± σ through running integrals, then two moving averages, ends continued
  by point symmetry, Catmull-Rom read-back; built once per path, frame and σ, at most 4 windows per path) and the aim on
  one half as wide, at most 6 % of the camera's distance: GPS noise and tight zigzags no longer shake the camera
  (sideways jerk p99 22,000 → 64 m/s³ on a noisy zigzag walk in « Poursuite ») while the marker stays near the centre
  (≤ 0.16 of the half-frame). « Lissage de la caméra » at 0 keeps the previous behaviour. `computeCameraView` costs
  ~145–225 µs per call (was ~100) with an analytic sampler, 163–251 terrain samples per call.
  Styles (`settings.camera.style`): `chase` (behind the marker), `sway` (swing toward the outside of bends:
  50° · tanh(0.8 · T / 50°), T = sum of turn angles weighted by a tent over ±2w, continuous and calm), `orbit` (`orbitTurns`
  full turns around the marker over the flight at speed x1, 1 by default, whatever its duration, from the start heading), `top` (≥ 70°, distance × 2.5, north or heading up), `cinematic` (distance × 1.6,
  pitch / 2, lateral sweep ±35° with a 40 s period), `free` (follows nothing: `computeFilmView` takes `freeView` instead of
  the flight camera, below). All flight styles keep 80 m above the ground and a clear line of sight
  (`LINE_OF_SIGHT_SAMPLES`, 48 samples); the tests check continuity in progress.
  **Marker kept visible**: the aim is the marker (the time smoothing only averages the progress), so it never leaves the
  frame (`markerVisible.test.ts`: at most 0.15 of the half-frame from its centre over every preset, with stops and speed
  portions); what hides it is the relief. The sight line is checked from the camera to the marker itself (at its
  steady ground height) down to 100 m short of it, at most 1/20 of the line, against the grid ground raised by the
  relief its cells miss (`reliefGround`: each corner by the largest rise of its four cell centres above their corners'
  mean: fixed samples, so a crest narrower than a cell is cleared without riding it), tapering to 20 m of clearance;
  48 samples, each grid corner and centre read once per view. Where a metre of ground lifts the camera by more than
  3 m (eased out up to 6), the line is checked against a coarse grid instead (cells ≥ 150 m, each corner the mean of
  2 × 2 samples over a cell when the ground cell is finer), ending on that grid's ground at the marker, never above its
  steady height (#186, see "Steady heights"). When the ground between the marker and the camera
  (gradient over ± half the camera's ground distance, ≥ 150 m, averaged along the track like the heading) rises faster
  than tan(pitch + 10°) − 0.05, the camera turns around the marker away from uphill (`swingAngle`: odd quintic easing,
  continuous with its slope, eased in over 0.2 of excess steepness; straight uphill it climbs instead of picking a
  side). Hidden frames on the steep synthetic tracks over the 12 presets: 0.3 % (up to 8 % for one flight) → 2 of
  21,600 → 0 with the coarse grid near the marker (#186); at most 4 shown ↔ hidden switches per flight; the marker stays within 0.15 of the half-frame (0.2 through stops). Keyframes
  (`keyView`) are untouched.
  **Steady heights** (no frame-to-frame state, so preview and export stay identical): the heights are read from
  terrain samples that stay put while the marker moves, so the camera follows the relief without riding each bump
  or a sight-line sample (whose height counts up to 10× near the marker). Ground radius / cell r = w / 4, at most
  150 m (37.5 m below a 7.5 km track at smoothing 1: scaled with the track, so with the marker's step per frame), and
  at least 0.25 s × v, at most 500 m (`GROUND_SPEED_S`: a fast film no longer rides the relief at a few frames a cell).
  The camera looks at the aim point, not the marker (`marker`, on the draped track): the aim's x / z at
  the smooth maximum (over 40 m, `AIM_GROUND_EASE_M`) of `trackGround` and the grid ground there + lift;
  `trackGround` = mean of the terrain at fixed track distances (multiples of r / 4, ≤ 8 samples) over ±r, weighted
  as above; the grid ground at the aim is a cubic B-spline over the 4 × 4 corners around it (exact on a plane, no
  slope break at a cell edge, 16 samples). Clearance rules (80 m under the camera, the sight line to the
  aim) read the grid ground: bilinear between the terrain at the corners of a fixed r grid of the local frame (exact
  on a plane, cliffs softened over a cell); the floor is eased in (smooth maximum over 80 m, at most 20 m above),
  then the camera is kept `MIN_TERRAIN_CLEARANCE_M` (40 m) above the actual terrain below it (a bump narrower than a
  cell). About 130 terrain samples per frame. On ±15 m bumps 30 m wide, the frame-to-frame height change drops from
  3–35 m to 0.16 m on flat ground, the change of vertical speed from 1–70 m to under 2.5 m on hills.
  **Sharper tiles** (#186): the sampler reads the sharpest tile loaded, so a sharper tile arriving changes the ground
  by a few metres. The camera's own height follows it 1:1 (the aim's mean); the sight-line lift multiplied it by up to
  1 / (share of the line left), 20 near the marker: on the test hills with one height every 150 m blended, the aim moved
  14–17 m and the camera height up to 6.3× that (« Cinéma »). Near the marker the line now reads the coarse grid, its
  end on the same grid, so the change shifts both alike: 2.1× at most (`camera.test.ts`); over 100 m cliffs, the change
  of vertical speed per frame of « Orbite » / « Cinéma » drops from 43 / 70 m to 23 / 7 m. Left as is: the sideways
  swing (`swingAngle`, up to ~120 m for « Cinéma » on the hills) and the far presets on the steep massif (« Cinéma »
  8.9×: there the ground cell is already 150 m, and the change itself varies within 200 m of the marker).
  **Shots, lift** (#213 follow-up; `shotLift`, `liftedOnTheWay`): the lift a shot's camera needs (glide slope, ground,
  sight line) is a function of the blend `k` only (the views at the ends are given), read at 40 fixed points of it; the camera
  takes the largest of the lifts of the points around, each in full within `SHOT_LIFT_HOLD` = 0.06 of the blend and down to
  nothing at `SHOT_LIFT_REACH` = 0.25 (raised cosine: the same anticipation as the flight's `anticipatedRaise`, in blend
  units), so a crest passing under the line of sight, which asks 2 km in less than half a second, becomes a gentle hump.
  The lift fades toward the end views over `SHOT_EASE_SHARE` = 10% of the blend, longer the higher it is (+ lift / (2 ×
  distance), at most 50%): the glide slope asks 2.5 km above an overview on the steep massif, which a 0.4 s fade dropped at
  6 km/s. The floor takes over from the blend through a `smoothMax` of 20% of the distance (the plain maximum made the
  climb change pace at a point). On the closing of « Descente » over the loops: the second difference 127 m → under 30 m a
  frame; « Balayage » (0.02–0.11 → none), « Satellite » (marker out of the frame by 20% → in).
  **Audit of the film camera** (`src/flyover/filmAudit.ts`, pure): `replayFilm` places the camera of a film every frame
  (30 i/s) and `auditPoses` lists, with their time, the **jolts** (second difference of the camera's or the aim's
  position over the camera's distance, above `AUDIT_JOLT_LIMIT` = 0.025: a calm flight stays under 0.006, #186 / #213
  reached 0.03–0.11), the camera **low** (under `MIN_TERRAIN_CLEARANCE_M` above the terrain) and the marker **out** of
  the frame. The frames next to a change of shot or stop are not read (the marker starts or stops, the camera follows
  at once). `filmAudit.test.ts` holds the guard: the opening and closing of the 12 presets × 4 shot styles on the switchbacks,
  the loops (steep massif, slope 0.8) and a raid over hills: no jolt (but « Saut », a quick move of 0.6 s on purpose), never
  low, the marker never out of the frame. No in-app button yet: the audit reads the terrain it is given, and what the
  engine has loaded is not the terrain of the whole film.
  **Smoothing in film time** (`src/flyover/timeSmoothing.ts`, applied by `computeFilmView`; still no frame-to-frame
  state): the aim point follows the marker's progress averaged over « Lissage de la visée » `aimSmoothingS` (0 by
  default: on the marker), the camera follows it averaged over « Lissage de la caméra » `cameraSmoothingS` (3 s by
  default), each a raised-cosine window of 21 fixed samples of `clock.progressAtTime` centred on the film time; the
  camera then stands around its own point of the track (`cameraProgress`, heading and sway measured there) at the
  aim's height, looking at the aim (`aimProgress`), the marker staying on its progress. The progress is averaged, not
  the placements: at constant speed it is unchanged (the look of a film stays the same away from speed changes), the
  averaged point stays on the track (no corner cut, no angle to average), and a sample costs a clock look-up, not a
  placement. Speed changes, pacing pauses and stops are anticipated and eased: a stop entered over 1.5 s jerks about
  half as much at 3 s, a quarter at 7.5 s; the start of the flight (the flight view was still during the opening, then
  at full speed) no longer jumps. Edges: the clock holds the progress outside the flight (0 during the opening, 1
  during the closing) and clamps the film time, so the windows reach across the shots without a jump; the shot curves,
  the stop cameras (orbit angle, « Vue large », « Fixe » held motion time) and the keyframes keep the marker's time
  and progress, so a framing pinned for an item is the one seen during it. « Fin en douceur » `endingS` (0 by
  default): over its last seconds the camera time slows down at a constant rate to a stop (`easedEndTimeS`: speed 1 →
  0, held from the end of the flight at half the ease short of it), the time-based motions (orbit, cinema) too; the
  aim keeps following the marker to the finish, so the camera stops and turns to watch it. `filmViewMovesWithTime` is
  true during every stop when one of the three is on (the export then renders those frames instead of repeating one).
  **Anticipated height** (#211, `anticipatedRaise` in `timeSmoothing.ts`, applied by `flightAt` in `filmCamera.ts`): the
  clearance rules above raise the camera by what the relief needs at that very instant, and that raise changed fast as
  the marker and the camera swept the relief (0 → 590 m in half a second down a pass of the sample outing, with a 6 m
  shake on calmer stretches): the camera jumped and shook. The flight camera now takes the raises of the fixed film
  instants every `ANTICIPATION_STEP_S` (0.5 s) around now: all of each within `ANTICIPATION_HOLD_S` (1 s), less and less
  out to `ANTICIPATION_REACH_S` (3 s, raised cosine), the largest counting. The camera starts
  climbing 3 s before the relief needs it and comes down as gently after; an instant's raise stays the same from frame
  to frame (its marker and camera stay put), so nothing shakes. The opening and closing shots blend with the flight view
  of the flight's ends, still as before. Cost: the raise of an instant is kept per terrain sampler (`raiseTable`): a
  frame computes the instants that came into reach (about one flight camera every 0.5 s of film) and, while tiles
  arrive (`terrainVersion`, counted by `filmView.ts` from `engine.onChange`), refreshes one instant computed on older
  terrain, so the height follows sharper tiles an instant at a time. On the sample outing over the 12 presets: change of
  vertical speed per frame (max) 16,000–155,000 → under 3,000 m/s², shake 8–110 → under 2.5 m, terrain reads +5 %.
- **Camera settings**: `settings.camera { style, distance, pitchDeg, headingOffsetDeg, smoothing, northUp, turnSmoothingM,
  aimSmoothingS, cameraSmoothingS, endingS }` (a camera saved before the last four gets them from `DEFAULT_CAMERA`,
  `withCameraDefaults` in `SETTING_UPGRADES`: « Auto » keeps its multiplier, so the turns look the same) and named
  presets (`CAMERA_PRESETS`, 13: Poursuite (chase), Drone rapide, Oiseau, Hélicoptère, Orbite, Cinéma, Drone haut, Planeur, Montgolfière, Avion, Vue du dessus, Satellite, then Libre (style `free`, left out of `markerVisible.test.ts`: it does not follow the marker); `findCameraPreset`; « Préréglage » is a grid of tiles with distance and tilt, all twelve shown at once, plus a « Personnalisé » tile when the settings match no preset) in
  `src/flyover/cameraSettings.ts`; `settings.flyoverDurationS` (15–600 s, 60 by default) = duration at ×1, the timeline
  speed applies on top. "Survol" tab (`src/ui/CameraPanel.tsx`, "Caméra" and "Durée et rythme" sections; the
  smoothing sliders as rows of « Caméra », « Lissage des virages » showing the « Auto » length for
  the first track). While paused, a camera setting change repositions the camera.
  "Cadrer la caméra pendant cet élément" (frame the camera during this item; inspector of a text or a media item): `addItemCamera` places a framing where
  the marker is at the start of the item (selected, to be adjusted) and, if the marker moves during the item, a second one at
  its end that keeps the framing that was there: neighboring framings keep their values (between the end of the item and the
  next framing, the transition is recomputed over the new interval).
  The ◇ of the « Plans » lane header (`toggleKeyframe`) removes the keyframe under the playhead, else adds one (`addKeyframe`, « Ajouter une image clé »): it places
  a keyframe at the marker with the pose of the 3D view as framed (`captureKey`: `FlyoverRig` registers the reader of its
  camera and orbit target, `registerKeyCapture`, so the UI does not load three.js; a keyframe already at that metre takes
  it over), one undo step, selected for the inspector (see "Film and timeline", "Keyframes"). It replaced « Garder ce
  cadrage ici », which stored the film's own framing at the marker (`keyedCamera` of the settings), never the 3D view:
  the camera snapped back to the film's view and the film showed nothing new.
- **Pacing** (`src/flyover/pacing.ts`, pure; `settings.pacing`, "Caméra" section): progress remains the distance
  fraction; only the film time → progress mapping changes. Highlights: climb summits (`climbsOf`) and, among the OSM
  landmarks passed as an argument, passes crossed (≤ 150 m) and summits within ≤ 300 m. Relative speed
  r(x) = 1 − (1 − slowFactor)·max c(|x − h| / windowM), c = raised cosine (deepest dip when they overlap).
  Tabulated travel time (constant speed per step: exact inverse by bisection). Pauses: highlights less than
  windowM apart are grouped, one pause per group, each adds exactly `pauseS` with raised-cosine entry and exit
  (≤ 1.5 s). `keepDuration` (default) raises the base speed to keep `flyoverDurationS` (pauses ≤ 50% of the duration), otherwise
  the film gets longer. Disabled or without highlights: identical to `advanceProgress`. `FlyoverRig` advances the film time
  (`playback.timeS`; during a pacing pause the progress does not move) and restarts from `pacing.positionAt` after a slider
  move or a pacing change; playback stops only at `pacing.totalTime()`, final pause included (progress 1
  held with a film time, then 1 without a time). `timeAtProgress(1)` = end of the film. Pauses are now the film
  stops (`flightPacing`, see "Film and timeline"); `pacingFromHighlights` derives them as before. `useFilmClock`
  (`src/scene/usePacing.ts`) shares the computation with the panel. The export calls `pacing.progressAtTime(t)` over
  `pacing.totalTime()`.
- **`Timeline`** (`src/ui/Timeline.tsx`): film timeline below the view, in film time (see "Film and timeline") —
  play / pause, distance covered, elevation and recorded time at the marker, speed ×0.5 to ×4.
- **Elevation profile** (flyover block of the "Plans" (shots) lane): **recorded** elevations of the track resampled at
  400 constant distance steps (`elevationProfile`), placed in film time (`timeAtProgress`: flat during stops),
  SVG area, played part in light red. Vertical range of at least 100 m so GPS noise is not magnified; profile hidden
  if the track has no elevation.

## Atmosphere (phase 3)

- **`AtmosphereLayer`** (`src/scene/AtmosphereLayer.tsx`, in `TerrainLayer`): Takram's precomputed scattering model
  (`@takram/three-atmosphere`). `worldToECEFMatrix` = `mslLocalToEcef(frame)` (local frame raised by the geoid
  undulation: +51 m at Chamonix, see "Coordinate conventions"). Lighting **by sources** (`SunLight` +
  `SkyLight` placed at the origin, 1000 m): the terrain keeps its `MeshStandardMaterial`. Post-process (`EffectComposer`):
  aerial perspective (haze according to the real distance), **Khronos Neutral** tone mapping (keeps the hues of the
  orthophotos), SMAA, color grading (see "Color grading"). Exposure 5.
- **Precomputed textures**: EXR files from the package (~9.5 MB), served at `/atmosphere/` in dev and copied into the build by a
  `vite.config.ts` plugin — nothing is downloaded from a third party or versioned here. Do not generate them at startup: the
  generation runs in `requestIdleCallback` calls that never fire while the scene keeps the main thread busy,
  and the lights stay black.
- **Logarithmic depth**: `postprocessing` signals it with `LOG_DEPTH`, the Takram shader expects
  `USE_LOGARITHMIC_DEPTH_BUFFER`; the define is added to the effect, otherwise the whole scene is seen as infinitely far.
- **Track**: unlit materials; their color is divided by `renderer.toneMappingExposure` (`applyExposure`).
- **Sun at the time of the outing** (`settings.sunFromTrack`, on by default): when the first track (« À la suite »: the
  stage under the marker, see "Several tracks") is timestamped, the lighting
  date is the **recorded** time of the point under the marker (`sunDateAt`; shifted during a situation shot that
  moves the sun, `shotSunShiftMs`, see "Film and timeline"), updated every frame by
  `atmosphereRef.current.updateByDate(date)` in `useFrame` (not the `date` prop: the two do not combine). Points without
  a time are filled by interpolation over distance (`recordedTimeAt`); a pause or a jump between segments is crossed
  instantly, except a night. **Nights** (`src/flyover/nights.ts`, `film.nights`): `trackNights` finds each night whose
  sunset and sunrise (`sunTimes` where the track is at noon that day) are both recorded, placed between the two timed
  points sharing most of it; `flightNights` keeps those the flight would cross, at base speed, faster than dusk + night +
  dawn (a night spent riding is shown by the flight). Each is a pause of the flight (`FlightStop.extra`: added to the film
  whatever « Garder la durée », never shortened; `clock.nights`, phase 'flight', marker held). During it `nightSunDate` runs
  the sun date from the flight's sun to sunset + 1 h (dusk), to sunrise − 1 h (night), then to the flight's sun (dawn),
  linearly over parts proportional to their seconds: continuous, never backwards, a pure function of the film time. The
  export re-renders those frames (`nightAt` in the held-frame test). **Across a fade between two stages « À la suite »** (a junction `fondu-noir` / `fondu-blanc` with a hold; `junctionSunDate`, applied by `AtmosphereLayer` after the nights): over the whole pause of the junction (`startS` to `endS`, the same window as its dip) the sun date runs from the end of the stage before (`sunAt` just short of the cut) to the start of the next, with `blendSunDates`: the time of day runs the way the film goes (forward when the next stage is later, so an evening into a morning passes dusk, night and dawn; backward when it is earlier), eased by `smootherstep`, and the whole days between are taken at once half-way, under the dip. Both ends are the stages' own suns, so nothing jumps; the same sun on both sides (« Heure fixe », or one day) stays still. Keys on the sun hour (`sunKeyed`) replace the blend, a `nuit` junction keeps its own `nightSunDate`, a cut has no pause. A pure function of the film time; the export's held-frame test also re-renders the frames of the blend (`junctionBlendAt`). Clouds, weather and the sun-less settings still switch at the cut, under the dip. **Days and their cards** (`src/film/days.ts`, `film.dayCards`: `FilmDayCards`, optional, absent = off): `trackDays` splits the
  track on the local calendar date of its timed points (recording clock when `utcOffsetMin` is known, device clock
  otherwise); `dayFigures` gives each day its distance, climb (the cumulative ascent between its first and last point),
  first recorded time, time to its last point and, with weather loaded, its weather (`summarizeOuting` over the day's
  stretch). The clock keeps the days (`clock.days`: `startM`, `endM`, the night pause that leads into the day, `cardS`, `view`;
  none while the cards are off or for a sequence): the card of the first day shows with the flight, the others once the
  night pause before them ends (`holdEndS`), or, with the high view, as it starts (`holdStartS`). The card is **not**
  baked into the texts any more: `dayCardAt` (overlay/data.ts) reads the film time (`OverlayTime.dayCardsS`) and the
  figures every frame and `dayWidget` (overlay/draw.ts) draws « Jour n » (or the title, with `{n}` `{count}` `{date}`),
  the date and weather line, and the chosen figures as cells, like the card of a stage « À la suite » (the overlay's style
  decides its look; `fields`, `anchor`, `size`, `durationS` 2–15 s are the film's). The card follows the track, the nights
  and the weather; the `day-n` texts of earlier versions are removed when the cards are turned on (`withoutDayCards`).
  On the timeline each day's card is a block of « Textes » (`dayCardId(k)` = `card-day-k`, `dayCardWindow` in
  `film/days.ts`): its end edge sets the shared `durationS` (`dragFilm`), Delete switches all the cards off
  (`removeFilmItem`), and its inspector is `DayCardFields` (the panel of « Jours et nuits », `src/ui/NightSettings.tsx`).
  The opening title and the closing card take « Début (s) » / « Durée (s) » in their inspector (`withCardTimes` in
  `film/cards.ts`, the rules of a drag); the Habillage « Texte libre » says it stays the whole film.
  **High view** (`dayViewAt`, `computeFilmView`): in the night pause before a day with `view`, the camera blends from the
  flight view to `overviewView` framing the box of that day's stretch (`trackBox` with a `PathRange`), lifted like a shot
  (`liftedBlend`): up over the dusk, held over the night, back down over the dawn (the parts of `film.nights`, never under
  20% of the pause each), a pure function of the film time; the preview re-places the camera during it (`FlyoverRig`
  asks `dayViewAt`), the export already re-renders the frames of a night. « À la suite » it is « Revoir l'étape finie » of
  a transition instead (`FilmJunction.recap`): over a night it passes, `ClockJunction.view` and `dayViewAt` frames the stage the
  junction ends (from the cut before it to its `atM`); without a night, see "Stages". Without timestamps or with the option unchecked: fixed solar time. The timeline shows the recorded time at the marker
  (browser time zone).
- **Exposure** (`src/scene/exposure.ts`): `DAYLIGHT_EXPOSURE` (5) × 2^(automatic + `settings.exposureEv`). The automatic part
  opens up as the sun goes down: 0 EV above 10°, +3 EV at civil twilight (−6°), +6 EV at night (−18°). A faint
  night-sky light (`nightFillIntensity`) keeps the terrain readable: the atmosphere only models sunlight.
- **Settings**: `settings.atmosphere` (can be turned off: back to fixed lighting without tone mapping) and `settings.sunHour`
  (local **solar** time, 0 h–24 h, independent of time zones; at night: stars and moon), the day being `settings.sunDate`
  (`YYYY-MM-DD`, "Jour" (day) field in fixed time, `isSunDate`) or, if empty, the day the first track starts (today
  otherwise): `sunDayMs`.
- **Sunrise and sunset** (`sunTimes`, pure and tested): NOAA solar equations evaluated around local noon of the UTC day
  (declination, equation of time; zenith 90.833°: refraction and half disk), about one minute of error away from the poles;
  polar day or night flagged (`polar`, sunrise and sunset `null`). Converted to solar time by `solarHourOf` (inverse of
  `solarHourToDate`) for the "Lumière" interface.
- **Terrain cast shadows** (`src/scene/terrainShadow.ts`, `settings.shadows`, "Ombres du relief" (terrain shadows) checkbox visible with
  the atmosphere): the Takram `SunLight` receives a `TerrainShadow` (one shadow map, `deviceBudget().shadowMapSize`: 4096² on a computer, 2048² on a phone or tablet,
  1024² with 2 GB or less; PCF through `shadows="percentage"`).
  Its orthographic bounds are recomputed in `updateMatrices(light, viewCamera)`, which three calls just before
  drawing the map: area = view frustum cut at max(30 km, 5 × camera height above the lowest terrain),
  intersected with the box of the visible tiles (`fitShadowFrustum`, pure and tested); near plane pushed toward the sun
  up to the top of that box; bounds rounded in steps of 2^(1/8) and snapped to whole texels (no shimmering);
  texel-based bias (normalBias 1.5 texel, depth 2 m). Texel ≈ 8–9 m in chase, 15–25 m in overview.
- **Off-screen casters** (`engine.ts`): all tiles cast and receive; `frustumCulled` on. After the
  LOD selection, ready tiles that were visited but are outside the frustum and not covered by a drawn tile stay `visible`: a
  ridge behind the camera reaches the shadow map, and three drops them from the main pass. `stats.visibleTiles` only counts
  the drawn tiles.
- **Why shadows**: without occlusion, a grazing sun (~1° at sunset) lights the sunny side of every bump in
  valleys that should be in the shadow of the ridges — this was the cause of the orange streaks (N·L > 0 on thin facets).
- Limits: nothing outside the loaded footprint (track + 25 km) casts; shadows cut sharply beyond the shadow distance (the
  haze hides it); a single map (no cascades); map redrawn every frame.

## Color grading ("Couleurs")

- **Setting** `settings.grading` (`src/scene/grading.ts`, pure and tested): `contrast`, `saturation`, `warmth` (−1..1),
  `vignette` (0..1), and `preset` (`naturel`, `lumineux`, `doux`, `contraste`, `chaud-du-soir`, `froid-altitude`,
  `noir-et-blanc`, or `personnalise`). The preset always follows the values (`withGradingValue` → `matchingPreset`: a
  slider brought back to a preset's values selects it again). Validated when a project is opened (`isValidGrading`).
- **Effect** (`src/scene/gradingEffect.ts`): a single `postprocessing` `Effect`, per pixel, with no texture or depth:
  white balance (R / B gains, luminance preserved) → saturation (mix with the Rec. 709 luminance) → S-curve
  around middle gray in sRGB (0 and 1 fixed, nothing clips; slope 2^contrast) → vignetting (down to −60% in the
  corners, distance in frame units: same rendering at any resolution). A slider only changes uniforms (no
  recompilation). The overlay (2D canvas on top, composited after the scene in the export) is not graded:
  texts and logos keep the colors of their style.
- **Place in the chain**: with the atmosphere, last effect of the `AtmosphereLayer` composer, **after** tone mapping and
  SMAA (SMAA reads the input of its pass: an effect placed before it in the same pass would be ignored on the edges).
  Without the atmosphere, there is no composer: `GradingComposer` (`src/scene/GradingComposer.tsx`, loaded on demand
  by `FlyoverCanvas`) mounts a minimal one (render, SMAA, grading). Since the canvas is then transparent over the CSS sky
  gradient, the effect first lays the (premultiplied) image over the **same gradient** (`SKY_TOP_COLOR` → `SKY_HORIZON_COLOR`,
  interpolated in sRGB like the CSS): the sky is graded too and the output is opaque; the export, which paints this gradient under
  the canvas (`fillSky`), gets the same image. Chosen over grading in the export's 2D compositor: a single
  code path for the preview and the export.
- **Cost**: "Naturel" (natural; zero values, `isIdentityGrading`) mounts **nothing** (no effect, no pass; without atmosphere, no
  composer and no loading of `postprocessing`). Otherwise a few dozen operations per pixel, merged into the existing
  pass. Without atmosphere, switching from "Naturel" to a preset replaces the canvas's native MSAA with SMAA.
- **Interface**: "Couleurs" section of the Lumière tab (`src/ui/GradingPanel.tsx`): preset chips (one
  undo step each), the four sliders as rows.

## Lens (« Objectif »)

- **Setting** `settings.lens` (`src/scene/lens.ts`, pure and tested): `shutter`, `bloom`, `bloomRadius`, `flare`,
  `depthOfField`, all 0..1 and off (0) by default (`bloomRadius` 0.6). `SETTING_CHECKS`: `isValidLens`;
  `SETTING_UPGRADES`: `withLensDefaults`; projects saved before it load with every effect off. In the "carte" preset
  family. "Objectif" tab (`src/ui/LensPanel.tsx`; "Non" at 0; motion blur, bloom and depth of field open, flare and the bloom
  radius as rows). The vignette stays in "Couleurs" (it is part of the grading presets).
- **Chain** (`useLensEffects`): an effect exists only while its amount is above 0 (switching it on or off rebuilds the
  pass; a slider only sets uniforms). With the atmosphere, bloom, depth of field and flare are merged into the pass
  of the SMAA and the grading (after the SMAA, before the grading; `EffectPass` sorts depth effects first), on
  tone-mapped linear colours. Without the atmosphere, `GradingComposer` mounts its composer as soon as an effect is
  on (`lensActive`), grading effect included even at "Naturel" so the output is laid over the sky gradient and opaque.
  The motion blur is a last `EffectPass` of its own: it averages the finished image. Updated every frame at priority
  0.75 (after the sun, the camera and the marker, before the composer); priority 0 while every effect is off, since
  a positive priority takes the rendering over from R3F.
- **Bloom**: `BloomEffect` of postprocessing (mipmap blur), threshold 0.6 linear luminance (sun, snow, glittering
  water after tone mapping), intensity 2.5 × amount, radius 0.2–0.95. Without and with the atmosphere.
- **Depth of field**: `DepthOfFieldEffect` of postprocessing (handles the logarithmic depth buffer). Focus distance =
  camera to the progress marker (`flyover-marker`), so a function of the film time like the camera; the orbit target,
  else 1 km, without a marker. Full blur at 2× (amount 0) to 0.5× (amount 1) the focus distance from it; bokeh
  6 px × amount at 1080p, scaled with the frame height (same look at 4K). The marker sprite writes no depth: it
  takes the depth of the ground behind it, sharp.
- **Lens flare** (atmosphere only, the slider is disabled without it): `FlareEffect`, custom, no dependency. The sun
  direction of the atmosphere (ECEF) is brought into the scene frame and projected; the effect reads five taps of
  its pass input over the sun disc: the disc saturates the tone mapping unless the relief or a volumetric cloud
  (which writes no depth) hides it, so occlusion needs no extra pass. Glare, a horizontal streak, a faint ring and six
  ghosts along the line from the sun through the centre (tinted with the palette's periwinkle, water green and
  chartreuse), faded over the last 5% of the frame; none when the sun is off screen. Five texture reads per pixel.
- **Motion blur**, export (exact and deterministic): each film frame is rendered up to `SHUTTER_SUBFRAMES` = 8 times at
  instants stratified over ± shutter / 2 frame intervals around it (`shutterSamples`, progress and film time
  interpolated between neighbouring frames of the schedule; an instant in another clock phase, i.e. across the cut of
  an opening or closing shot, is replaced by the frame itself). `ExportController` sets the sub-frame
  (`setShutterSubFrame`) and renders each with `renderSettledFrame`; `ShutterEffect` keeps the running mean in half
  floats, **weighted per pixel by the radial mask** (below): sub-frame k weighs mix(c_k, 1, m(r)), c_k a narrow bell of
  its offset in the shutter (`shutterCentreWeights`, σ = 0.05 of the window: in the centre only the sub-frames nearest to
  the frame instant, the two middle ones for an even count; in the corners the plain mean). The weights are a function
  of (k, r), so the shader sums them (uniform array of c_k) and updates the mean by w_k / W_k, with no weight buffer
  and nothing in the alpha channel (a sub-frame rendered again while tiles load restarts from the previous mean); the canvas
  then holds the mean and is composed as usual (overlay sharp, on top). Same capture for WebCodecs and the desktop's
  native encoder. Held frames are reused when the progress of every sub-frame is unchanged. The volumetric clouds do
  not average their 16/24/32 noise slices on every sub-frame: `subFrameSlices` gives each sub-frame its own share
  (2/3/4), so a frame still averages all of them, now spread over the shutter. Cost: up to × 8 for the scene and the
  other passes, the clouds unchanged. Stills and posters are never blurred.
  - *Adaptive count* (`adaptiveShutterSamples`, a pure function of the film time: same film, same counts). `viewMotionPx`
    places the film (`filmViewAt`, **without** the terrain, so the tiles loaded never change a count) at the first and
    the last of the 8 reference renders and takes the largest on-screen move (pixels of the video frame) of the marker
    and of fixed points of the relief seen at the centre and the four corners, at the aim distance and at a quarter of
    it (`NEAR_FRACTION`, a deliberately pessimistic near-relief parallax; translation and rotation both count). The
    shutter window is 8/7 of that span (max / (max − 1) with a lower export-quality cap `max`, `Export quality tiers`); count = clamp(⌈window / `SHUTTER_STEP_PX`⌉, 1, max = 8), 1 px being the step at which
    shifted images still average into a continuous blur (vs the 8 renders: ≤ 10% of the contrast on a hard edge, ≤ 3%
    rms on pixel-scale detail, tested on a synthetic displacement model). Fewer renders are the evenly spaced centred
    ones (`shutterSamples` with that count); 8 are the former ones bit for bit; 1 (stops, held frames, distant or slow
    cameras) leaves the radial blur alone, which works with a single render. `subFrameSlices` splits the cloud slices
    exactly, so the clouds' quality does not change. Not counted: an animated figurine, the drift of the clouds, the
    ripples of the water (a figurine's legs are sharp while the camera stands still). Fast, low flights stay at 8; slow
    ones, stops and high cameras (« Satellite », « Avion », « Montgolfière ») fall to 1–4. 4K counts twice the pixels.
- **Radial speed blur** (same "Flou de bougé" slider, preview and export alike): the speed effect of the user's
  request, edges streaked and centre sharp. The same mask serves the three parts of the motion blur (streaks, export
  sub-frames, preview trail): `radialMask` in `lens.ts`, one GLSL helper in `shutterEffect.ts`. The shutter blur alone only gives it for a camera flying straight towards
  its aim (focus of expansion on the aim point); in chase it mostly shifts the whole frame. So `ShutterEffect` first
  blurs each render along the ray to a centre, in the same blend draw (10 fixed taps). Centre: the direction of
  travel projected on the frame (camera placements of the film at t and t − 1/fps, `filmViewAt`) when it lies ahead
  and inside the central 70%, else the frame centre (the aim). Profile (the mask m(r) = smoothstep(0.3, 1, r)): streak = length × m, r
  the distance to the centre in frame units over the distance to the farthest corner (sharp inside 0.3, full in the
  corners); taps from the pixel towards the centre. Length = shutter × min(0.15, 0.1 s × relative speed), relative
  speed = distance the camera moved / aim distance / interval (1/s), 0 below 0.02/s: nothing during stops and held
  shots (a default 60 s flight in chase: about 0.6/s, length 0.06; a 15 s one reaches the cap). A pure function of the
  film time, so a paused preview shows the blur of its frame like the export does; none when the camera is farther
  than 5% of the aim distance from the film's placement (moved by hand, poster overview; the rig's own camera drifts
  by metres from a placement recomputed later, ground lift and finer tiles). Cost: two
  `computeFilmView` per frame on the CPU, 10 texture reads per pixel outside the central zone.
- **Motion blur**, preview: no motion vectors; while the film plays, `ShutterEffect` keeps a weight w · m(r) of the previous
  output (`previewShutterWeight`: the lag Δt·w/(1−w) of the trail equals half the exported shutter time, at any
  preview frame rate; no trail in the sharp centre, full towards the sides). Paused or scrubbing: the image as is. An approximation (a trailing smear, not the centred
  blur of the export). Cost: one blend per frame.
- **Field of view**: left fixed (`CAMERA_FOV_DEG` = 50°). It enters the framing of the film camera (situation
  distances, region fit, headroom), the overview of the poster, the offline tile plan and the LOD; making it a setting
  means threading it through all of these and through the export, not a small change.
- Verified: unit tests (setting, sub-frame instants, trail weight, mask and sub-frame weights, slice sharing) and SwiftShader screenshots;
  inferred: the cost and the look on a real GPU (the manual checks of `docs/handbook/index.html`).

## Track color

- **Setting** `settings.trackColorBy`: `'none'` (each track's own color, default), `speed`, `slope`, `elevation`,
  `heartRate`, `cadence`, `power`, `temperature`. Metrics missing from the first track are disabled.
- **Values** (`src/flyover/trackColor.ts`, pure), on the recorded points: speed in km/h over a centered window of at least
  ±25 m **and** ±15 s (capped at ±300 s) against GPS noise; slope in % over ±50 m (unknown below 20 m); elevation; sensors
  averaged over ±5 s.
- **Range** shared by all tracks: 2nd–98th percentile. Perceptually uniform sequential **palettes**: viridis by
  default, magma (from 0.2) for HR, power and temperature. **Missing values**: gray `#55626b`, never interpolated.
- **TrackLines**: per-vertex colors (`LineGeometry.setColors`, `vertexColors` on the line and glow materials); points
  inserted by `densify` are interpolated (`resampleValues`). Changing the mode only rewrites the color buffer. With
  per-vertex colors, the material color is white divided by the exposure (`applyExposure`).
- **Legend**: a widget of the film overlay (`trackLegendWidget` in `src/overlay/draw.ts`), so the preview and the
  export draw the same one: title, gradient, bounds with units, "Sans donnée" (no data) chip if values are missing. Its
  values come from the pure `trackLegend(tracks, colorBy)` (`trackColor.ts`), handed to `drawOverlay` through
  `OverlayExtras.trackLegend` (`overlayExtras`, cached per tracks, quantity and language). Drawn while
  `trackColorBy !== 'none'`, even with the « Habillage » overlay off; it fades with the cards like the live widgets and
  stacks with the widgets of its anchor `settings.trackLegendAnchor` (one of the nine `OVERLAY_ANCHORS`, default
  `'bottom-left'`; « Position de la légende » under « Colorer selon », in the « Trace » presets).

## Track and marker

- **Settings** `settings.trackStyle { width, dash: 'plein' | 'tirets' | 'points', glow, glowIntensity, glowWidth, glowColor, drawOn,
  smoothingM }` and `settings.marker { kind:
  'boule' | 'figurine' | 'image', figure ('auto' | figure id), image, size, animated, color, ballStyle, figureColor, shape,
  label, labelText, stick }` (`src/scene/markerSettings.ts`, pure). `color` ('#rrggbb', '' = track colour, default
  white): ball, and edge of the figure / picture badges; `figureColor` (default ink): disc of a figure, or the figure
  itself with `shape: 'contour'`. Racers ignore both and keep their track colour (ink edge). `ballStyle`: `plat` (as
  before; a saved `anneau`, dropped in 2.1.1, opens as `plat`), `halo` (radial glow), `pulsation` (ring widening and fading, period
  `PULSE_PERIOD_S` = 1.5 s, phase `pulsePhase(timeS)`: a function of the film time; a pulsing sprite redraws its own
  canvas instead of using the shared cache). `markerMovesWithTime(marker)` tells the export when held frames must be
  rendered again. `figure: 'auto'` (default): `figureOfActivity(track.activityType)` (GPX `<type>`, FIT sport, Strava),
  per track (each racer, each stage « À la suite »; kite → paraglider; unknown → hiker); 17 figures (+ traileur, vae, ski-rando, nageur,
  kayak, cavalier). `shape`: `rond`, `epingle` (map pin, anchored at its tip), `carre` (rounded square), `contour`
  (figure alone with an outline). `label`: `aucune` / `nom` (track name) / `texte` (`labelText`, 40 characters, lead only;
  racers show their track name). `stick`: badge raised on a stick with a soft ground shadow, anchored at its foot (bounce
  off, sway kept). One sprite and one texture per marker (`markerLayout` / `drawMarker`: badge, stick, label;
  `sprite.center` = anchor); texture cache of 48 textures and 8 Mpx. 3D models (glTF) evaluated, not done (licensing,
  lighting parity, poster overview hiding). `animated`: the
  figurine bounces and sways with `figureMotion(timeS)` (two steps per second of film, lift up to 8% of the badge through
  `sprite.center`, ±5° through `material.rotation`), a function of the film time only: a paused film keeps its pose,
  the export matches the preview; the ghost-race markers stay still. Defaults = the previous look
  (4 px, solid, white ball). Checked by `SETTING_CHECKS`, completed by `SETTING_UPGRADES` (keys added later).
  `image`: 128 px square PNG as a data URL (`fileToAvatarDataUrl`, platform picker), 300,000 characters at most.
  « Trace » and « Marqueur » sections of the Trace tab (`src/ui/TrackMarkerSection.tsx`).
- **Width**: pixels of a 1080 px image, times `renderScale` in the export (as before). **Glow** (« Halo de la trace »):
  a second `Line2` on the same geometry, `glowWidth` px wide (6–64, default 24), `LineMaterial` material whose fragment
  shader is replaced (`onBeforeCompile`, own program key): colour × `glowStrength` × gaussian radial fade (`vUv`,
  exp(−4x²) shifted to reach 0 at the edge) from the edge of the line (`glowInner` = width / glowWidth) outwards,
  **MAX** blending — no beads at the joints of overlapping pieces. Strength = `glowIntensity` (0.1–1, default 0.75) ×
  `TRACK_GLOW_MAX_STRENGTH` (0.8): the halo stays dimmer than the line, which keeps a clear edge instead of merging
  with it into a wider line; on white snow a light halo cannot show. Drawn in the
  opaque pass (`transparent: false`, renderOrder 1) after the terrain and before the line (renderOrder 2), which
  covers it: the line keeps its colour. Depth-tested like the line. Colour: `glowColor` ('#rrggbb'), '' = the track
  colour or its per-vertex colours; divided by the exposure like the line (`applyExposure`).
- **Smoothing** (`smoothingM`, 0–300 m, 0 = off by default; `src/flyover/smooth.ts`, pure): tent-weighted moving average
  of the positions over `smoothingM` metres of recorded distance, per segment, end points kept, other fields kept.
  `TrackLines` densifies the smoothed points (rebuild when the value changes); `FlyoverRig` and the export camera use
  `smoothedTrackPath`, so the marker and the chase camera follow the smoothed line without the GPS jitter, and so do
  the ghost racers (`raceTrackOf(track, smoothingM)`, cached for the last value). Both keep the **recorded distances**
  (carried over to the densified points with `resampleValues`): progress, film stops, climbs and the draw-on cut keep
  their scale. Labels, picking and the overlay stay on the recorded points.
- **Dashes / dots**: `LineMaterial` dashes measured in world units (`computeLineDistances`, redone on each
  drape). Scale = size of a pixel at the camera → controls target distance, **rounded to a power of two**
  (`quantizedPixelSize`): dashes stay fixed on the ground during the flight and only change length when the zoom
  doubles. Patterns in line widths: dashes 3 / 2, dots 1 / 1.5.
- **Track that draws itself**: each segment keeps the distance of each point from the start of its track (like
  `buildTrackPath`, without counting the jumps between segments). `cutLine` limits `geometry.instanceCount` to the pieces covered
  and moves the end of the last piece exactly onto the marker (restored at the next cut; a drape rewrites it).
  Distance: progress × length for the first track, `racer.distanceM` for the others during a ghost race,
  whole track otherwise. Applied through a store subscription (the progress set by `FlyoverRig` is picked up in the same
  frame, although `TrackLines` runs before it) and on every frame (after a drape); no effect when nothing changes.
  A function of progress alone: preview = export. The part still to come is hidden.
- **Trail** (« Traînée », `settings.trackTail { enabled, lengthM }`, 100 m–20 km, default off and 1 km; shown with the
  draw-on only, checked by `isValidTrackTail`): only the last `lengthM` metres behind each marker are drawn. The pieces
  drawn always start at the first, so the start is hidden by the painting step instead: each piece carries the track
  distance of its ends (`setTrackDistances`, attributes `instanceTrackStart` / `instanceTrackEnd`) and the line and
  halo materials of each set discard what lies before the uniform `trailStartM` = cut distance − `lengthM`
  (`addTrailCut`, own program keys; `NO_TRAIL` = −1 otherwise). Colours by metric and dashes stay on their pieces.
  Each set its own value: every racer and every stage « À la suite » gets its trail; a track drawn whole stays whole.
  Set on every cut, even when the marker has not moved (a paused film follows the slider). The oldest half of the
  trail (`TRAIL_FADE_SHARE`, uniform `trailFadeM`) thins out to nothing: the vertex shader scales the width of each
  piece end (smoothstep), the opacity stays whole (a translucent line would darken at the joints where pieces overlap)
  and the halo shrinks with its line.
- **Markers** (`markerSprite.ts`): one `Sprite` per marker (head: `FlyoverRig`, name `flyover-marker`; race:
  `RaceMarkers`; drawn without depth test so the slope never cuts them, hidden (opacity 0, never half-transparent) behind the relief with the labels'
  line of sight: `reliefClearance`, `occludeMarker`), badge texture drawn on a canvas (`markerBadge.ts`) and cached by key (48 at most). Ball:
  white disk (head) or track color in an ink halo ×1.35 (race) — same look as before. Figurine: 24 × 24 line
  pictogram (Lucide style; bike and car taken from Lucide) on an ink disk edged in white (head) or track color
  edged in ink (race), ink or white stroke depending on luminance. Image: round photo, white edge; racers
  keep their ball. Badge diameter: 2.2 times the ball. Constant screen size: radius = max(1, distance × 0.007)
  × `size`. Colors divided by the exposure, like the lines.
- **Direction**: the figurine is flipped when the track runs from right to left on screen, based on the track points within
  ±60 m of the marker projected with the frame's camera (`headsLeft`, stateless: same image in preview and export).

## Film and timeline (phase 4, increments 1 to 4 of 4: model, engine, timeline, texts, photos and videos; music and video sound, phase 7)

- **Principle**: as in video editing software, "the GPX is the base": the continuous flyover of the first track carries the
  film; items placed on separate lanes are added to it. Increments: 1 pure model and engine (done); 2 timeline below
  the view (lanes, drag to move / stretch, inspector; done); 3 text lane drawn in the overlay (done);
  4 media lane (photos and videos done, with their sound: see "Video sound").
- **Model** (`src/film/model.ts`, `settings.film`: saved in the project document, undoable, validated by
  `isValidFilm` in `SETTING_CHECKS`; an old project receives `DEFAULT_FILM`, and the v1 → v2 migration in `src/project/document.ts` gives a v1 project without a film `{ autoMode: 'rythme' }`):
  `opening` / `closing` `{ style: 'aucune' | 'descente' | 'saut' | 'situation' | 'balayage', durationS, transition?, dipS?,
  startHeight?, highlight? }` (0.5–30 s; default descente 6 s / 5 s; a shot switched to `situation` while it still has its
  default duration gets `SITUATION_DURATION_S`, 9 s, for its long dive (`updateShot`; saved films keep theirs); `transition` `'enchaine'` by default, `'coupe'`,
  `'fondu-noir'`, `'fondu-blanc'`, `dipS` 0.3–2 s, 1 s by default: optional, an old film stays continuous (a dip is centred on the cut and a pure function of the film time, so one longer than a very short shot simply extends past it);
  `startHeight` `'region'` (default) or `'pays'`, `highlight` (default off) and `regionId` (« Lieu », OSM area
  `relation/<id>` or `way/<id>`, absent = automatic) and `holdS` (« Maintien », 0–10 s, `SITUATION_HOLD_RANGE`,
  absent = 0) and its framing, `tiltDeg` (1–60°), `distanceKm` (5–400 km), `heading` (`'libre'` / `'boussole'`),
  `bearingDeg` (0–359°), `headroomPct` (0–30 %), all absent by default (`SituationFraming`), and `sun` (`'jour'` / `'survol'` /
  `'accelere'`) for any shot but `aucune`, with `sunHours` (« Heures de soleil », 1–12 h, `SHOT_SUN_HOURS_RANGE`, absent = `SHOT_SUN_HOURS` = 4 h) for « Accéléré »; absent: « Plein jour » for `situation` (« Accéléré » with an old `moveSun`),
  « Heure du survol » for the other styles,
  see below);
  `autoStops` (generated stops) and `autoMode`: `'temps-forts'` (default for new projects) or `'rythme'` (earlier
  projects);
  `stops[]` `{ id, atM, durationS (0.5–60 s), camera: 'film' | 'orbite' | 'large' | 'fixe', label?, source?: { kind, ref? } }`
  (`STOP_CAMERA_LABELS`: "Comme le film" (like the film), "Tour lent" (slow orbit), "Vue large" (wide view), "Fixe" (fixed));
  `markerKeys[]` `{ id, atM, kind, figure, imageId?, color?, figureColor?, size? }` (places along the film track where the marker changes its look:
  ball, figure or picture, with its own colours and size; anchored in metres like the camera keys, round dots on the « Marqueur » lane
  (`data-testid="timeline-key"`); added from the « Marqueur » section of the Trace tab with ◀ ◉ ▶, `addMarkerKeyHere`). `markerAt(film, settings.marker, atM, fadeM, imageOf)`, pure: the marker of the settings
  before the first key; around a key a smootherstep cross-fade of `fadeM = MARKER_FADE_S (0.8 s) × length / flightS`
  metres centred on the key, from the previous look; a key replaces kind, figure and picture, and `color` / `figureColor` ('' = the track's) / `size`
  where set (`withKey`; unset = the settings', same rules as `isValidMarker`, no version bump), while label, stick and animation stay those of
  the settings; a new key (`markerKeyLook`) copies the stored look of the key before it, else the settings' shape and figure with colours and size unset (they keep following the panel), so adding it changes nothing. The colours and size
  cross-fade through `markerAt` like the rest (`from` / `to` are full settings, two sprites); they enter the texture cache key through `leadMarkerColors`
  and the sprite scale, never the film time, so the export stays deterministic. The timeline dot takes the colour as `--key-color`
  (`figureColor` for a figurine), its title lists shape, colour and size. `FlyoverRig` draws the new look over the old one on a second sprite
  (`flyover-marker-from`) until the fade ends; the export uses the same rig and a stop holds the look.
  `markerKeysMoveWithTime` feeds the held-frame test. Picture of a key: `imageId` in the project media table (128 px
  PNG, kept by `usedMedia`); no id = the picture of the Trace panel. `withFilmDefaults` gives `[]` (no version bump); not
  kept by « Un film par trace »;
  `paramKeys[]` `{ id, atM, param, value }` (a film-wide setting pinned along the first track in metres,
  like the camera keys, so values hold during stops; `param`: `speed` (×0.25–×4), `exposure` (±4 EV), `sunHour` (0–24 h
  solar), `saturation`, `contrast`, `warmth`, `vignette`, `labels` (0–1 opacity of the 3D labels, 1 without a key: read by `Labels`
  per frame, set from « Habillage » › « Étiquettes 3D », hidden under 2 %); `paramAt(film, base, param, atM)`, pure: base value without a
  key, first key's value before it, last key's after it, smootherstep between keys (geometric for the speed). Readers:
  `AtmosphereLayer` (exposure; sun hour: sun keys replace `sunFromTrack`, linear, no wrap past midnight),
  `useGradingEffect` (grading per frame; composer mounted when a grading key exists), the clock (speed keys become a
  `SpeedCurve` of `flightPacing`, multiplying the speed portions and slow-downs; with « Garder la durée » they only
  redistribute time; speed portions are not migrated). A function of the progress only, so the export stays
  deterministic and held frames need no change. Speed keys (◆) are on the « Vitesse » lane with a ◀ ◇ ▶ group in its
  header; every other parameter gets a lane of its own once keyed (`paramLanes`, labels `PARAM_LANE_LABELS`: « Exposition »,
  « Soleil », « Saturation », « Contraste », « Température », « Vignettage », « Étiquettes » (the opacity of the 3D labels, `labels`); the lane disappears with its last key; ◆:
  click selects and goes there, drag moves, Delete removes). Their ◀ ◇ ▶ group (`PanelKeys`, `src/ui/KeyControls.tsx`) is
  under the slider in the left panels: exposure and sun hour in the Lumière tab (`SettingsPanel`), saturation, contrast,
  warmth and vignette in `GradingPanel`. ◇ = `addParamKeyHere(param)`: a key at the marker holding the value the film
  shows there (the setting without key, ×1 for speed), then the playhead is put exactly on it (`parkOnSelectedKey`); on a
  key it removes the key under the playhead (`toggleParamKeyHere`, `toggleMarkerKeyHere`, `toggleKeyframe`: the same button adds and removes). Once a
  parameter has keys its slider edits the key under the playhead and creates one on the first change (`useKeyedValue` in `scene/usePacing.ts`;
  `KeyControls.tsx`, `GradingSlider`). The camera follows the mouse: with the playhead on a camera key, orbiting or zooming writes the
  pose into it as it goes (`FlyoverRig.tsx`: listeners on the OrbitControls start / change / end, `beginGesture()` makes one undo step).
  The timeline's right-click menu (`onContentContextMenu` in `ui/Timeline.tsx`, `data-lane` on the lanes) offers « Ajouter une image clé ici »
  (camera, parameter and marker lanes), « Supprimer » and « Supprimer l'image clé », and works on the selected group. Empty by default, `[]` for a film saved earlier, no version bump);
  `cameraKeys[]` `{ id, atM, distance, pitchDeg, headingOffsetDeg, aimRightM?, aimAheadM? }` (keyframes, « Images
  clés »: exact poses of the flight camera along the first track, not rounded; `CAMERA_KEY_RANGES` (distance ×0.01–×1000,
  pitch 0–90°, any view the orbit can take; heading ±180°), `isValidCameraKey`; empty by default, a film saved earlier
  receives `[]`, keys saved by « Garder ce cadrage ici » load unchanged);
  `speeds[]` `{ id, fromM, toM, factor (×0.25–×4) }` (sections of the first track, in meters, never overlapping,
  they may touch; `isValidSpeed`, empty by default, a film saved earlier receives `[]` through `withFilmDefaults`);
  `texts[]` `{ id, startS, durationS, text, subtitle?, anchor, size, color?, font?, stopId? }` (placement of the overlay text widget;
  own color and font, otherwise those of the overlay) and
  `media[]` `{ id, startS, durationS, kind: 'image' | 'video', src, layout: 'plein-ecran' | 'carte', anchor, size, kenBurns,
  caption?, inS?, outS?, muted?, sync?, stopId? }` (`stopId` on a text or a media item: the stop it is attached to, see
  "Attached to a stop"; `isValidFilm` only requires a non-empty string, `withFilmDefaults` loads an item attached to a
  stop the film does not own (unknown id, generated stops, not a string) free, without rejecting the film; `src` = id of the image or video in the document's media table, see
  "Photos" and "Videos"; video: `inS` / `outS` = start and end of the clip in the file, default its start and end,
  `clipTimeS`; `muted` and `volume` (0–1, default 1): video sound, see "Video sound"; `sync` `{ startMs, offsetS, follow }`: video
  synced to the track, see "Video synced to the route"; `MEDIA_DEFAULTS`: full
  screen, Ken Burns, caption at the bottom left, size 1, also applied by `withFilmDefaults` to a media item saved without these
  fields), and `audio[]` `{ id, src, startS, durationS (0.5 s–1 h), inS, volume (0–1), fadeInS, fadeOutS (0–30 s) }`
  (music tracks, see "Music"; `src` = id of the sound file in the media table; `AUDIO_DEFAULTS`: volume 1, fades
  0.5 s / 2 s; `isValidAudio`; a film saved earlier receives `[]`), anchored in film time, and `duckMusic` ("Baisser la
  musique sous les vidéos" (lower the music under the videos), false by default and for a film saved earlier); `pois[]` `{ id, lon, lat, name }` (points
  of interest placed by hand, see "Points of interest"; a film saved earlier receives `[]`); `nights` `{ enabled, duskS, nightS, dawnS }`
  (« Jours et nuits », 0–20 s each, `NIGHT_PART_RANGE`; on, 3 / 2 / 3 s by default; off for a film saved earlier, so its
  timing stays); `junctions[]`, `junctionDefault` and `firstCard`, optional and absent from a film saved earlier: the
  junctions between the stages « À la suite » and their cards, and `stageDurations` (flight time in seconds, 5–600, by track id, of the stages that set one), see "Several tracks"; ids unique within the film
  (`stop-3`, `text-1`, `media-2`, `music-1`, `poi-2`, `nextFilmId`).
- **Automatic assembly** (`src/film/assemble.ts`): as long as `autoStops` is true, stops are generated on every computation
  (`autoStops`) at the highlights (`stopCandidates`: passes crossed and summits nearby among the landmarks shown, whatever the pacing; climb tops are no
  longer offered: stops come from the landmarks, the points of interest and the timeline; `'rythme'` keeps the pauses of
  the pacing, climb tops included (`climbCandidates`), so older films are unchanged; stops already written into a film,
  climb tops included, stay ordinary stops; one per `film.autoStopGroupM` group (`AUTO_STOP_GROUP_M` = 1 km by default; `'rythme'`:
  `pacing.windowM` and `pacing.landmarks`), like the pauses they replace), id `auto-<meters>`,
  label ("Montée 1 · cat. 3 · 1 653 m", OSM name) and source; they follow OSM landmarks loaded afterwards.
  `'temps-forts'`: one stop per highlight **even with pacing disabled**, `AUTO_STOP_S` (4 s), `orbite` camera (they are all
  summits or passes). `'rythme'`: the pacing pauses from before the timeline (nothing if pacing is disabled,
  `pauseS`, `film` camera, like those pauses before). `materializeStops` writes the generated stops into the film (`autoStops: false`) on the first
  edit of a stop.
- **No slow-down or title at landmarks.** A film does not slow down or add a title card at landmarks by itself: the only
  things it does at a landmark are its stop (« Arrêt », generated by `autoStops` or added by hand) and the 3D labels. The former
  option « Ralentir et titrer aux repères » (`film.landmarkTitles`) was removed as redundant with the stops. **Old projects**: a
  project saved with the option keeps loading without warning; `withFilmDefaults` drops the `landmarkTitles` flag (like
  `epochs`) and the `auto-speed-<OSM id>` / `auto-text-<OSM id>` items it had written stay as ordinary speed sections and texts,
  editable and deletable. A new film has none.
- **Old projects**: a film saved without `autoMode` is completed by `withFilmDefaults` (`SETTING_UPGRADES`, also for
  presets) with `'rythme'`; a v1 project without a film receives `{ autoMode: 'rythme' }` through the v1 → v2 migration
  (`MIGRATIONS[1]`), completed the same way: their stops remain those of their pacing.
- **Presets**: only the film's opening and closing shots are saved; `stops`, `speeds`, `texts`, `media`,
  `audio` (and `autoStops`) belong to the track and remain those of the current project when applied (`presetSettings`, including for
  a preset saved with the whole film).
- **Film clock** (`src/film/clock.ts`, `buildFilmClock` / `filmClockFor`, `useFilmClock` hook in
  `src/scene/usePacing.ts`; in the same place `getFilmSource` outside React and
  `editFilm(edit, { stops, step })`: edit of the stores' film in one undo step, generated stops written first
  for a stop, selection of the block returned by the edit): film time (s at ×1 from the first frame) →
  `stateAt(t)` = `{ phase: 'opening' | 'flight' | 'stop' | 'closing', progress, flightTimeS, stop, localS, lengthS }`.
  [0, O) opening (progress 0), [O, O + F) flight, [O + F, total] closing (progress 1). The flight is `flightPacing`:
  pacing slow-downs at highlights, film speed sections, film stops inserted with raised-cosine entry and
  exit (≤ 1.5 s), each with its own duration; `keepDuration` keeps F = `flyoverDurationS` (stops ≤ 50%, shortened
  proportionally; a sped-up section slows the rest down by as much; a stage « À la suite » with its own duration flies that long, see
  "Several tracks"). **Speed sections** (`flightPacing(…, speeds)`): the
  local speed (slow-downs included) is multiplied by m(x) = factor^w(x) over [fromM, toM], w rising from 0 to 1 as a raised
  cosine over `pacing.transitionS` ("Transitions", 0.5–4 s, default `SPEED_EASE_S` = 1.5 s; the same length bounds the
  ease into and out of pauses and stops) at base speed, inside the section (at most half of it), and
  falling back the same way at its end: never a speed jump; 32 grid steps per transition, inverse always exact.
  Applied whether pacing is active or not, combined with stops (a stop inside a section remains a stop). The clock
  places them in film time (`clock.speeds`: `startS` / `endS` = marker passing the edges). A stop's window
  (entry, hold, exit) is the `stop` phase. `timeAtProgress(0)` = start of the flight (after the opening), `timeAtProgress(1)` =
  end of the film; starting playback from the beginning or the end without a film time starts from the first frame (`setPlaying` sets
  `timeS = 0`), opening included. Without opening or closing and without edits, the clock replays the old pacing exactly
  (equivalence tests).
- **Camera** (`src/flyover/filmCamera.ts`, pure): overview = center of the track's box in the local frame at ground level,
  distance 1.6 × diagonal (terrain included, × height / width for a frame taller than wide: 9:16 keeps the whole
  track), 40° above the horizon, on the side the flight camera looks from at the cut (no turn during the transition).
  Transition: interpolated target, tilt and heading interpolated apart (`blendDirections`: the heading the short way round, never through the zenith), geometric distance, smootherstep, 80 m above the ground;
  in between (not at the wide view nor at the flight view, eased in over the first and last 10% of the blend) the camera is
  also raised (`clearOnTheWay`, pure: sampled terrain only, so preview and export agree) to a glide slope of
  `SHOT_GLIDE_DEG` = 15° over the flight camera (no dive far from the start of the track), to `SHOT_CLEARANCE_SHARE` = 8% of its
  distance (at least 80 m) above the ground under it, and so that the line to the aim clears the relief (16 samples up to 90%
  of the way, the clearance tapering to 20 m): the straight blend knows nothing of the ridges between the two views.
  Its aim's height is blended as straight (from the region's ground 40 km away to the track's, say), so the aim can pass
  hundreds of metres inside a slope: the line ends on the ground above the aim, never inside it (the camera rode 10× that
  depth, a 500 m jump up and back down in half a second, Ariège opening at 5.7 s), and like the flight's sight line
  (`LINE_OF_SIGHT_FINE_GAIN`) the samples whose lift passes 3× the ground under them ease out up to 6×. Test: a ridge
  under the aim's way, every style, 30 i/s, the change of motion from one frame to the next under 0.005 of the
  distance (0.11 before);
  `descente` over the whole shot duration, `saut` holds the overview then moves in 0.6 s. `balayage`: the
  overview turns by `SWEEP_DEG` = 75° around its target (`turnedView`, `sweepRad`) during the first 60% of the opening
  shot and ends on the flight side, then glides like `descente` (closing: the reverse). `situation` ("Depuis la
  région" (from the region), establishing shot): like `descente`, but from (or, when closing, to) the region view
  (`regionView`): same side as the overview, 65° above the horizon, distance `regionDistanceM` =
  8 × diagonal clamped to 55–165 km for the "Région" start height (`startHeight: 'region'`), × 2.5 for "Pays"
  (country), × height / width in portrait. With `highlight` and a region found, the view aims at the centre of the
  region's box and its distance fits that box in the frame (margin 1.15) instead. Bounded so that the farthest corner
  of the frame (`groundReach`, flat ground, 50° vertical field of view) stays within 400 km of the track's box
  (`REGION_REACH_M`; the terrain area of a film with a region view reaches 500 km, `REGION_AREA_MARGIN_M`), never
  closer than the overview. A single move (geometric distance, smootherstep), which passes the overview distance on the way
  without stopping there: no jerk, never backing up. **Hold and push-in** (« Durées » in the inspector: « Maintien »,
  then « Plongée » at the opening, « Remontée » then « Maintien » at the closing): `holdS` of the shot's `durationS`
  is spent still on the region view (`situationTiming`: hold + move = duration, so the clock and the timeline keep
  reading `durationS`; a move made longer than 30 s in all shortens the hold), the smootherstep move takes the rest
  (`shotBlend(…, holdS)`). Without a hold (every film saved before), the move takes the whole shot: exactly the
  curve of before. **Framing** (« Cadrage », `regionView(…, { framing })`, pure): « Écart à la verticale » `tiltDeg` from the
  vertical (default 25°, `SITUATION_TILT_DEFAULT_DEG`, = `REGION_PITCH_DEG` 65° above the horizon); « Distance »
  `distanceKm` used as is instead of the automatic distance (« Auto » at the left of the slider; no « Pays » or
  portrait factor, « Hauteur » greyed out), still within the reach of the terrain for that tilt and never nearer
  than the overview; « Cap » « Libre » (the overview's side, as before) or « Boussole » (looking toward
  `bearingDeg`, « Orientation », 0° = north up: camera on the opposite side of the target); « Marge » (`headroomPct`,
  a row): view and target moved up along the frame's vertical by 2 · h · distance · tan(25°), so the
  target sits h of the frame height below the middle. « Capturer la vue actuelle »: `FlyoverRig` registers
  (`registerFramingCapture`, `src/osm/region.ts`, so the UI does not import three.js) a reader of its camera around
  `situationTarget` (region centre when the shot highlights and it is loaded, else the track's), and
  `situationFramingOf` turns it into tilt, distance and bearing, rounded and clamped (heading set to « Boussole »).
  A compass bearing opposite to the flight's direction turns the camera round all along the push-in, its tilt easing
  from one view's to the other's (`blendDirections`, as for every shot; the 3D direction was nlerped before #211 and
  went through the zenith there: a nose-down dive spinning 145° in half a second). **Sun of the shot** (« Soleil », `shotSunOf`, every style but « Aucune », opening and closing): « Plein jour » (default
  of a `situation` shot) lights the wide view (overview or region view) at
  `SHOT_DAYLIGHT_HOUR` (13 h solar) on the day of the outing and eases to the flight's sun with the same curve as
  below, so a dawn or evening outing does not open on a dark region; « Heure du survol » keeps the flight's sun (default
  of the other styles, so a track ending at night still closes in the dark unless « Plein jour » is chosen);
  « Accéléré » is a time lapse
  of `shotSunHoursOf(shot)` (`sunHours`, 4 h by default; it was a fixed 2 h before, too short to see the shadows move) of the day over the whole shot, added to the sun date of the frame
  (`shotSunShiftMs`; both through `shotSunDate` in `src/flyover/sun.ts`, applied by `AtmosphereLayer`, so the clouds and the
  weather under the marker follow it): opening −H · (1 − u)², closing +H · u² (H = the shot's hours, u = share of the shot). Chosen as
  the simplest that joins the flight: the shot ends (starts) exactly at the flight's own sun, with the sun slowing to
  a stop there, so no jump of light or of shadow speed at the boundary; the shadows sweep the relief seen from above.
  No setting of its own (hours, direction): a start before dawn simply rises the sun. A pure function of the film
  time: preview and export light the same frames. Only with the atmosphere (the fixed lights have no sun date). The camera far plane (5,000 km) limits nothing; seen from above,
  the engine picks coarse tiles (loading in time: the manual checks of `docs/handbook/index.html`). **Region highlight** ("Mettre en avant la
  région" (highlight the region) in the shot inspector, under "Hauteur de départ" (start height), "Hauteur de fin" for
  the closing; `highlight`, off by default: no Overpass request and an unchanged framing for every film saved before,
  the user opts in): while the opening or the closing highlights it (`highlightsRegion`), `RegionHighlight` calls
  `syncRegion` with the first track's box and the place chosen (`src/osm/region.ts`: one Overpass `is_in` request for
  the areas around the centre of the box, tags and box only: admin levels 4 to 6, `boundary=national_park` /
  `protected_area`, `leisure=nature_reserve`, `place=island`, `natural=mountain_range`, relations and closed ways;
  those containing the whole box, smallest first, are offered in « Lieu » (`containingRegions`, store `candidates`,
  published as soon as this query answers, before the geometry);
  `regionId` of the first shot that highlights (`filmRegionId`; `setFilmPlace` writes both shots: one place per film,
  one region in the store), else the automatic choice, unchanged: the smallest administrative boundary that contains
  the track's box and is at least 5 times larger (`chooseRegion`, `autoId`); a saved place no longer offered falls
  back to it. Then the geometry of the chosen area only, rings stitched with `stitchRings` (a closed way is one ring)
  and simplified to 2,000 points, same queue and cache as the landmarks); `FlyoverRig` and the export frame it
  (`useRegionStore.frame`), and the camera is placed again when it arrives, except during a video export: the export
  holds the region it started with (`holdRegion`, none if it was still loading) and an answer arriving meanwhile is
  applied at its end, so neither the framing nor the highlight changes mid-film. No boundary, offline or a failed query
  (`status` `'none'` / `'error'`, said in the inspector's hint, « Réessayer » after an error: `retryRegion`): no
  highlight and the framing of the track, nothing in the console. Drawing (`src/scene/RegionHighlight.tsx`, geometry in `regionMesh.ts`, pure): the outside darkened
  (ink at 55 %) by one mesh, a box `REGION_AREA_MARGIN_M` around the region with the outer rings cut out and the
  enclaves filled (even-odd by ring depth, earcut on an equirectangular plane); the border as two `LineSegments2`
  sharing one geometry, a 16 px white glow (`createGlowMaterial`, max blending) and a 2 px white line; an orange dot
  (`LABEL_KIND_ACCENTS.climb`) at the centre of the track's box; the name (OSM `name`, bilingual names kept) at
  `labelPoint`, the cell of a 32 × 32 grid inside the region farthest from its border and from the dot, slightly
  pulled to the middle. The border points, the dot and the name are draped like the track (re-drape debounced, flushed
  by the export); everything skips the depth test (seen from almost straight above, the long triangles of the mask
  cannot be occluded correctly anyway), drawn after the terrain and the track line, under the marker
  and the labels. Unlit colours divided by the exposure. Opacity `regionHighlightOpacity(clock, timeS)`, pure: from the
  share of the region view in the shot (1 − `shotWeight` at the opening, `shotWeight` at the closing), smootherstep
  between 0.55 and 0.85 (`REGION_HIGHLIGHT_FADE`): whole at the top and during the hold, gone a little before the
  middle of the dive, back as the closing climbs; held whole until the cut with "Coupe" or a fade. Shots always move with time
  (`filmViewMovesWithTime`), so the export draws every frame of it; hidden on the poster's overview still. OpenStreetMap
  is credited while a region is loaded (status bar, burned-in credits). Stop camera: `film`, the
  flight camera continues (the marker holds, so does the camera, except the orbit and cinematic styles, which keep their motion);
  `orbite`, rotation around the marker of 6°/s × duration (≤ 120°) out and back; `large`, distance × 2.5 and pitch + 20°
  in the middle of the window, out and back (`widenedCamera`); both follow the bump (1 − cos 2πx) / 2 of the window
  (`stopBump`), zero and still at its edges; `fixe`, the framing holds, including the motion of the orbit and
  cinematic styles: their time glides toward the middle of the window during the entry, stays there during the hold, comes back during
  the exit (`heldMotionTimeS`, smootherstep, never backward, no jump). The orbit and cinematic styles follow the
  flight time (`flightTimeS`), so they start where the opening hands them over.
  **Keyframes** (`keyPoseAt`, `keyView`, pure): a keyframe is the pose of the camera around the film's aim point at its
  place (`flight.target`: time smoothing of the aim included), not camera settings: distance × `autoDistanceM`, pitch
  above the horizon, heading from `keyReferenceAt` (the direction of travel over the default turn window, averaged over
  four of them: the track alone sets it, so a key keeps its pose when the camera settings change), aim point shifted by
  `aimRightM` / `aimAheadM` (a panned view). `keyView` places it with no style, time smoothing, pitch clamp or ground
  clearance, so the film shows the very view that was taken; a « Vue large » or « Tour lent » stop widens or turns it
  like the film's camera. Capture (`keyFramingAt` → `keyFramingFrom`, the inverse): the aim point is taken on the line
  of sight at the height of the film's aim point, so a panned or refitted orbit keeps its look; a stop's widening and
  orbit at that time are taken out. `computeFilmView` blends the flight camera into `keyView` (`blendViews`): between
  two keys weight 1, the pose from one to the other in smootherstep over distance (linear in distance and pitch, heading
  the shortest way, aim shift linear: `keyedCamera` gives the same blend of the first three for « Cadrer la caméra
  pendant cet élément »), raised above the ground (`keepAboveGround`) by a share 4u(1 − u) that is 0 at the keys; before
  the first and after the last, that key's pose with a weight easing in and out over `cameraKeyEaseM` (3 s of flight at
  base speed, `CAMERA_KEY_EASE_S`); elsewhere the film's camera. Field of view: `CAMERA_FOV_DEG` everywhere. At the key's
  film time (`clock.cameraKeys[].timeS`) the view equals the capture (tests: under 1 cm and 0.01° with a cinematic
  style, time smoothing and relief; e2e `image-cle`: orbit, « Ajouter une image clé », away and back with « Image clé
  précédente », Alt+← / Alt+→ and a click on the ◆, within 5 cm and 0.1°). A function of progress alone between stops. Style `free` (« Libre », `freeView`): no flight camera at all
  (no stops, smoothing or style motion); each key is placed around its own place on the track (`freeKeyBase`: the marker
  and the direction of travel at `atM`, also used by `keyFramingAt`), so its pose is fixed in the scene; the first key's
  pose up to it, the last one's after it, smootherstep from one to the next over the film time (`timeS` of the keys: it keeps gliding through a stop,
  so `filmViewMovesWithTime` is true for `free`; raised by 4u(1 − u) like above); without
  keys, `overviewView` seen along the start's direction of travel, fixed for the whole film.
  The marker stays on the track (`view.marker`); the target is the flight's aim point (smoothed ground height
  above the marker) during the flight.
- **Shot transitions** (`transition` of the opening, at its end, and of the closing, at its start; "Transition" in the
  shot inspector): "Enchaîné" (continuous) = the continuous move above. "Coupe" (cut), "Fondu au noir" (fade to
  black), "Fondu au blanc" (fade to white): the shot holds its wide view (overview, or region view) and the camera cuts
  at the boundary with the flight (`shotWeight`: weight 0 during the whole opening, 1 during the whole closing; the
  style only chooses the wide view). A fade adds a full-frame black or white layer (`transitionDipAt`, `dipAlpha`,
  pure) centred on the cut, `dipS` long (half in the shot, half in the flight), smootherstep opacity from 0 at the
  edge to 1 at the cut: the camera jumps during the darkest frame, one render per frame. No true cross-dissolve
  between two 3D views (two renders per frame). No transition of their own for stops: their camera leaves the flight
  view and comes back to it (`stopBump`), there is no other view to cut to.
