- **Preview and export**: `FlyoverRig` and `ExportController` call the same `computeFilmView` with the same clock (the
  width / height ratio comes from the render size, the video size during the export). The camera is repositioned while paused
  and an export frame is recomputed when the progress changes **or** when the time changes while the view depends on it
  (`filmViewMovesWithTime`: opening and closing shots, orbit or wide-view stops, orbit and cinematic styles, at the current
  or previous frame). The export panel reads the duration and `progressAtTime` from the same clock (`useFilmClock`).
- **Film time preserved**: when the clock changes (film edit, duration, pacing), `FlyoverRig` keeps `playback.timeS`
  and derives the progress from it: an edit while paused does not make the playhead jump.
- **Timeline** (`src/ui/Timeline.tsx`, pure logic in `src/film/timeline.ts`, inspector `src/ui/FilmInspector.tsx`):
  strip below the 3D view (the stage above, the view shrinks accordingly; ~150 px, collapsible lanes). Bar, a single
  control style (Lucide icon buttons, `Tip` tooltip with the shortcut from the registry, short label hidden at
  1280 px): play, ■ (pause and back to the first frame), time `m:ss,d / m:ss`, distance / elevation / time at the
  marker, "Arrêt" (stop; S: at the marker position, to the meter, source `manual`, 4 s, orbit), "Arrêt à un temps fort…" (stop at a highlight)
  (passes and summits without a stop), "Texte" (text; T: at the playhead, 4 s, bottom center), "Média" (media; see "Photos" and
  "Videos"), speaker (mutes or unmutes the preview sound, music and videos, `useMusicPreview`, shown when the film has
  music or a video with sound; neither saved nor undoable, the export keeps the sound), speed,
  zoom (− / logarithmic slider / + / "Ajuster" (fit) = the whole film), "Options" menu ("Ajouter une musique…" (add music), see
  "Music"; "Arrêts automatiques" (automatic stops) checkbox, `setAutoStops`:
  checked, `autoStops` + `'temps-forts'`, own stops cleared; unchecked, generated stops written; "modifié" /
  ↺ "modifié" button for the film, `ModifiedMarker keys={['film']}`; a dot on the button when the film departs from the default; it
  closes with Esc, a click outside or Tab), collapse.
  Ruler (`rulerTicks`, step from 1 s to 1 h depending on the zoom, ≥ 56 px): click-drag to seek, **opening and closing
  included** (`setProgress(clock.progressAtTime(t), t)`, end of film = progress 1 without a time); it is also a keyboard
  slider (arrows ±1 s, Shift ±5 s, Page ±10 s, Home / End). The keys are set where they live. Camera keys: the ◀ ◇ ▶ group (`KeyGroup`,
  `src/ui/KeyControls.tsx`: previous key / add or remove at the playhead / next key; Alt+← / Alt+→ unchanged) sits under the label
  of the « Plans » lane header; on a portrait phone (centred timeline, no lane headers) it leads the sideways-scrolling
  tool row. Lanes: « Plans », « Vitesse », one lane per keyed parameter, « Marqueur » (only with marker keys), « Arrêts »,
  « Textes », « Médias », « Musique »; « Textes » also carries the opening title and the closing card (see below); there is no « Réglages » lane and the bar has no « Ajouter un réglage » or
  « Ajouter un changement de marqueur » button. `seekKeyframe` puts the film time exactly on the key
  (`adjacentKey`, one under the playhead skipped) and moves the inspector to it when it showed a keyframe. Lanes "Plans" (shots; opening, flyover with profile, closing;
  "aucune" (none) = selectable dotted stub; stops are also marked on the flyover bar, tinted window
  and top edge in the accent color, white for the selected stop; keyframes are small diamonds (◆) where the marker passes,
  selectable, focusable and draggable; a click without dragging (a tap on a phone) also seeks to it), "Arrêts" (stops; window of each stop, entry and exit
  included; dotted while they are generated), "Textes" (texts), "Médias" (media; photos and videos, with their thumbnail; a
  video also carries a small camera icon). "Vitesse" (speed) lane under "Plans": one block per section, from its entry to
  its exit in film time, label "×2" (solid frame if sped up, dotted if slowed down); "Vitesse" in the bar
  (`addSpeed`: ×2 over 1 km from the marker, shortened or moved forward to stay on the track and outside the other
  sections; message if the marker is already in a section or if there are not 50 m free). "Musique" (music) lane under
  "Médias": one block per music track, file name, note icon and waveform (`waveformPath`, the part of the file
  played, as a symmetric band, scaled to the loudest passage); when empty, it says where to drop a file.
- **Gestures** (`dragFilm`, pure): dragging a stop moves it along the track — the start of its hold follows the pointer,
  position found by bisection on the clock (`stopPositionAt`, 32 steps); its right edge lengthens it (proportionally to the
  `keepDuration` cap); a text or a photo moves or stretches by either edge; the inner edge of the opening changes its duration; the inner edge of the
  closing is a roll edit (`rollClosing`): the end of the film stays, the closing starts at the pointer and the flight
  duration (`flyoverDurationS`, one undo step with the film) takes the difference; the left edge of a video also moves its start in the file (`inS`, never before the
  start of the file: the frames stay in place); a music track moves, its right edge never goes past the end of the
  file (`DragContext.audioFileS`), its left edge moves its start in the file like a video; a speed section moves (length in meters kept) or
  stretches by either edge, the held edge follows the pointer in film time, position found in meters by bisection
  on the clock of the edited section (`DragContext.clockOfSpeeds`, like a stop), never on its neighbors, 50 m at
  least (`MIN_SPEED_SPAN_M`), to the meter; a framing moves: its place is that of the marker at the target time
  (`clock.progressAtTime`, to the meter). The **magnet**: `snapTargets` returns named targets (`SnapTarget` `{ timeS, label, kind }`:
  start and end of the film, edges and centres of shots, stops, speed portions, texts, photos, music, junctions and the
  cards, the camera, parameter and marker keys, highlights, ruler ticks, playhead; the dragged item left out); `snapTime`
  catches the nearest within 8 px and lets go beyond 14 px (hysteresis: `SnapState.held`, the targets caught by the previous pointer move),
  `hits` being every target at the time it lands on; `snapper(ctx)` is the `snap` of a gesture and records the hits in
  `ctx.snapState`, which the component draws as an alignment guide (a line across the lanes and the
  names of the targets, `data-testid="timeline-guide-tag"`). A moved stop snaps to highlights in meters (`snapValue`). The magnet button of the bar
  (`Preferences.timelineMagnet`, per device, on by default) turns it off, Alt inverts it while dragging. Values
  bounded to the model ranges, rounded to the hundredth of a second (moved stop: to the meter). A press without moving
  3 px only selects. On a block that moves, an edge grip only takes a press in the outer third of its side
  (`pressedGrip`): a narrow block (a 4 s stop on a phone) is moved, not stretched, although the 16 px touch grips or
  the browser's touch adjustment reach its middle. The gesture is shown on the timeline only (local draft) and committed on release in **one
  undo step** (`history.transaction`); the scale stays that of the committed film during the gesture. Keyboard on a block:
  arrows ±1 s (Shift ±0.1 s, steps merged like a slider), Delete / Backspace deletes (a shot becomes 'aucune'),
  Esc deselects; Space starts / stops playback outside fields and buttons. Ctrl+wheel zooms around the pointer
  (`zoomAt`, ×1 to ×50), − / + buttons and slider around the playhead, "Ajuster" goes back to ×1; native horizontal
  scrolling. **Auto-scroll**: zoomed, a drag (a block already moved past the threshold, or the ruler) within 48 px of
  an edge scrolls the lanes (`edgeScrollSpeed`, pure: linear up to 900 px/s at the edge and beyond), a
  `requestAnimationFrame` loop that replays the gesture at the same pointer (the gesture's offset counts the scroll
  since the press, sub-pixel remainder carried over), stopped on release or cancel; a mere press near the edge does not scroll.
- **Attached to a stop** (`stopId`, pure in `timeline.ts`): `attachToStop` attaches a text or a video (moved to the start
  of the stop's hold, duration kept) or a photo (fitted to the hold: shown while the marker holds), or frees it (time
  kept). `followStops(before, after, clockOf, clockBefore?)` runs on every edit of the film (`editFilm`, the timeline's
  `commit` and `change`, the draft of a gesture) and on
  the flyover duration and pacing (`setFlightTiming`: the setting and the film in one change, `clockBefore` = the clock
  before it; their "Par défaut": `followFlightTiming` inside the `resetSettings` step) and when the first track's
  landmarks are published again (`followLandmarks` from the `LandmarkPanel` effect, `clockBefore` = the clock with the
  landmarks published before, since the pacing slows down at landmarks; not an undo step, landmarks are not an edit):
  an item the edit did not move itself keeps its offset to the start of the hold (an
  item fitted to the hold stays fitted, stretched with the stop) when the stop moves in film time (drag, position,
  duration, other stop, speed portions, shots); an item whose stop is no longer one of the film's own stops (deleted,
  "Arrêts automatiques" checked again) becomes free where it is; moving the attached item changes its offset.
  A **free** text or medium (no `stopId`) the edit did not touch keeps its place on the route through the same
  calls (`routeTime(t, from, to)`): in the opening it keeps its time from the start of the film, in the closing its time
  from the end of the flight, in a stop or junction hold (same id) its offset to the start of the hold, elsewhere it is at
  the film time where the marker reaches the position it had (`progressAtTime` of the old clock, `timeAtProgress` of the
  new); its duration is unchanged. So a slowed or sped-up portion, a stop, a shot or the flyover duration moves what was
  placed after it with the place it illustrated. The music (`audio`) is not tied to a place and keeps its film seconds,
  and so does everything when the stages are put in another order (the cuts of the two clocks differ). The speed keys
  (`paramKeys` of `speed`) count as a change of the film in time.
  Inspector: "Attaché à : Aucun arrêt / <arrêt>" (attached to: no stop / a stop) for a text, a photo or a video
  (attaching to a generated stop writes the stops out first; one undo step); T with a stop selected adds the text
  attached to it (`addText(film, startS, stop)`, same undo step). Landmarks and stops: the generated stops
  follow the landmarks but carry nothing; written out, a stop keeps its meters and only its film time moves with the
  slow-downs. Landmarks are not saved but fetched again: the first ones published for a track (project opened, track
  imported) move nothing, the film was saved with the times they gave.
- **Selection**: `filmSelection` in the store (added at the end of the state, neither saved nor undoable), set by a press or
  focus on a block and by additions (`editFilm` selects the new block), cleared by Esc, closing
  the inspector, a deletion, or when the block no longer exists (undo, other track, generated stops moved:
  `hasFilmItem`, `Timeline` effect).
- **Several keys** (`filmKeys` in the store, next to `filmSelection`, neither saved nor undoable): the camera, parameter and marker keys can be
  selected together. Ctrl (Cmd) + drag from the background of the lanes draws a rectangle (gesture `marquee`, in the content's coordinates so that
  it survives the scroll and the edge auto-scroll; the keys it touches are read from their elements, `data-item`, and shown pressed while it is
  drawn) and selects them on release; the first one takes the focus. Ctrl + click on a key (Ctrl + Enter or Space on a focused key) adds it or
  removes it. `setFilmKeys(ids)` keeps the group at two keys or more; one key is the block selected on its own (`filmSelection`, the inspector),
  none clears both, and `setFilmSelection` replaces any group. A plain drag on the background still moves the playhead (the rectangle needs Ctrl:
  there is none on a touch screen, whose drag is the playhead's or the scroll's). Pressing a key of the group drags them all (`dragKeys`, pure:
  the key under the pointer is snapped like a key alone, with the keys that move left out of the magnet's targets, `snapTargets(…, except)` takes
  several ids; the others keep their gaps in film time; the shift is bounded so that the first and last key stay on the flight; each key goes
  through `updateCameraKey` / `updateParamKey` / `updateMarkerKey`), released without moving it selects that key alone and goes to it. A drag, the
  arrows (±1 s, Shift ±0.1 s) and Delete on a key of the group act on all of them, each in one undo step (`removeFilmItem` for each key inside a
  single `editFilm`). Esc clears the group (`App`'s Escape chain, like `filmSelection`); a key that goes away (undo) leaves it.
- **Timeline keys and navigation** (`src/ui/shortcuts.ts`, `App.tsx` `SeekShortcuts`, `film/timeline.ts`): K plays / pauses like Space;
  J / L step one frame of `settings.video.fps` back / forward (Shift: ten, `FRAME_LONG_STEP`; `seekTime`); Delete removes the
  selected block whatever the focus (until now only a focused block took it), Ctrl/Cmd+D duplicates a text or a photo just after
  it (`duplicateFilmItem`: own id, no stop, none for a clip synced with the track), X cuts a text or a photo in two at the
  playhead (`splitFilmItem`: pieces of at least `SPLIT_MIN_S` = 0.5 s; not videos nor music, whose place in the file would have to
  follow); the new item is selected. On a zoomed timeline the plain wheel scrolls sideways (Ctrl+wheel still zooms) and, while
  the film plays, the lanes page to keep the playhead in view (`followScroll`: it stays 10% of the view from the edges, paged
  back to a quarter of it).
- **Work area** (`src/film/workArea.ts`, `useAppStore.workArea`, `FlyoverRig`, ruler of `Timeline.tsx`): I / O set the in / out point at
  the playhead (`markIn`, `markOut`: the other end goes to the edge of the film when it would no longer be at least
  `WORK_AREA_MIN_S` = 0.5 s away), Shift+X clears it, either end of the band on the ruler is dragged (`dragWorkArea`, a `range`
  gesture). While the film plays, a step that crosses the out point comes back to the in point by as much as it went past
  (`loopTime`, applied in `FlyoverRig`'s frame loop after `clock.advance`; a playhead already past the out point plays on). Not
  part of the film: not saved, not an undo step, cleared with the tracks and when the project is reset, clamped to the film
  (`clampWorkArea`) when it gets shorter. The export still renders the whole film (a range export would need `fromS` / `toS` in the
  schedule, the request and the audio mix).
- **Foldable and lockable lanes** (`src/ui/laneState.ts`, `Timeline.tsx` heads): every lane has an id (`stages`, `shots`, `param:<p>`,
  `marker`, `holds`, `texts`, `media`, `music`) and its head carries two buttons (they show on hover or focus, and always on touch
  or once used): fold (a strip `FOLDED_HEIGHT` = 16 px, the blocks `invisible`, still in the page: the e2e counts them by label)
  and lock (the lane dimmed). Both set `inert` on the lane (no focus, no pointer: a press falls through to the scrub, the
  right-click menu finds no block), through `laneProps(id)`. Kept per device in `localStorage` (`openflyover.lanes.v1`:
  `{ folded, locked }`, `parseLaneState` drops what is unreadable), not in the project, no undo step; not applied to the phone's
  centred timeline. A selected block of a locked lane stays selected: Delete / Ctrl+D act on the selection. The head column is
  wider (112 px, 128 on touch) and shows the full name in a tooltip; edges (`GRIP`) are 10 px wide and keys have an 8 px
  invisible margin (`before:`) for the mouse.
- **Timeline view helpers** (`film/timeline.ts`, `Timeline.tsx`, « Réglages › Timeline »): **zoom to the selection** (button in the zoom
  group and the Z key, `zoomToSpan`): the selected block or key (its span read from where it is drawn, `spanOfItem`), else the work
  area, else the whole film, fills the view with 15 % of margin at each side (`ZOOM_SPAN_MARGIN`, at least `ZOOM_SPAN_MIN_S` =
  2 s), the zoom within `ZOOM_RANGE`, the stretch centred (the scroll is applied at once when the zoom does not change, else after the
  zoom through `pendingScrollRef`). **Timecode in frames** (`preferences.timelineFrames`, off by default): the bar shows `mm:ss:ff`
  at `settings.video.fps` (`formatFrameTimecode`) instead of seconds and tenths; the ruler keeps seconds. **Magnet by family**
  (`preferences.timelineSnap`: `SNAP_GROUPS` = the playhead, the blocks and keys (`edge`, `centre`, `end`), the ruler's marks, the
  junctions and highlights (`snapGroupOf`, `snapTargetsOn` in `dragContext`)): each can be turned off, Alt still inverts the magnet.
- **Filmstrip of a video clip** (`src/ui/Filmstrip.tsx`, `readFilmstrip` / `filmstripTimes` in `film/video.ts`): the block of a video on the
  « Médias » lane gets frames along its length behind its label (`count` tiles of 40 px: `filmstripCount` takes the largest of 2, 4, 8,
  12 that fits, none under 80 px; 64 px JPEG tiles at the middle of each equal part of the clip's `inS`–`outS`, `CanvasSink.canvasesAtTimestamps`
  of mediabunny). Read lazily and once per clip, stretch and count (a `WeakMap` per clip, kept for the session, never in the project), one
  clip at a time (a promise chain: the preview and the export decode too) and not while an export runs; a clip that cannot be read keeps
  its first-frame thumbnail only.
- **Blocks picked together, copied and rippled** (`film/timeline.ts`, `Timeline.tsx`, `App.tsx` `SeekShortcuts`, `ui/blockClipboard.ts`): the texts, media and
  music (`timedBlockIds`, the items placed by film time) can be picked together with Ctrl / Cmd + click (`toggleBlock`; a group is only of blocks or only of
  keys: `filmKeys` holds either, `focusKey` keeps it when a block is focused), dragged by one of them (`dragBlocks`: the shift `dragFilm` gives the lead, magnet
  included, applied to all, none before the film's start), removed together (Delete anywhere, `removeFilmItem` for each, one undo step). **Ripple**
  (« décalage en chaîne »): Shift + Delete, or « Supprimer et fermer l'espace » in the right-click menu, removes blocks and moves what follows in each lane
  earlier by their length (`rippleRemove`; what is attached to a stop follows the stop, not this); Shift held while an edge is dragged moves the following
  blocks of the lane by as much as that end moved (`rippleDrag`, `rippleRef`). Ctrl / Cmd + C copies the picked blocks (`copyBlocks`: in memory for the session,
  `blockClipboard.ts`, not the system clipboard, so a text selected on the page still copies as before), Ctrl / Cmd + V pastes them with their gaps, the earliest at the playhead
  (`pasteBlocks`: own ids, attached to no stop, a medium whose file is gone and a clip synced with the track left out), the pasted ones left picked. Stops, speeds and
  keys are not copied or rippled (their place is by metres: a stop shifts the whole clock).
- **Opening title and closing card on the timeline** (`src/film/cards.ts`): « Textes » carries them while the card's own switch
  (`overlay.title.enabled` / `overlay.end.enabled`) is on, whatever `overlay.enabled` (which only shows the live widgets; the
  cards and the timeline texts are drawn with it off too, as plain cards since the style's structure goes with the live
  widgets; both off by default; project v5 switched off the cards of an overlay that was off) (fixed ids `card-title` and `card-end`, `cardShown`,
  `cardWindow`). They are blocks of the overlay settings, not film items: `overlay.title` / `overlay.end` stay the
  source of truth. Until a card is placed, its window is the one of earlier versions: the title block runs from film time 0 to
  `openingS + title.end × flightS`, the closing block from `openingS + end.start × flightS` to the last frame. The user then
  places it freely (there is no « Apparition » slider any more): dragging an edge or the whole block sets `title.window` /
  `end.window` (`CardWindow`: film seconds, `0 ≤ startS < endS ≤ CARD_WINDOW_MAX_S`, optional; `dragCard`: the edge that moves
  or, for `move`, the start with the length kept, snapped, inside the film and at least `CARD_MIN_S` = 0.5 s long, to a
  thousandth) and the card fades in and out inside it (`titleCardOpacityOf`, `endCardOpacityOf`; a closing window that ends
  with the film stays whole to the last frame; `creditsRollProgress` rolls inside the window). A window is read through `fitWindow(window, totalS)` (`overlay/settings.ts`, used by `cardWindow`,
  the two opacity functions and `creditsRollProgress`) because the film may not be the length it was placed in (flyover
  duration, a shot or a stop changed): `CardWindow.toEnd` (set by `dragCard` when an edge reaches the end of the film) keeps
  the length and glues the card to the end, any other window that would pass the end slides back to it, none is longer than
  the film. `end` and `start` stay valid
  and are read while no window exists, so earlier projects play as before. The « Ouverture » / « Clôture » shots of
  « Plans » are separate and no longer dragged by the card's body. Arrows move the whole block (Shift: 0.1 s),
  committed on release as one undo step through `setSetting('overlay', …)`. Arrows nudge the same edge (Shift: 0.1 s). Delete,
  or « Supprimer » in the inspector (and on the phone's block bar), switches the card off (`disableCard`: `enabled: false`,
  settings kept, one undo step, selection cleared); switching it on again from the panel brings the block back. Selection,
  focus and tap-to-select on the centred phone timeline are those of the other blocks.
- **Inspector** (`FilmInspector`, no props, in the right dock, sticky header and ✕ like the export drawer): shot
  (style, duration), stop (label, duration, "Caméra pendant l'arrêt" (camera during the stop) as a list with a help sentence, position and window),
  « Image clé » (distance, tilt, aim from the captured pose, sliders widened to show a value beyond the camera ranges, "À (km)" (at, km),
  `updateCameraKey`: keyframe ranges, aim brought back within ±180°, nothing rounded), speed (chips ×0.25 ×0.5 ×1.5
  ×2 ×3 ×4, "Réglage fin" (fine adjustment) in powers of 2, from / to in km bounded by `updateSpeed`, effect on the duration), text (text, subtitle, position,
  size, start, duration, attached to), photo (thumbnail, full-screen / card display, Ken Burns, caption, position, size, start, duration, attached to),
  video (the same without Ken Burns, plus "Début dans la vidéo" (start in the video), file length, "Son" (sound) block: "Son de la
  vidéo" (video sound) checkbox and volume in %, grayed out according to the flyover speed, and "Calage sur le parcours" (sync to the route) when the filming time
  is known), music (file name, volume in %, fade-in and fade-out 0–10 s, start, duration, "Début dans le
  fichier" (start in the file), bounded by `updateMusic` to the file length, the film's "Baisser la musique sous les vidéos" checkbox,
  "Caler la durée du film sur la musique" (fit the film duration to the music)).
  Position: 3 × 3 grid (`radiogroup` of 9 `role="radio"`, roving focus, arrows that move and select,
  `nextGridIndex`, position label alongside). Changes go through `editFilm(…, { step: false })` (keystrokes
  merged into one step); editing a generated stop first writes all the stops.
- **On the track** (`src/scene/TrackPicker.tsx`, mounted in `FlyoverCanvas`): a click on the first track places the
  playhead at the target point (`setProgress(p, clock.timeAtProgress(p))`); a right-click opens "Ajouter un arrêt ici" (add a stop here) /
  "Ajouter un texte ici" (add a text here) / "Accélérer / ralentir ici" (speed up / slow down here; ×2 section from the point; inside a section, it
  selects it) (`TrackMenu`, a `Menu` of the primitives anchored at the pointer, `role="menu"`, arrows, Esc, click outside; each addition
  through `editFilm`: one undo step, block selected); hand cursor when hovering the track. Click = press and release of the
  same button within 4 px (an OrbitControls drag is not a click; the menu opens on release, not on
  `contextmenu`, which fires on press under Linux and macOS). Picking in screen space (`pickProjectedPath`, pure): 1,500
  points spread along the track, draped like the line (`trackHeightM`: loaded terrain, otherwise recorded elevation, the
  recorded one on a flight, × exaggeration + 3 m; recomputed at most once per second), projected by the camera, closest segment within 12 px,
  interpolated distance. Nothing during an export. Limit: a section hidden by the terrain can also be picked (the line
  shows it through the terrain). Right-clicking on the terrain, on the track or elsewhere adds "Point d'intérêt ici" (point of interest here; the only
  choice off the track): camera ray against the drawn tiles (`Raycaster` on the visible meshes of the engine
  group, only on right-click), target point on the track if no tile is there yet.
- **Texts in the film** (increment 3): drawn by the overlay (see "Film overlay", film time and timeline
  texts), in the preview as in the export.
- **Photos** (increment 4): "Média" (picker, several files, photos and videos) or files dropped on the
  timeline; the files are read one by one (`readMedia`: `readPhoto` or `readVideo`), added to the media table
  (`useMediaStore.add`, ids `photo-<n>` / `video-<n>`) then to the film at the playhead, one after another (`addMedia`: 5 s per
  photo), in one undo step; other files are ignored with a message. `readPhoto` reads the EXIF from the first 128 KB (`parseExif`, pure parser:
  GPS position, original time and its offset, otherwise GPS time in UTC, otherwise local time read in the
  browser's time zone, `photoTimeMs`), decodes the upright image (`createImageBitmap`, `imageOrientation: 'from-image'`),
  scales it down to 2,560 px at most as JPEG 0.85 and makes a 160 px thumbnail (JPEG 0.7). Suggested placement
  (`photoFilmTime`, pure): track point closest to the position (within 2 km; on an out-and-back, the
  recorded pass closest to the time, `nearestOnPath`), otherwise the point recorded at the photo's time (timestamped
  track, 15 min tolerance at the ends, `distanceAtTime`); film time = `clock.timeAtProgress(distance /
  length)`. The message (toast) offers "Placer sur le parcours" (one more undo step).
- **Videos** (`src/film/video.ts`): MP4, WebM or QuickTime (`isMediaFile`: type `video/*`, or extension `.mp4`,
  `.m4v`, `.mov`, `.webm` without a type), of at most `MAX_VIDEO_BYTES` (50 MB), with a clear message beyond that: the file is kept
  as is (no cheap way to shrink it in the page) so the project stays a single file, on the web as on the
  desktop. `readVideo` opens the file with mediabunny, rejects a video without frames or that the browser cannot decode
  (`canDecode`, WebCodecs: the same decoder as the export), reads its display size and its length, makes the thumbnail
  of its first frame (160 px, JPEG 0.7). Added at its full length, 30 s at most (`NEW_VIDEO_MAX_S`), without Ken Burns; past
  the end of the file (stretched block), the last frame stays displayed, without sound. Preview (`getPreviewVideos`, in
  `OverlayCanvas`): one `HTMLVideoElement` per media item, muted except during playback at ×1 (see "Video sound") (object URL of the
  file; a MOV is passed as MP4), played along with playback (playback speed carried over, resynced beyond
  0.3 s of drift), paused and resynced to the film time when seeking (the last resynced frame, copied onto
  a canvas of at most 1,920 px, stays displayed during the next resync); videos not drawn by a frame
  are paused, those that leave the film are released; nothing during an export. Export: see "Video export".
- **Video synced to the route** (phase 7, "vidéo embarquée synchronisée" (synced onboard video)): start time of the filming read when added
  (`readVideo`, stored in the media table: `recordedMs`, `recordedApprox`): date with time zone from Apple devices
  (`com.apple.quicktime.creationdate` in the mediabunny metadata, `quickTimeDateMs`), otherwise creation time from the movie
  header (`moov` › `mvhd`, seconds since 1904 in UTC, `mp4CreationTimeMs`: pure parser that only reads box
  headers, `moov` at the start or at the end; read as seconds since 1970 when an encoder got the epoch wrong; unset
  clock, before 2000, ignored), otherwise, flagged as approximate, the file's last-modified date minus its
  length (a camera writes the file until it stops); WebM: file date only. Clock offset
  `offsetS` (± 1 day, positive: the video was filmed later than the file says). `clipSyncOffsetS`: 0 when the
  video overlaps the recorded outing, otherwise the whole number of hours closest to 0 (up to 14) that makes it
  overlap (camera set to local time that writes it as UTC, the GoPro case), otherwise nothing. Placement
  (`syncClipPlacement`, pure): start = marker passing the point recorded at the start of the video
  (`distanceAtTime` → `clock.timeAtProgress`); a video started before the outing begins at the start of the flight, `inS` skips the
  earlier part; duration: when following the flyover, until passing the point recorded at its end (or the end of the outing), otherwise
  its length in the file from there, at most its current duration; nothing outside the outing or without times. Offered by the
  toast on addition ("Caler sur le parcours" (sync to the route), or "Placer sur le parcours" when there are photos as well: a single undo
  step, clock at that moment) and by the inspector ("Calage sur le parcours" block: filming time, approximate
  flag, "Caler" (sync) / "Recaler" (resync) button, "Décalage de l'horloge (s)" (clock offset) and "Suivre la vitesse du survol" (follow the flyover speed) checkbox, which
  resync immediately, `syncClip`; outside the outing, only `sync` is kept and a message says so). The placement is not
  redone automatically when the film changes (duration, stops): "Recaler". **Follow the flyover speed** (`sync.follow`): time
  in the file = instant recorded under the marker − (filming start + offset) (`clipTimeS(media, t,
  recordedMs)`, bounded to `inS` / `outS`), a pure function of progress: slow-downs, speed sections and opening /
  closing are followed; during a stop the progress does not move, **the frame stays frozen** (by choice: the frame always shows
  the marker's location); a pause recorded on the track (time passing without distance) is skipped. `recordedMs`
  comes from `recordedAtProgress(path, progress)` (`overlay/data.ts`, at the distance of `overlayFrameAt`), the same number for
  drawing (`drawOverlay`, from the frame) and for decoding in the export (`loadFrameMedia(time, progress)`, path of the
  first track kept per track): deterministic export. Preview: `clipRateAt` (file seconds per film second,
  difference over 0.25 s of film) × playback speed → `playbackRate` (rounded to the hundredth, at most ×16); resync
  beyond `FOLLOW_DRIFT_S` (0.2 s); below ×0.0625 (stop), the element is paused on the frame. Without times on the
  track, a following video plays like any other.
- **Music** (`src/film/audio.ts`): "Options" › "Ajouter une musique…" (platform layer picker, several
  files) or sound files dropped on the timeline (`isAudioFile`: known `audio/*` type, otherwise extension `.mp3`, `.m4a`,
  `.aac`, `.ogg`, `.oga`, `.opus`, `.wav`, `.flac`; a drop mixing sounds, photos and videos dispatches them). `readAudio` rejects
  files over `MAX_AUDIO_BYTES` (30 MB, with a message suggesting an MP3 or an M4A), decodes the file once
  (`OfflineAudioContext.decodeAudioData` at 22,050 Hz: checks that the browser can read it, gives its length) and derives the
  waveform from it (`computePeaks`: peak level of 10 slices per second, 4,000 at most, to the hundredth); the file is
  kept as is (`data:audio/mpeg|mp4|aac|ogg|wav|flac|webm`). Added in full (`addMusic`) after the end of the music tracks already
  there (at the start of the film when there is no music), in one undo step, block selected; the toast offers "Caler la durée du
  film" (fit the film duration). Volume (`musicEnvelope`, pure): 0 → volume during the fade-in, volume, → 0 during the fade-out,
  linear, shortened proportionally when they overlap, over the played duration (`musicLengthS`: never past the end
  of the file); same points for the preview (`musicGainAt`) and the export (gain automation). Several music tracks
  can overlap: they are mixed. Preview (`startMusicPreview`, started by `Timeline`, `createMusicPreview` tested
  on fake elements): one `HTMLAudioElement` per music track (object URL of the file, created as soon as it is in the film so
  that it loads), played during playback at the file time, at the playback speed, at the current volume, resynced
  beyond `MUSIC_DRIFT_S` (0.25 s) of drift when it has enough data; paused outside playback, outside its block and
  during an export (playback is stopped then); released when the music leaves the film. "Caler la durée du film sur la
  musique" (`fitFilmToMusic`): flyover duration (within 15 s – 10 min, to the hundredth) so that the film ends at the end of the
  last music track, found by the secant method on the film clock (`durationForFilmEnd`: stops, speed
  sections and shots included); one undo step, toast with the new duration or the bounds reached.
  **Beats** (`src/film/beats.ts`, pure and tested on click tracks at 90 / 120 / 150 BPM with noise, silence and
  noise alone): `detectBeats` on the sound already decoded by `readAudio` (22,050 Hz, channels mixed), once per file,
  kept in its media table entry (`beats`: `bpm`, `confidence`, `times` in file seconds to the millisecond,
  `downbeat`; validated by `isValidBeats`); a file read earlier is analyzed on demand (decoded again, entry
  completed, saved with the project). Onsets: rises (only) in the log intensity of the low band (first-order
  low-pass at 200 Hz) and of the rest, in frames of about 10 ms; tempo from 60 to 180 BPM by autocorrelation of this
  envelope, weighted toward 120 BPM (one octave of standard deviation: separates a tempo from its half or its double),
  interpolated between two frames; confidence = autocorrelation normalized at the tempo, below
  `MIN_TEMPO_CONFIDENCE` (0.15) no beats (noise alone ≈ 0.05, clicks ≥ 0.45); beats by dynamic programming
  (D. Ellis, 2007: strongest chain of onsets, deviation from the period penalized by 100 × log², a chain only
  starts when nothing before is worth linking: no invented beats in a silent intro), free to drift
  slightly with the music; downbeat every 4 beats, starting from the beat whose series has the strongest
  onsets (heuristic: may land on the 2nd or 3rd beat). Beat marks (longer on downbeats)
  are drawn at the top of the block (`beatTicksPath`). "Caler sur le rythme" (snap to the beat; music inspector,
  `snapFilmToMusic`): beats of all music tracks mapped to film time (`filmBeats`: only where each block
  plays its file); each title (start, film time) then each stop (start of its hold, placed in meters:
  position found on the clock by `stopPositionAt`, to the tenth of a meter) goes to the closest downbeat within
  `BEAT_SNAP_S` (0.4 s), otherwise to the closest beat (`beatNear`); already within `ON_BEAT_S` (20 ms):
  left alone. No overlap is ever created: a title that would overlap another title stays, a stop that would pass or
  land on another stop stays, a stop whose hold cannot land on the beat (inside another stop, at the
  end of the track) stays. Then each speed section (`snapSpeeds`, with `clockOfSpeeds`): its start (film
  time where it begins, clock recomputed with the moved section) goes to the beat, its length kept, to the meter,
  between its neighbors; it stays if its start lands more than `BEAT_SNAP_S` / 4 from the target beat. One undo
  step (`editFilm`); the generated stops are written (like any stop
  edit). Resyncing moves nothing. Toast "N éléments calés
  sur le rythme (≈ 112 BPM)"; uncertain tempo: message, nothing is done.
- **Video sound** (`clipHasSound`, `clipSounds`, `duckEnvelope` in `src/film/audio.ts`, pure and tested): an added video
  has its sound (`VIDEO_SOUND_DEFAULTS`: `muted: false`, `volume: 1`); a video saved without `muted` (earlier
  projects) receives `muted: true` from `withFilmDefaults`: its film does not change. Heard when it is not muted, at a
  volume > 0 and **does not follow the flyover speed** (its time in the file then changes pace: resampled sound
  would be ugly, so it stays silent in the preview as in the export). It plays its file from `inS` as long as
  it shows it: past `outS` or the end of the file (last frame held), silence. 20 ms fades at its
  edges (`CLIP_EDGE_FADE_S`, through `musicEnvelope`) against clicks. "Baisser la musique sous les vidéos"
  (`film.duckMusic`): all music tracks drop by 10 dB (`DUCK_DB`, gain ≈ 0.316) during each video with sound,
  ramping down over the 0.3 s before (`DUCK_RAMP_S`, so its first words are not covered), back up over the 0.3 s after;
  videos less than two ramps apart are merged (no bounce); a video at the very start finds the music
  already low. Preview: the video element is unmuted to its volume only during playback at ×1 (muted while seeking, at
  any other speed, when following the flyover, or when the timeline speaker mutes the preview sound, which also mutes
  the videos and shows as soon as a video has sound); the preview music follows the same ducking (`duckGainAt`),
  at all speeds, as in the exported film. Export: see "Video export", "Sound". A video without a sound track
  stays silent, but the music still ducks under it (rule derived from the model alone, identical in the preview and
  the export): mute its sound to avoid that.
- **Media table** (`src/film/media.ts`): the bytes are not in the settings (lightweight history and
  presets) but in a document table `{ id: { data, thumb, width, height, name?, durationS? } }` (JPEG data URL;
  video: data URL of the `video/mp4 | webm | quicktime` file and its length `durationS`, with the filming start time `recordedMs` / `recordedApprox` when known; sound: `audio/…` data URL,
  `durationS` and waveform `peaks`, without thumbnail or size, ids `audio-<n>`). An image
  stays in the table when its photo leaves the film (undo finds it again); saving writes only the images
  used, by media items and music tracks (`usedMedia`). File access behind `readMedia(blob)` (a `File` from the picker or the drop on the web,
  a `Blob` read from disk for Tauri, later a file path instead of the bytes for large videos). Images decoded on demand (`getMediaBitmaps`: `ImageBitmap` per id, at most 6
  kept, least recent released, also released when the film no longer uses them, decoded again if the table changes
  when a project is opened; an unreadable image is not retried).
- Limits: videos heard only at ×1 in the preview (no edge fade in the preview), 50 MB at most, no GPS position
  read (placed at the playhead, or synced to the track time); the time of a video added before this sync feature is
  not read again; GoPro GPS stream (GPMF) not read; rejected where WebCodecs is missing (desktop application on Linux); EXIF is only read in JPEGs (not HEIC, which
  most browsers do not decode, nor PNG / WebP); a full-screen photo is cropped to cover the frame (a
  portrait photo loses its top and bottom).
  Other limits: the 3D preview only
  follows a gesture on release; in `'temps-forts'` mode, the pacing's
  "pause" slider does not set the duration of the generated stops (4 s, to adjust per stop); only the film's own stops
  (not the generated ones while "Arrêts automatiques" is checked) can carry items; landmarks loaded for the first time
  after an edit made without them (landmarks off since the import) do not move attached items.

## Project (phase 4)

- The project file is `<name>.opnflvr` (MIME `application/x-opnflvr`, `src/project/file.ts`): the ASCII signature `OPNFLVR`, a
  version byte (1 = gzip of the JSON text, 2 = uncompressed JSON where `CompressionStream` is missing), then the exact text
  produced by `serializeProject`. The reader detects the signature; otherwise it parses plain JSON (older `.openflyover.json`
  projects and the « Mes projets » documents); anything else gives an error in the interface language.
- **Save in place**: `Platform.saveDocument(produce, { target, ask })` returns an opaque `FileTarget`: on the website the
  `FileSystemFileHandle` (Chromium, `showSaveFilePicker` / `showOpenFilePicker` with `rewritable`; write permission is requested
  when needed, a refused permission or a vanished file falls back to the picker, other errors throw); on the desktop the path (in the
  fs scope: dialog plugin, or added in Rust for files opened by the OS); Firefox, Safari and iOS download or share, with no
  target (nothing is overwritten). The target lives in `useAppStore.projectFile` (session only, not in the file); it is set by a
  save, « Enregistrer sous… » or « Ouvrir » and forgotten by `closeProject` / `newProject`. « Enregistrer » (the save icon of the top bar,
  Ctrl+S) = `saveProject`, « Enregistrer sous… » (Projet tab, Ctrl+Maj+S) = `saveProjectAs`; a failed write keeps the project
  « Modifié »; the autosave to « Mes projets » is unchanged (plain JSON).
- **Desktop opening** (`src-tauri/src/opened.rs`, `src/platform/openedFiles.ts`): a `.opnflvr` or `.json` argv path (Windows and
  Linux), or a macOS `RunEvent::Opened`, is added to the fs scope and queued; the command `opened_files` hands the queue over once,
  the event `openflyover://opened-files` (macOS, app running) makes the frontend take it again; `reopenLastProject` takes the
  OS files first. The `--rendu` arguments of `cli.rs` are ignored by the parser. Windows and Linux have no single-instance plugin: a
  double-click while the app runs opens a second instance. `bundle.fileAssociations` declares the type (UTI
  `io.github.yannickriou.openflyover.project`). The document icon: NSIS uses the exe's (`exe,0`); MSI, macOS (no
  `CFBundleTypeIconFile`) and Linux cannot be set by configuration; the deb installs `mime/openflyover-opnflvr.xml`.
- (Before the `.opnflvr` format) a project was a single JSON file `<name>.openflyover.json` (`format: "openflyover-project"`, `version`, `name`, `settings`,
  `playback.speed`, `tracks`, and `media` when the film has photos: table of images by id, one line per image after
  the tracks, see "Film and timeline", "Media table"; optional field, no version change). On
  open, invalid images are discarded and film photos without an image are removed with a warning (music
  tracks without a file too);
  `applyProject` replaces the table before the settings. Tracks are **embedded** (self-contained file) as columns per segment (`lon`, `lat`, then
  `ele` / `time` / `hr` / `cad` / `power` / `temp` only if present, `null` for a point without a value), rounded to 1e-7° and
  0.01 (whole ms for time); stats and footprint recomputed on load by `buildTrack`, ids and colors kept.
- Settings handled **generically on the keys of `DEFAULT_SETTINGS`**: each value is checked against the type of its default
  value (nested objects included), otherwise it falls back to the default for that key; unknown keys are ignored. **A new setting
  needs no code here**; add an entry to `SETTING_CHECKS` only if a value of the right type can be invalid
  (enumeration, catalog id, range). Any format change increments `PROJECT_VERSION` and adds `MIGRATIONS[n]`
  (version 2: film of v1 projects, see "Film and timeline"; version 3: the default junction « À la suite » written
  out, `withExplicitNightJunctions`, see "Stages"; version 4: stage cards trimmed to what they change,
  `withStageCardsOverCommon`; version 5: the cards of an overlay that was off switched off and the automatic stops kept
  as the pacing made them, `withTitlesAndStopsOnTheirOwn`; version 6: the night and the high view of a junction on their
  own, `withNightAndRecapOnTheirOwn`).
- Settings **history** outside the store: subscribed to `useAppStore`, each step keeps only the changed keys; changes to the
  same keys less than 400 ms apart are merged (keyboard), a pointer slider drag = one step from press to release however
  slow it is (`beginGesture`, wired to every `input[type=range]` by `installSliderGestures`), a preset = one step, a timeline gesture = one step; the regional imagery picked on import
  is not recorded; nothing is recorded during "Un film par trace" (one film per track; `suspend`); opening a project clears the history. Shortcuts Ctrl/Cmd+Z, Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+Z, Ctrl+Y (ignored in
  text fields), installed once by `App`. The project name, save and open are in the top bar
  (with Ctrl/Cmd+S, Ctrl/Cmd+O); the "Projet" panel is the complete place for project actions: a « Projet » section
  (« Nouveau projet », « Ouvrir… », « Enregistrer », « Enregistrer sous… », « Fermer le projet »), then "Mes projets" and the presets.
- **Closing the project / a new one** (`closeProject`, `newProject` in `ui/projectActions.ts`, « Projet » section of
  `ProjectPanel.tsx`): through `confirmClose` like the desktop window: the change waiting for the autosave is written
  first (`flushAutosave`), then a question only when changes would still be lost (`projectCloseLoss` in `ui/library.ts`:
  a project with a track still « Modifié »; a project emptied of its tracks loses nothing). The question is shown in
  place in the section on both targets (« Fermer sans enregistrer ? », `unsavedChangesText`: « Enregistrer » / « Fermer
  sans enregistrer » / « Annuler ») and is cancelled when another project is opened or imported, or an export starts.
  Then `applyEmptyProject` (`project/apply.ts`: no track, no media, `DEFAULT_SETTINGS` set as a whole, default playback
  speed, no block selected, imagery choice forgotten so the next import picks the regional imagery), history cleared,
  name emptied, marked saved, no open entry (`setOpenEntry(null)`, which forgets the remembered last project): the home
  screen appears by itself, the closed project among its « Récents ». « Nouveau projet » then opens the GPX / FIT
  picker. Disabled during an export; « Fermer le projet » also when nothing is open. Removing the last track still keeps
  the project open and empty.
- **"modifié" chip + "Par défaut" button** (`ui/ModifiedMarker.tsx`) at the top right of each settings panel: list
  of the panel's `Settings` keys, deep-compared with the values of `DEFAULT_SETTINGS` (`modifiedSettings` /
  `sameValue`, `project/apply.ts`); "Par défaut" resets these keys to default in a single history step (`resetSettings`,
  `project/history.ts`). The imagery source is not tracked (picked by region on import). A new setting in a
  panel: add its key to the marker's `keys` list.
- **Mes projets** (`platform/projectLibrary.ts`, `ui/library.ts`, "Projet" tab): `getPlatform().projectLibrary`
  (null on the website without Cache Storage). Two text files per project, named by a checked id (`[a-z0-9-]`, never a
  path coming from elsewhere): the document `<id>.openflyover.json` (plain JSON, media included) then the entry
  `<id>.entry.json` (`ProjectEntry { id, name, updatedAt, summary, sizeBytes, thumbnail? }`, the only one read for the list; an unreadable
  entry is discarded). `ProjectLibrary.load` returns the document as a `Blob` (`LibraryFiles.read`: `Response.blob()` over the cache entry on the
  web, one `Blob` over the bytes on the desktop); only the small entry files are read as text. `lastOpenProject` / `libraryFile` wrap it in a
  `File` and `openProject` reads the text once and parses it, so the raw string never lives beside the document's other copies. Desktop: `<app data>/projects/` (plugin-fs, `readFile` / `writeFile` in UTF-8, same permissions as
  the packs). Website: cache `openflyover-projects-v1` (keys `/openflyover-projects/<file>`, `persist()` requested
  once). **Thumbnail**: each write takes the 3D view (`scene/thumbnail.ts`, loaded with the scene: the canvas has no
  `preserveDrawingBuffer`, so `invalidate` asks for one frame and R3F's `addAfterEffect` copies it in the same
  animation-frame task onto a 2D canvas over the CSS sky, 240 px on its longest side, JPEG 0.7, ~10 KB) into the entry as a
  data URL (`isProjectThumbnail`: an image data URL of 80,000 characters at most, otherwise dropped on save and on
  read). No picture (hidden page, no frame within 2 s, tainted canvas): the entry keeps its last one; entries from
  before have none and show an empty frame in the list. `useLibraryStore { entries,
  currentId }`: **a project is always kept** (`autosaveWrites`): the first autosave of a project with a track and no
  entry creates it (imported tracks, the sample on opening, a project opened from a file); no entry while it has no
  track; "Garder dans Mes projets" (keep in my projects) is left for a project whose entry was deleted while open (it
  then gets no new one by itself until another project is opened) and goes through the same queue (never two entries).
  The id of the open entry is remembered in `getPlatform().storage` (`openflyover.lastProject.v1`,
  `rememberOpenEntry`); at start-up `reopenLastProject` (once, from `App`, after `runCliRenderIfAsked` said no batch
  render was asked on the desktop) opens it with « Projet … rouvert », unless a track or project came first (Android
  share target, a quick click); an entry gone is forgotten and the app starts on the home screen. iOS discards a
  background tab and reloads it on return: this is what brings the work back. Then **automatic save**
  (`installLibraryAutosave`, installed by `App`) 3 s after the last change to the settings, the tracks or the name
  (`createAutosave`: restarted on each change, retried later during an export or a batch render, never two
  writes at once, a single toast per series of failures); each write marks the project as saved ("Enregistré"
  in the bar). Opening an entry goes through `openProject(file, entry)` (same validations and warnings, the entry's
  name wins); `openProject` first writes a pending change (`flushAutosave`), and a project opened from
  a file has no entry. **Closing** (`installCloseGuard`, installed by `App`): `closeLoss` says what would be lost (the
  export in progress, otherwise a "Modifié" (modified) project). Desktop: the window waits for `confirmClose`
  (`guardClose` of the platform, `onCloseRequested` then `destroy`): pending write finished (4 s at most), then a
  native question if something would be lost ("Enregistrer" / "Fermer sans enregistrer" / "Annuler" (save / close
  without saving / cancel), or "Fermer quand même" (close anyway) during an export; the window stays open if the save
  did not happen). On exit, `lib.rs` stops the ffmpeg encodings in progress and deletes their partial files
  (`cancel_all`). Site: `pagehide` and a hidden page start the write without being able to wait for it (a Cache
  Storage write started there usually finishes, with no guarantee); `beforeunload` shows the browser's question (its
  text, not ours) when something would be lost, a write still in progress included. Renaming changes the entry (and
  the name of the open project; its document follows at the next save); deleting asks for an inline confirmation, and the open project stays open without an entry.
- **Presets** in `getPlatform().storage` (`openflyover.presets.v1`, in-memory fallback); a key missing from a preset keeps its
  current value. A preset keeps every setting or one family (`Preset.scope`, `PRESET_SCOPES`: "Carte et ambiance (fond, relief, nuages, couleurs, objectif)",
  "Trace, marqueur et étiquettes", "Habillage", "Prise de vue (caméra, soleil, exposition)"): `save(name, settings, scope)`
  stores only the keys of `PRESET_SCOPE_KEYS[scope]`, so applying it changes nothing else; a stored preset with an
  unknown family is dropped on reading. `save` returns false when the storage refuses (full or blocked): the preset remains usable for the
  session and the panel shows "Préréglage non enregistré : stockage du navigateur plein"; the weather cache, on the other hand, stays
  silent (it only loses its persistence).

## Historical weather (phase 7)

- **Source**: Open-Meteo archive (`archive-api.open-meteo.com/v1/archive`, no key, CC BY 4.0, free for non-commercial
  use; checks in `docs/sources.md`). Attribution "Données météo : Open-Meteo.com (CC BY 4.0)" in the panel
  and the status bar as soon as data is displayed.
- **Request** (`src/weather/openMeteo.ts`): 2 to 12 locations along the first track (one every ~10 km, start and finish,
  rounded to 0.01°, deduplicated by 0.05° cell), at the **recorded elevation** (the `elevation` parameter brings the temperature
  to the track's elevation: ~10 °C of difference in the mountains), over the UTC days of the outing (±1 h) in a single request
  (`timezone=GMT`, `timeformat=unixtime`). Refused without a request: track without timestamps, start more than 16 days ahead or before 1940,
  outing longer than 31 days. An outing that ends after today goes through the **forecast** (see "Planned outing").
  `visibility` is always null in the archive and is not requested.
- **Cache**: per rounded location and UTC day, in memory then `getPlatform().storage` (`openflyover.weather.v1`, ≤ 300 location-days, LRU,
  every access inside try/catch); days less than 7 days old are not persisted because the archive revises them.
- **Series** (`src/weather/series.ts`, pure): 13 hourly variables per location (NaN if missing). `weatherAt` interpolates
  linearly in time and by inverse squared distance between locations (WMO code of the closest location, wind direction
  as a vector); hourly totals apply to the hour that ends. `weatherWidgetData(series, path, progress)` gives the
  conditions under the marker (recorded time via `recordedTimeAt`), `summarizeOuting` the summary weighted by real time.
  WMO codes → label (`t('weather.…')`) + icon id.
- **State** (`src/weather/store.ts`): `status` idle / loading / ready / unavailable / error, `message`, `series`, `trackId`;
  `syncWeather` is called by the panel on each change of the first track or of `settings.weather.enabled` (true by
  default), cancels the previous request and explains the lack of data. « À la suite », `syncStageWeather` fetches the
  later stages into `stages` (by track id, see "Several tracks").
- **Coming next**: rain and snow particles (driven scene: "Weather in the scene" and "Volumetric clouds").

## Planned outing (route scouting)

Before an outing: load a **planned** route (GPX from Komoot, Visorando, IGNrando…, usually without times), fly over
it, know at what time you will pass each point, where the sun will be and what the weather will be like.

- **Estimated times** (`src/plan/timing.ts`, pure, tested): `estimateElapsedS(segments, activity, pace)` gives the
  seconds since the start at each point, without breaks, step by step on the smoothed elevation as for the D+
  (`smoothElevations`; a segment without complete elevation = flat; the jump between two segments takes no time).
  Models (`stretchHours`): hiking **DIN 33466** (4 km/h on the flat, 300 m/h uphill, 500 m/h downhill, the longer of
  the two times + half of the other); trail running **ITRA km-effort** (1 km + 1 km per 100 m of D+) at 8 km-effort/h;
  cycling, additive in the manner of Naismith (20 km/h + 1 h per 600 m of D+), descents counted as flat for the last
  two. Pace: a factor on all durations (0.7 to 1.5).
- **"Prévoir la sortie"** (plan the outing; track card, `OutingPlanForm` in `TrackList`, for a track without times or
  with estimated times): date, start time (device clock: every time shown follows the browser's time zone), activity
  (derived from `activityType` by `planActivityOf`, hiking otherwise), pace. `withEstimatedTimes` writes the `time` of
  the points, recomputes the stats and sets `Track.timesEstimated: true` and `utcOffsetMin` (device offset on that
  date); the track keeps its id and replaces the old one (`replaceTracks`, playback at the start). Everything that
  reads times works as is: sun, counters, timeline clock, weather, ghost race. "horaires estimés" (estimated times)
  chip; "Effacer les horaires" (clear the times, `withoutTimes`) returns the track without times. The overlay's
  "Temps" (time) counter shows "≈" before an estimated time (`OverlayTrackStats.timesEstimated`). A timestamped track
  is not re-estimated (its real times would be lost).
- **Project**: `timesEstimated: true` saved with the track, read back only on a timestamped track; old projects
  unchanged. `elevationEstimated` is saved the same way ("Elevation from the terrain").
- **Weather forecast** (`src/weather/openMeteo.ts`): `outingDays` chooses the source, the archive if the outing ends
  today (UTC) at the latest, otherwise `api.open-meteo.com/v1/forecast` (same parameters and hourly variables, same
  places, same response), up to the 16th day (today included, end trimmed to that day); beyond: "Sortie dans plus de
  16 jours : pas encore de prévision météo." (outing more than 16 days ahead: no forecast yet). Forecast days in memory
  only, under a key that changes every 3 h (`forecastCacheKey`), never in storage. `WeatherSeries.forecast` is true:
  the panel writes "Prévision" (forecast) and reminds that it should be checked again the day before. `syncWeather`
  reloads when the start time of the same track changes.
- **Limit**: the start time is read in the device's time zone, not one derived from the coordinates.

### Landmarks and points of interest (« Repères et points d'intérêt »)

- **Rows** (`LandmarkPanel`, Carte tab): the panel lists the points of interest (`film.pois`, by position, `poiStopAtM`);
  the OSM landmarks of the film track (shown and hidden, climbs are not listed or labelled) are chosen in a window, never in
  a narrow scrolling list (long names stay whole).
- **Window of the map** (« Choisir sur la carte et le profil », `LandmarkMap` in `src/ui/LandmarkMap.tsx`, a `Modal`):
  the altitude profile of the film track (`elevationProfile`; a click seeks the flyover, `setProgress`) and a map of the
  track with a marker per landmark listed (`mapItems`, along the track). A marker is a button on both (profile: at the
  elevation of the track under it; map: at its lon/lat); its look is its mode (filled accent « Toujours », filled « Auto »,
  dashed ring « Jamais »); a click on one in either selects it (`selectedId`, local state of the panel), and `SelectedLandmark`
  shows it in full: kind, name, km, distance from the track, the eye (Auto ⇄ Jamais), the mode button, the ▲ ▼ ranking,
  « Arrêt » and its duration, « Aller à ce repère », and the arrows to the previous / next landmark along the track. What a
  click does is the window's tool (`MapTool`, chips « Un clic sur un repère », session state of the panel): 'choose' (the
  above; M hides the selected one and selects the next, `onHideSelected`), 'hide' (Auto ⇄ Jamais at once, `onToggle`) or
  'zone' (a rectangle drawn on the map, the map not panning meanwhile, or a stretch dragged on the profile, gives `zoneIds`;
  `ZoneActions` « Masquer » / « Afficher » sets them all to Jamais or Auto, one `setRowsMode` undo step). The map
  is drawn on two canvases: the tiles of OpenTopoMap (`opentopomap`, a topographic map of OpenStreetMap data, already in the
  catalogue and in the Tauri CSP; its attribution is shown under the map) fetched through `createTileFetcher` (so the offline
  packs, the retries and the HTTP cache apply; at most 30 tiles a view, a fetcher of its own kept between openings), then the
  track and a band as wide as « Distance à la trace » (`settings.landmarks.maxDistanceM`) to judge what was found; a failed
  tile layer leaves the track and the markers. It reuses `planFlatMap` of the poster (`src/poster/view.ts`; `margin` 0 and
  `unproject` for the views once moved): drag pans, the wheel and the buttons zoom, « Tout voir » refits the track and the
  landmarks.
- **Ranking**: ▲ ▼ move a landmark one place (`movedInRank`); `settings.landmarks.rank` holds the OSM ids in that order
  (empty until the first move, then the others keep their own importance). `withRank` hands the importances of the
  ranked ones back in that order (strictly decreasing), so the label overlap (`resolveOverlaps`) follows it;
  applied in `publish` (`osm/store.ts`) after `buildLandmarks`, before `splitHidden`.
- **« Garder les « Toujours » » and « Relancer la recherche »**: « Garder les « Toujours » » puts the id of every landmark that is not pinned in `settings.landmarks.dismissedIds`
  (`buildLandmarks` leaves it out after the cap and the deduplication, so it frees no slot and no other landmark pops up in its place; a row has no cross of its own); « Relancer la recherche » empties `dismissedIds` and calls
  `syncLandmarks(…, { refresh: true })`, which asks Overpass again for every track (the known landmarks stay until the answer, and if it fails; « Réessayer » does the same; `fetchTrackFeatures(…, refresh)`
  skips the memory and the persistent cache, then caches the answer). `withLandmarkDefaults` gives `[]` to both lists.
- **Density**: `settings.landmarks.density` (`few` / `normal` / `many`, « Peu / Normal / Beaucoup », default `normal`)
  replaces the former raw `maxCount`: `LANDMARK_DENSITY_COUNTS` (15 / `MAX_LANDMARKS` = 40 / 80) is the cap of each track in
  `buildLandmarks` (which still takes a `maxCount`, computed by `publish`) and, globally over the tracks, of the 3D labels in
  `landmarkLabels`. The landmarks kept are the most important ones (`landmarkPriority`: kind, elevation, closeness to the
  track; the ▲ ▼ ranking below). An old project's `maxCount` becomes the nearest density (< 28 `few`, < 60 `normal`, else `many`)
  and is dropped (`withLandmarkDefaults`); a `density` that is not one of the three invalidates the setting.
- **One button, three modes** (`LandmarkMode`, `ModeButton`): the card of the selected landmark has one button that cycles « Auto » (the density
  decides), « Toujours » and « Jamais » (`nextMode`, `withMode`); the model is unchanged, `landmarkMode` reads it:
  `hiddenIds` = Jamais, `pinnedIds` = Toujours, neither = Auto (a landmark has one mode: `withMode` removes it from the other
  list; `withLandmarkDefaults` drops from `pinnedIds` an id that an old project also hid, hidden wins). **Toujours**: the
  landmark is exempt from the cap and from the name deduplication (it wins over an unpinned duplicate), counts in the cap, the others
  share what is left; a pinned id absent from the Overpass answer is ignored; its 3D label gets `PINNED_LABEL_BOOST` (+100) so
  it wins every overlap over the other landmarks, the waypoints and the kilometres (ranked among the pinned ones by the arrows).
  The summary line reads « 14 affichés · 3 toujours · 2 jamais » (Toujours counted among the shown ones); « Tout remettre en auto »
  empties `hiddenIds` and `pinnedIds` (one undo step); « Tout masquer » sets every shown label to Jamais.
  « Garder les « Toujours » » (`withPinnedOnly`, one undo step with the stops of the shown ones) dismisses every landmark of
  every track that is not pinned; since a dismissed landmark frees no slot, nothing new appears (the cross of a row is gone,
  « Relancer la recherche » brings the dismissed ones back).
- **Jamais**: `settings.landmarks.hiddenIds` holds OSM ids and point-of-interest ids (a point of interest keeps its eye: no
  Toujours for a point you placed). Hiding removes, in the same undo step,
  the row's own stop (`placeStop`: the stop whose `source.ref` is the landmark, else a stop placed by hand within 1 m)
  (`withoutPlaceItems`). Generated stops follow `splitHidden`; a hidden point of interest gets no label. Deleting a point of interest drops its id
  (ids are reused).
- **Overlaps on screen** (`updateLabelSet`, `resolveOverlaps`): the label with the higher priority is drawn, the other is not; a
  label too transparent to read (`FAINT_OPACITY` 0.35: far away or half hidden by the relief) is visited after all the others, so a ghost
  never takes the place of a clear label.
- **« Arrêt »**: `addStop` (landmark: label = name, source `{ kind: 'landmark', ref }`; point of interest: name, nearest
  point of the track); duration with `updateStop`; through `editFilm({ stops: true })`, so the first edit writes the
  generated stops out.
- **« Tout remettre en auto » / « Tout masquer »**: every label of every track and every point of interest, one undo step
  (`withMode`). Landmarks found later stay shown. `settings.landmarks.seenIds` (OSM ids listed at the last show / hide;
  `withLandmarkDefaults` gives `[]`, keeps strings only) marks the others « nouveau » (`newLandmarkIds`, none before a
  first choice); the « N nouveaux » badge marks them seen.
- **Water points**: landmark type `waterPoint` (Overpass `amenity=drinking_water`, named "Eau potable" (drinking
  water) when it has no name, and named `natural=spring`), off by default for the film and the labels; an old project
  receives the type through `withLandmarkDefaults`.


## Figures corrected by hand (« Corriger les chiffres »)

`Track.statsOverride` (`src/core/types.ts`): distance, D+, highest point, duration and top speed typed in the track's
card (`TrackCorrections` in `src/ui/TrackList.tsx`, store action `setTrackStatsOverride`), each absent when not
corrected. `Track.stats` is left as computed from the points: the path, camera, profile and counters do not move.

- **Where it shows**: the opening and closing cards (`prepareOverlayTrack` in `src/overlay/data.ts`), the poster and
  the track's summary line (`correctedStats` in `src/import/stats.ts`). « À la suite », `sequenceStats` moves each
  total of the whole film (distance, D+, time) by the stage's correction and takes the highest point and top speed over
  the stages as corrected.
- **Project**: saved with the track, read back through `statsOverrideOf` (finite numbers, ≥ 0 but the highest point;
  an empty correction is dropped). Old projects have none.

## Elevation from the terrain (« Calculer l'altitude »)

`src/terrain/elevation.ts`, pure apart from the loader. A track whose points lack elevation (routes drawn on a map, some
exports) gets it from the terrain source of the settings.

- **Tiles**: read at z12 for 512 px sources and z13 for 256 px ones (about 14 m per pixel at 45° N), only under the
  points without elevation and the recorded point on each side of a gap. They go through a `createTileFetcher`
  (4 requests, offline packs first, then the network and its HTTP cache), are decoded with `decodeDem` and sampled
  bilinearly (`sampleGrid`); each grid is dropped once its points are read. A 4xx answer means no data there; any other
  failure rejects, nothing is half-computed.
- **`withTerrainElevation(track, sampler)`** fills the missing points, shifting each gap onto the recorded points around
  it (offset blended by distance, so no step and no false D+), recomputes the stats and sets `Track.elevationEstimated`
  (`'all'`: the file had none; `'gaps'`: gaps filled).
- **At import** (`importFiles`, `ImportSink.completeElevation`, default `completeImportedElevations` with the settings'
  terrain source): automatic, before the tracks are added, for a track needing at most `AUTO_ELEVATION_MAX_TILES` = 64
  tiles, the whole batch within 15 s. Measured with Mapterhorn z12 on straight lines at 45.9° N: 20 km → 4 tiles,
  100 km → 16, 200 km → 30, 540 km → 80; about 150 KB per tile, so 64 tiles ≈ 10 MB, about what the 3D view loads when
  it opens. A longer, failing or too slow track comes without elevation, and its card offers « Calculer l'altitude ».
- **Track card**: chip « altitude calculée » / « altitude complétée », « Effacer l'altitude » only for `'all'` (for
  `'gaps'` the computed points cannot be told apart after a reload).
- **Project**: `elevationEstimated` saved with the track, read back only when its value is valid and the track has
  elevation; old projects unchanged. The Habillage altitude and D+ counters turn on by themselves (`stats.maxEle`).
- **Limits**: bridges and tunnels follow the ground; times estimated before the calculation are not re-estimated;
  « Un film par trace » and chaining do not compute it or keep the flag.
- **Flights** (`src/flyover/flights.ts`, `withFlights`): at import, within the same 64 tiles and 15 s, the same ground
  is read under every point of a track with recorded elevation, and `detectFlights` keeps `Track.flights` (from / to
  metres along the track, device offset), saved with the project (old projects: none, the track stays on the ground).
  Clearance = recorded − ground, every 20 m, moving average over 100 m, minus the device offset (median, clamped to
  ±50 m, taken again outside the flights of a first pass: a paraglider is in the air most of the time); a flight is a
  run above 15 m that reaches 50 m and lasts 300 m. Decided once on a fixed zoom, not on the drawn terrain: it does not
  change while the tiles load, and the preview and the export agree. Accepted: a cable car, a chairlift high over a
  gully or a high bridge counts as a flight (drawn where it was recorded); kite jumps (a few metres, below the error of
  a recorded height) and a paraglider skimming a ridge under 50 m stay on the ground; a track over 64 tiles (raids)
  is not looked at.
## Climbs and labels

- **Climbs** (`src/flyover/climbs.ts`) on the **recorded** elevations of the first track: resampling every 20 m,
  100 m moving average, high and low points confirmed by a 10 m hysteresis, merging of two climbs separated by a
  dip ≤ 40 m (and ≤ 25% of the gain, over ≤ 1.5 km) when the second ends higher, flat ends trimmed (< 2% over 200 m),
  minimum thresholds 500 m, 50 m of elevation gain, 3% average; maximum gradient over 100 m. Category based on the score length (m) × average
  gradient (%): cat. 4 ≥ 8,000, 3 ≥ 16,000, 2 ≥ 32,000, 1 ≥ 64,000, HC ≥ 80,000 (uncategorized below).
- **Waypoints**: `Track.waypoints?` (optional addition to the contract) holds the GPX `<wpt>` elements, attached to the first track of the
  file; the project document saves them.
- **UTC offset**: `Track.utcOffsetMin?` (optional addition to the contract, minutes by which the local clock is ahead of UTC) comes
  from the `localTimestamp` of a FIT `activity` message (to the quarter hour, `readUtcOffset`) or from the first `<time>` of a GPX
  written with an offset (`gpxUtcOffset`; "Z" and "+00:00" say nothing); the project document saves it.
- **`Labels`** (in `TerrainLayer`): WebGL sprites with a canvas texture (constant screen size, ink panel, white text,
  accent stroke per type) for the summit of each climb, the waypoints, the kilometer markers (`kmLabels`, one every
  `labels.kmStep` km of the first track, counter distances, priority 20: below everything else), the points
  of interest (`poiLabels`, light orange pictogram instead of the stroke, `FilmPoi.icon` among 9 Lucide paths in
  `Labels.tsx`, priority 200: above everything) and any external source registered through
  `setLabelSource(id, LandmarkLabel[])` (e.g. `'osm'`). Height = terrain (or recorded elevation) × exaggeration, re-draped on
  `engine.onChange` (same debounce as the track). Hidden when the line of sight goes below the terrain (24 samples, the 2 lowest dips within 60 m refined by a golden-section search between their neighbours, so the cut does not flicker as samples slide over a ridge) and
  between half the range and the range (`labels.rangeKm`, 70 km by default: `distanceFade`); shared size
  `labels.size` (×0.6 to ×1.6); on overlap, the highest priority wins. Opacity is a function of the view alone (no
  temporal smoothing): each export frame is rendered in isolation. The opening and closing cards do not hide them (the cards are drawn over the 3D view, labels
  included). Start and finish (`endpointLabels`, priority 150): a flag pin at both ends of every track (one when
  both ends are less than 100 m apart: a loop), always shown; with `labels.endpoints` ("Départ et arrivée") the pins of the
  first track read "Départ", "Arrivée" or "Départ et arrivée", otherwise the pin alone (a square panel). They and the photos of the film whose file gave a GPS position (`photoLabels`,
  `labels.photos`, priority 190, `MediaAsset.lon` / `lat` read from EXIF when the photo is added) use the pictogram
  panel of the points of interest. Setting `settings.labels { waypoints, kmStep, endpoints, photos, size, rangeKm }` (old projects completed by
  `withLabelDefaults`, values checked by `isValidLabelSettings`), section
  « Habillage » › « Étiquettes 3D » (`LabelSettings`, with the colours and the keys).

## OpenStreetMap landmarks (phase 7)

- **Source**: OpenStreetMap through the public Overpass API (`overpass-api.de`, VK Maps fallback), no key, ODbL data (attribution
  "© contributeurs OpenStreetMap (ODbL)" in the panel and the status bar). Checks and terms of use:
  `docs/sources.md`. The request must stay "simple" (POST `application/x-www-form-urlencoded`): the CORS preflight
  returns 406.
- **One request per track** (`src/osm/overpass.ts`): track bounding box widened by 3 km (several consecutive boxes
  beyond a 40 km footprint), 10 tagged and named statements (summits / saddles, passes, huts, lakes, waterfalls, places (cities,
  towns, villages, hamlets; nodes, ways and relations), viewpoints, glaciers, springs; drinking water even without a name),
  `out tags bb` (ways and relations reduced to their bounding box, positioned at its middle; the box is kept for places as
  `OsmFeature.area`, with the raw `population` tag). The `around:` form on a
  polyline exceeds the public server's timeout; the distance to the track is computed locally, so changing the types or the
  distance never triggers a new request. Queue (one request at a time), memory cache (the 32 most recently used, `MEMORY_CACHE_ENTRIES`) +
  `getPlatform().storage` for 30 days per request fingerprint, one retry on 429 / 504 (`Retry-After`, otherwise 15 s) then the other instance; a 200 response carrying an
  error `remark` (timeout exceeded) is an error.
- **Pure post-processing** (`src/osm/landmarks.ts`): projection onto the path → distance and offset, filter by type and distance
  (`settings.landmarks`, default summits + passes + huts + lakes + places (cities, villages, hamlets) within 1.5 km;
  `settings.landmarks.places { city, village, hamlet }` filters the places, « Villes » = city + town), elevation from `ele`
  (free formats), priority in [0, 50) below waypoints and climbs (pass crossed > high and nearby summit > neighboring pass > hut >
  lake > waterfall / viewpoint / glacier > place). A place mapped as an area is measured to its bounding box (0 when crossed);
  places rank by `population` when tagged (3·log10 − 6, in [0, 10]), else city 9 > town 6 > village 3 > hamlet 0.
  Deduplication by name within 1 km or inside the other's box, cap of the density's count (40 by default, see "Density") with `PLACE_SHARE` (25 %) of them kept for
  places when there are enough (also for the 3D labels), label "Nom · 1 653 m" for summits and passes.
- **State** (`src/osm/store.ts`): `useLandmarkStore` driven by `syncLandmarks` from `LandmarkPanel`; the landmarks of all
  tracks are published to the 3D labels through `setLabelSource('osm', …)` (empty list when disabled). The panel list follows
  the first track (click = `setProgress`). The same effect calls `followLandmarks` (items attached to a stop follow it when the landmarks are published again).
- **Hiding one by one**: « Jamais » on a landmark adds its OSM id to `settings.landmarks.hiddenIds` (project document,
  undoable, "modifié" chip). After filter and cap, `splitHidden` separates the shown landmarks (`landmarks`: labels, automatic
  stops, titles) from the hidden ones (`hidden`, listed greyed out and struck through in the panel so they can be shown again;
  « Tout remettre en auto » empties the list), without a new request. Projects saved without `hiddenIds` are
  completed on load (`withLandmarkDefaults`); ids that are not strings invalidate the setting.
  Projects saved without `places` get every size (`withLandmarkDefaults`); a saved `kinds.place` is kept.

## Points of interest

- **Use**: name a place that OpenStreetMap does not know or that matters to you ("Pique-nique" (picnic), "Le chalet de
  Paul" (Paul's chalet)), shown like landmarks in the 3D view and in the film.
- **Model**: `settings.film.pois` (`FilmPoi { id, lon, lat, name, icon? }`, ids `poi-<n>`, `icon` missing = pin,
  `setPoiIcon`), like stops: saved in the
  project, undoable, validated by `isValidFilm` (coordinates within bounds, ids unique across the whole film), kept by
  presets like the rest of the film (it belongs to the track). In the film rather than separate: stops, which are also
  places of the outing, are there, and an old project receives `[]` from `withFilmDefaults` without migration. OSM landmarks, on the other hand,
  are not saved (re-read from Overpass); only their settings are.
- **Adding**: right-click on the terrain › "Point d'intérêt ici" › name typed in the menu (empty: "Point d'intérêt 3",
  `defaultPoiName`), or « Point d'intérêt au marqueur » (add at the marker) in the « Repères et points d'intérêt » section of the Carte tab (`LandmarkPanel`, name
  field selected immediately). Each addition, removal and rename (keystrokes merged) goes through `editFilm`: one
  undo step, without selection on the timeline.
- **List**: name editable in place (an empty name has no label), "Arrêt" = 4 s stop at the recorded point of
  the first track closest to it (`poiStopAtM`, `nearestOnPath`), named after the point, selected in the inspector; ✕
  deletes.
- **Rendering**: `Labels` labels (pin, priority above climbs), so fading under the terrain and in the distance,
  deduplication, fading out under the opening and closing cards, and export are identical to the other labels.

## Film overlay (phase 4)

- **Principle**: the overlay is not DOM. A pure, synchronous function, `drawOverlay(ctx, frame, settings, size, assets)`
  (`src/overlay/draw.ts`), draws it on a 2D context, on screen or off screen. The preview (`OverlayCanvas`, a 2D canvas above
  the 3D canvas, without mouse events) and the video export (`exportOverlay.ts` → `ExportController`) call
  the same function: the film shows exactly the preview.
- **Styles** (`OVERLAY_STYLES`, `OVERLAY_THEMES`): `editorial`, `broadcast`, `app` and `neon` (« Néon nuit »: deep-blue glass,
  glowing cyan edge) place the widgets at the user's anchors and only differ by colours, fonts and panels. Three styles
  have a structure of their own (`src/overlay/structures.ts`, `styleStructure`, a pure function of the frame like the rest):
  `cinema` (« Cinéma »: two black letterbox bars of 11 % of the height; the counters as one centred subtitle line in the
  bottom bar, « 12,4 km · alt. 2 450 m · D+ 980 m · 4 h 12 », widely spaced, thin dots between; the profile as a thin line
  along the top edge of the bottom bar; weather in the top bar, the map just under it at the right; the opening card centred in light Fraunces
  capitals with wide letter spacing), `journal` (« Journal de bord »: a full-height cream sheet with a thin ink border on
  the left edge, about 24 % of the width; from the top, the title in italic serif, the counters as a list with dotted
