  leaders, the weather, the framed map with its north arrow, the profile as an ink line with altitude ticks; the closing
  card replaces the figures with the totals in the same list; nothing in the right half) and `tableau` (« Tableau sportif »:
  a translucent dark band over the bottom 20 %, the counters as big condensed numbers spread evenly with the unit in the
  accent and a small capital label, the profile as a faded area filling the band, the title on an accent stripe at the top
  left (two lines at most), the map clipped to a circle at the top right). `STYLE_LAYOUTS` gives each of them the default
  anchors and sizes of its widgets, applied by `withStyleLayout(overlay, style)` when the style is chosen in the picker
  (nothing is overwritten while the style stays the same; the free offsets of the laid-out widgets are dropped; leaving a
  structured style puts the anchors back to the defaults of a new project). A widget is drawn by the structure only while
  it sits in a slot of the style (`inStyleSlot`: the style's anchor, or the left column for `journal`, and no free offset);
  moved elsewhere, it is an ordinary widget with the style's theme. The structure leaves an `area` of the frame to the
  ordinary widgets, the timeline texts, the photos and the credits (`layoutWidgets(items, size, gap, area)`). A structure is
  drawn whenever the overlay is on. Removed ids (`sport`, `mono`, `topo`, `magazine`, never released) open in `broadcast`,
  `editorial`, `editorial`, `editorial` (`withKnownStyle`, in `SETTING_UPGRADES`, for the overlay and the poster). Also for
  the poster (`POSTER_LAYOUTS`). **Text backing** (`OverlayOverrides.textBacking`, styles without panel: Éditorial, Cinéma):
  'blur' (default: `drawPanel` draws a rounded rect off-canvas and only its `shadowBlur` shadow lands in place, no
  `ctx.filter` for Safari; replaces the former oval scrim), 'shadow' (darker, wider text shadow), 'outline' (`strokeText`
  rim). **Labels** follow the style (`OverlayTheme.label`, `resolveLabelStyle`: font, panel, text colour, radius, one accent
  per `LandmarkKind`), even with the overlay off; `OverlaySettings.labelColors?` (partial kind → '#rrggbb',
  `isValidLabelColors`, `withLabelColor`) overrides an accent; Éditorial reproduces the former look.
- **Units**: 1 u = 1% of the shorter side of the image; safety margins of 5% on each side; shadow blurs corrected for the
  context transform (devicePixelRatio). Widgets that share one of the 9 anchors stack (`layoutWidgets`). A widget with a free position (`offset`) leaves its
  anchor's stack (the others close up as if it were not there): it is placed alone at its anchor, moved by the offset
  from the anchor's corner of the safe area towards the inside of the frame (to the right / down on a centred axis), and
  may overlap the others. Every choice of a position in the frame uses the same 3 × 3 grid (`src/ui/AnchorPicker.tsx`,
  arrow keys via `nextGridIndex`): overlay elements, source credits (`allowed`: the four corners), timeline texts and photos.
- **Data** (`data.ts`): `prepareOverlayTrack(track, weather?)` once per track: path, cumulative elevation gain (same smoothing and same
  3 m hysteresis as `computeStats`, so the last value equals `stats.ascentM`), smoothed speed and heart rate,
  slope, power and temperature (`metricValues`), profile, closing statistics, weather summary, mini-map outline (`miniMapOutline`: local equirectangular
  projection, x east / y south, longest side = 1, proportions kept, at most 1,500 points). `overlayFrameAt(data, progress)` is
  a pure function of progress (including `mapPoint`, the marker position on the mini-map, and `clockMs`, the recorded
  time of day under the marker).
- **Counters** (`COUNTER_IDS`): distance, altitude, D+, slope, elapsed time, time of day (`clock`), speed, pace (min/km
  from the speed, « – » below `PACE_MIN_KMH`), heart rate, power, temperature; each is left out when the track does not
  record it, and greyed out in the panel. The five added in 3.3 (`slope`, `clock`, `pace`, `power`, `temperature`) are
  filled in off in older projects by `withOverlayDefaults`.
- **Settings**: `settings.overlay` (`src/overlay/settings.ts`, disabled by default; the opening title and the closing card keep only their switch, a hint and a « Modifier » button in the « Habillage » panel (« Titres »): their other settings live in the inspector of their timeline block, `CardFields`, shared with the camera shot of the same end of the film: `card-title` and `opening` open one « Ouverture » inspector, `card-end` and `closing` one « Clôture » inspector): style (`editorial`, `broadcast`, `app`) and
  widgets — opening card, closing card, counters, profile, mini-map (optional north arrow), logo, free text,
  weather, ranking — each with `enabled`, `anchor`, `size`. Optional `markerColor` ('#rrggbb', '' or absent = the 3D marker's colour:
  `marker.color`, else the colour of the track under the marker; `OverlayExtras.markerColor`) on `profile` and `minimap`: the position dot
  (« Point de position » in the widget's Style tab, « Comme le marqueur » to reset). Optional `offset {x, y}` on every placed widget (`PLACED_WIDGETS`), absent by
  default (stacked): whole pixels of the exported film (`OFFSET_MAX_PX` = ±10 000), checked by `isValidOffset`;
  `withWidgetOffset` sets it or drops the key. `overlayExtras` passes `exportSize` (`videoSize(aspect, resolution)` without
  the device cap); `drawOverlay` scales the offsets by the ratio of the shorter sides of the drawn frame and of
  `exportSize`. Ignored on the closing card while its credits roll. `logo.opacity` (0–1, default 1, filled in by
  `withOverlayDefaults`) is the opacity the logo is drawn at. Widgets added after the first format (`minimap`,
  `credits`, `leaderboard`) are completed with
  their default before validation (`withOverlayDefaults`, via `SETTING_UPGRADES` in `src/project/document.ts`): old projects
  and presets always load (`minimap` and `leaderboard` disabled, `credits` enabled). Live widgets fade out during the
  cards.
  Logo as a PNG data URL of at most 512 px (self-contained projects). Validation: `isValidOverlay` (`SETTING_CHECKS`).
- **What the cards show** (`CardExtras` on `overlay.title` and `overlay.end`, every key optional and absent in older
  projects, so no upgrade: the cards are the same): `figures` (ids of `CARD_FIGURES`, drawn in that order; absent =
  `cardFigures`: none on the opening card, `END_CARD_FIGURES`, the five it always showed, on the closing one), `line`
  (≤ `CARD_LINE_MAX` characters, a line of its own: after the date and subtitle line on the opening card, before the
  weather line on the closing one; `cardWidget` draws one subtitle line per `\n`), `image` (PNG data URL like
  the logo, drawn above the title, decoded by `createCardImages` in `src/overlay/assets.ts` for the preview and the
  export), `showProfile` and `showMinimap` (`cardGraphics` in `src/overlay/draw.ts`: the profile and mini-map widgets
  drawn inside the card without their own panel, the whole route covered, no marker). `cardStats` turns the stats into
  the figures, leaving out those the track does not record; descent and lowest point come from `Track.stats`, the
  average speed from the (corrected) distance and duration. A card with a picture, the profile or the mini-map
  (`cardHasGraphics`), and an opening card with figures or a line, is drawn as a card even in a style that places it in
  its structure, like a closing card with credits; the « Journal de bord » sheet follows the closing card's `figures`
  and `line`.
- **Colors and fonts** (`settings.overlay.overrides`, optional, absent by default: old projects and default
  look unchanged): accent, text, panel background (color, opacity) as `#rrggbb`, font for titles and figures
  from a short list (`OVERLAY_FONT_IDS`: bundled Fraunces, IBM Plex Sans and Sans Condensed, Georgia, system
  font, system monospace; nothing is downloaded). Key outside `DEFAULT_OVERLAY`: its shape is not
  checked by the document, `isValidOverrides` checks everything (object, known fields, values). `withOverrides` applies
  a change and removes the key when nothing is changed any more ("Revenir au style" (back to the style)). The drawn theme comes from a single
  pure function, `resolveOverlayTheme(style, overrides)` (`themes.ts`), called by `drawOverlay`: preview and export
  identical. The accent also recolors what the style drew in its accent (profile, mini-map), the text gives the
  secondary text (same color at 80%), the background also recolors the photo mat; a style without panels
  ("Éditorial" (editorial)) keeps its veil. Per element (`STYLED_WIDGETS`: title, closing, counters, profile, weather, mini-map,
  ranking, text): optional `overrides` on the element, same fields, on top of the overlay's
  (`widgetOverrides`, `withWidgetOverrides`); `drawOverlay` draws this element with its own theme; validated by
  `isValidOverlay` (an invalid element rejects the overlay, like the rest). "Couleurs et polices" at the bottom of each
  element of the Habillage tab; "Comme le reste de l'habillage" (like the rest of the overlay) removes its changes.
- **Ghost race ranking** (`settings.overlay.leaderboard`, off by default, anchorable, offered from two
  tracks, drawn only when the ghost race is on): `leaderboardRows(raceAt(race, progress), tracks)` (`data.ts`,
  pure) sorts the racers with `rankRacers` and gives rank (ties at the same point and the same gap: 1, 1, 3), name, color
  and gap to the leader (difference between the `raceAt` gaps to the followed track: in time if the race has it, otherwise in
  distance; "Tête" (lead), then "Arrivée" (finish), for the leader). Computed on every frame from the progress by
  `overlayExtras(time, progress)` (`extras.leaderboard`), for the preview as for the export. Live widget
  (fades out under the cards), "Classement" (ranking) row then one line per track; the gap column keeps its width.
- **Film time**: `drawOverlay(ctx, frame, settings, size, assets, extras)`; `extras.time` (`OverlayTime`: film time
  of the frame, durations of the opening, the flight and the film, from `overlayTime(clock, progress, timeS)` = `timeS ??
  clock.timeAtProgress(progress)`, like `FlyoverRig`); without a clock, `progressTime(progress)` (one-second flight, time =
  progress: the tests and the old behavior). The cards are timed on the film time: by default the opening card
  appears on the first frame (opening shot included) and fades out at `opening + title.end × flight`; the closing card
  appears at `opening + end.start × flight` and stays until the last frame (closing shot included); fades of 1% and
  2.5% of the flight. A card placed on the timeline (`window`) shows between its two times. Rolling credits
  (`end.credits`, optional, one line per name, `creditLines`): the card leaves the layout and heads a centered roll
  (`drawCreditsRoll`), still while it fades in, then rolling up so the last line leaves the top on the last frame
  (`creditsRollProgress`, also in `overlayTimedState` so the export redraws each frame of the roll).
- **Timeline texts** (`extras.texts` = `settings.film.texts`): visible within `[startS, startS + durationS)` of film
  time, 0.4 s fades (at most a quarter of the duration, `filmTextOpacity`), same style as the "Texte libre" (free text; body, style
  panel, size × `size`, safety margins), text on two lines at most (the second cut with "…"), subtitle
  below in the label style. They are added after the widgets of their anchor (stacked); a text alone on its row
  (top, middle, bottom) can take the whole safe width, next to a text from another anchor in the row a third
  (`filmTextMaxWidths`): no overlap between texts. They do not fade out under the cards.
- **Timeline photos and videos** (`extras.media` = `settings.film.media`, images decoded by `assets.photo(src)`,
  video frame by `assets.video(media, time in the file)` (`clipTimeS`); drawn even with the overlay
  disabled, the preview mounts `OverlayCanvas` as soon as the film has media): visible within their window with the text
  fades, only once the image is decoded. A video is placed like a photo (full screen or card), without
  Ken Burns. Full screen: below everything else, cropped to cover the frame
  (`kenBurnsCrop`, pure) with, if `kenBurns`, a slow zoom from 1 to 1.1 (or the reverse) and a diagonal glide of at most
  4%, at constant speed over the window, direction derived from the id (the same on every frame); live widgets
  fade out underneath as under the cards; the caption is drawn like a timeline text at the photo's
  anchor. Card: widget at its anchor (after the widgets, before the texts), image 26 u × size high (40 u wide
  at most, within the safe area), style mat (`theme.photo`: white shadowed print in "Éditorial", dark glass and
  accent bar in "Diffusion" (broadcast), rounded paper card in "Application" (app)), one-line caption below ("…" if too
  long). Credits stay above everything.
- **Fade of a transition** (`extras.dip` = `transitionDipAt(settings.film, time)`, read by `overlayExtras` for the
  preview and the export): a full-frame black or white rectangle at its opacity, drawn even with the overlay turned
  off (the preview mounts `OverlayCanvas` as soon as a shot has a fade), after the widgets, texts and photos, under the
  credits only: it is a transition of the whole picture (widgets left on a black screen would float), brief, and the
  credits required by the licenses stay readable. The export counts its opacity in `overlayTimedState` (a held frame
  is not repeated during a fade); the overlay-only export contains it too.
- **Source credits** (`settings.overlay.credits { enabled, position }`, enabled by default, four corners, chosen on the same 3 × 3 grid with only the corners enabled): small line
  along the edge, outside the safety margins, on a discreet background specific to each style (`theme.credits`: translucent ink
  and white text for "Éditorial" and "Diffusion", paper and ink for "Application", readable on snow as on forest), wrapped
  to the safe width if it is long. Content: `overlayCredits` (`data.ts`), the same strings as the status bar
  (current terrain and imagery, Open-Meteo when the weather is loaded, OpenStreetMap when landmarks, water or the
  highlighted region are), read from the
  stores by `overlayExtras` (`exportOverlay.ts`) for the preview as for the export. Drawn even with the overlay disabled
  (the preview mounts `OverlayCanvas` as soon as either one is active). With credits disabled, the Open-Meteo credit is still drawn
  alone when the weather is shown (as before); enabled, it joins the line. Disabling them means citing the sources
  elsewhere (text in the Habillage panel).
- **Fonts**: a canvas does not trigger the download of web fonts on its own; `loadOverlayFonts()` requests them before the
  first frame, and the export must wait for it too. Bundled fonts (`src/ui/fonts.css`, `public/fonts/`):
  Fraunces 300–700, IBM Plex Sans and Sans Condensed available offline.
- **Weather**: widget and line on the closing card (`weatherWidgetData`, `summarizeOuting`), with the Open-Meteo credit drawn
  as soon as they are visible (in the credits line, or alone at the bottom right if it is disabled).

## Video export (phase 5)

- Since the flyover is a pure function of `playback.progress` and the film time, a film is a list of film times
  (`buildFrameTimes`: ramp 0 → duration over `duration × fps` frames, plus 1 s held at the start and 2 s at the end) and the matching
  progress values (`buildFrameSchedule`); each frame sets both (`setProgress(progress, time)`). `ExportController` (in `TerrainLayer`) runs the
  request placed in the export store (`src/export/store.ts`, separate from the application store): playback paused,
  `frameloop 'never'`, renderer and camera at the video size with a pixel ratio of 1 (reapplied before each render;
  canvas shown letterboxed during the export), pointer disabled.
- The schedule follows the film clock (see "Film and timeline"): the ramp lasts `clock.totalTime()` (opening and closing
  included) and frame k shows `clock.progressAtTime(k / (n − 1) × duration)` at that film time (slow-downs and stops as in
  the preview; held frames reuse the already composited image, except when the view moves with time: opening
  and closing shots, orbit stops, orbit and cinematic styles, or when the timed overlay changes (`overlayTimedState`:
  opacities of the cards, the texts, the photos and the videos of the timeline, plus the film time while a full-screen
  photo moves or a video is visible; a text that appears during a fixed stop is therefore rendered, and so is a video
  during a stop); the images of the visible photos and videos are decoded before the frame is rendered
  (`loadFrameMedia`, since composition must follow rendering in the same task; `releaseFrameMedia` at the end of the export).
  Videos: exact and reproducible frame, never real-time playback; `createExportVideos` opens each visible
  video with mediabunny (`CanvasSink`, rotation applied, 3 rotating canvases) and `createClipReader` gives the last
  frame that starts no later than the requested time in the file, reading forward (each frame decoded once);
  going back or jumping more than 2 s: reopened at the keyframe; an unreadable video stops the export with a
  message; the frames held at the start and the end stay frozen at
  times 0 and duration).
- For each progress value (`renderSettledFrame`, `src/export/capture.ts`), `advance` until the **view is final**:
  `stats.pendingVisibleTiles === 0` right after an `engine.update` of the camera that will be captured, i.e. the selection
  of that update asks for no tile that is loading (DEM and imagery together: a tile is `ready`, hence drawable, only with
  its texture), queued or waiting for its retry (export), and has no child left to request after its parent loaded (the
  update that finds a parent ready queues the children, so the count is not 0 until the finest level is in). Limit 5 min
  per frame (`DEFAULT_SETTLE.timeoutMs`, shared by the settles of one frame, longer than two retry chains of the engine in
  a row). `renderSettledFrame` resolves `'complete'`, `'timeout'` or `'missing'` (`stats.missingVisibleTiles > 0` at the
  capture). Anything but complete **stops the export like a cancel** (file removed) with an error naming up to three
  tiles: host, z/x/y, layer, cause, attempts (`tileFailureMessage`); counted as `timeouts` or `givenUp` in the timings,
  the console line and the history, logged with `console.error` and a breadcrumb. The same applies to a 3D still and the
  3D poster. When the previous (sub-)frame needed no wait and the clouds have slices (`finalFirst`), the final render is
  made first; it is kept when it asks for no tile, runs no re-drape and leaves the camera within 1 m: the same image as
  the usual two renders, one render instead of two. The final render selects once more: if that selection asks for tiles, the frame settles and renders
  again. Everything cached: one `advance`, no wait. Then the
  pending re-drapes of the track and labels run right away (`flushDrapes`, instead of their delay) and
  the frame is rendered once more. While tiles load, nothing is rendered: every 16 ms only `engine.update` runs for the
  camera of the last render (`updateTiles`; the other layers load their data per track, not per view), then the frame
  is rendered again once the view has its tiles, or at the timeout. A (sub-)frame waiting for tiles costs 2 renders
  (3 with a re-drape) instead of one every 16 ms + render time, each with the clouds' noise slices (e2e, SwiftShader,
  tiles answered after 300 ms: 320 → 36 renders for 20 frames; clouds and shutter open, 219 → 58 for 5 frames). The
  engine's delays are times, not numbers of updates, so these 16 ms updates do not hurry them (tile hosts unreachable:
  20 frames in 45–50 s instead of 82–107 s).
  With volumetric clouds (`exportHasClouds`: `CloudsLayer` registered with its slices) only the last render of a
  (sub-)frame pays for them: `settle` renders without the clouds (`advance(false)` sets `setSkipClouds`, the wrapped
  `update` returns at once and the clouds buffer keeps its previous content, never captured), then `flushDrapes` and
  the camera correction, then `renderSettledFrame` makes one final render (`advance(true)`) with all the noise slices.
  The captured pixels are those of the old last render (the slices start from scratch each time). Cost: one more
  render without clouds in the best case (1 render with clouds before; 1 without and 1 with now). Without clouds the
  renders are as before.
  The camera is repositioned (progress shifted by 1e-9) only if the final terrain moves it
  by more than 1 m, never after a timeout. Every 5 rendered frames, the tiles for frames +5 to +40 are
  requested ahead of time (`engine.prefetch`), and again at most every 100 ms while a frame waits for its tiles
  (`PREFETCH_WAIT_MS`): the network is mostly idle during those waits (one load in flight out of 24 was typical), and
  a prefetch only reaches one level deeper per call (children are selected once their parent is loaded). Prefetches (and the preview's retries) are fetched at a low network priority (`priority: 'low'`, Priority Hints, `BACKGROUND_PRIORITY` in `terrain/fetch.ts`; ignored where unsupported): the browser serves the tiles the frame waits for first.
- The WebGL image is composited in the same task as the render onto an `OffscreenCanvas` (sky gradient, image, then overlay
  `DrawOverlay(ctx, { progress, time }, w, h)` after loading its fonts, `time` = `overlayTime(clock, progress,
  frame time)`; still image: current playback time), then encoded by mediabunny (WebCodecs): MP4 H.264,
  otherwise MP4 HEVC, WebM VP9, WebM VP8, the first one accepted by `VideoEncoder.isConfigSupported`; bitrate = pixels × 24 × (fps / 24)^0.7 × 0.16 /
  0.28 / 0.44 bit depending on quality (1080p24 « élevée »: 13.9 Mbit/s), corrected per codec, bounded to 1–80 Mbit/s; keyframe every 2 s.
- **Direct write to disk** when `capabilities.canStreamToDisk` (desktop, or a browser with `showSaveFilePicker`:
  Chrome, Edge): the "Exporter la vidéo" click first asks where to save (`chooseVideoDestination`, called before any
  `await` because the browser picker requires a user gesture; dialog closed = no export; picker
  failure = in-memory fallback) and the export request carries the resulting `WritableFile` (`request.destination`). The encoder
  then writes through mediabunny's `StreamTarget`, in 4 MiB chunks written at their position (`STREAM_CHUNK_BYTES`); MP4
  without "fast start" (`fastStart: false`: `moov` after the frames, `mdat` size patched at the end), read by all
  players and editing software, whereas fragmented MP4 is less widely supported; WebM as is. The codec follows the extension of the
  chosen name (`candidatesFor`: ".webm" typed → VP9 / VP8). Cancel, error or write failure: mediabunny closes its
  stream even on cancel, so the encoder's intermediate stream calls `discard` (not `close`) when the session
  is canceled; `cancel` still calls `discard` afterwards (case of a stream in error) and so does `ExportController` when
  the export fails before the encoder; a request canceled before being picked up, or refused (export already running), deletes its
  file in the store. Result: `url: null`, the toast says "Vidéo enregistrée dans <nom>" and the drawer
  "Enregistrée dans <nom>", without a new download. The drawer shows "Enregistrement direct sur le disque";
  otherwise, above an estimate of 1.5 GB (`MEMORY_WARN_BYTES`, `warnsInMemory`), it warns that the film kept in
  memory may saturate the tab.
- Otherwise an in-memory file (MP4 with its index at the start), downloaded at the end. Written through the same chunked
  `StreamTarget` as the disk, collected as `Blob` parts (about 1× the file in memory at the end, not 3×).
- **Sound** (music and video sound): before the first frame, `mixFilmAudio(film, …)` (`src/film/audio.ts`) mixes the
  film's sound in an `OfflineAudioContext` (48 kHz, stereo) over the exact length of the video (`frame count / fps`,
  held frames included), each music track placed at its film time + the frames held at the start (`round(1 s × fps) / fps`:
  it starts with the film, not during the still waiting frame), read from `inS` over its played duration, gain automated by
  the `musicEnvelope` points (`musicMixPlan`, pure), then a shared bus whose gain follows the ducking under the videos
  (`filmMixPlan`, pure: `music`, `clips`, `duck`). The sound of each heard video (see "Video sound") is decoded
  once by mediabunny (`decodeClipSound` in `film/video.ts`: `AudioBufferSink` on WebCodecs, chunks placed at their
  sample position by `joinSoundChunks`, pure and tested, a single `AudioBuffer` from `inS` over its played duration) and placed
  at the same film time + held frames, directly on the output (never ducked), 20 ms fades at the edges. Rendering
  identical from one export to the next, never in real time; an unreadable file stops the export with a message (for a
  video: "coupez-le dans l'inspecteur"), a video without a sound track stays silent; nothing to hear: no sound track. The encoder (`createVideoEncoder(…, { audio })`) picks AAC in MP4,
  otherwise Opus (`AUDIO_CANDIDATES`, `canEncodeAudio`, 192 kbit/s; Opus only in WebM), adds the sound track
  (`AudioSampleSource`) and feeds it samples as the frames go, 1 s ahead (`AUDIO_LEAD_S`, chunks of at most
  1 s, separate `f32-planar` planes) so the two tracks interleave, the rest at the end; same path in memory and
  on disk. No audio codec: silent film, `audioCodec` null and note "sans le son : ce navigateur ne sait pas
  l'encoder" next to the result (`ExportResult.note`, "avec le son (AAC)" otherwise). Drift between picture and sound:
  at most one and a half frames at the end of the film (the ramp has `round(duration × fps)` frames). Memory: about 23 MB per minute
  of film for the mix, plus the decoded sound of the heard videos (about 23 MB per minute of video in 48 kHz stereo).
- Everything is restored at the end of the export, on error or on cancel (size, pixel ratio, frameloop, pointer,
  progress). Setting `settings.video { aspect, resolution, fps, quality, manualBitrate, bitrateMbps, codec,
  keyframeIntervalS, audioKbps }` in the project document (old `video.format` converted and missing fields filled by
  `withVideoDefaults`). `quality` drives the render tier and, unless `manualBitrate`, the bitrate; `bitrateMbps` (1–80)
  overrides it for every codec (`targetBitrate`, still bounded by the device cap); `codec` 'auto' (H.264 › HEVC › VP9 ›
  VP8) or 'avc' / 'hevc' / 'vp9' (only those the device encodes are offered: `encodableCodecs`); key frame interval
  0.5 / 1 / 2 / 5 / 10 s; audio 96–320 kbit/s; passed to the encoder as `request.encoding` (`videoEncoding`). The Vidéo
  panel groups them in « Format », « Encodage » and « Rendu ». formats 16:9, 9:16, 1:1, 4:5, 21:9 × short side 720p, 1080p, 1440p, 4K
  (`videoSize`); 24 / 30 / 60 fps.
  **Platform presets** (`PLATFORM_PRESETS`, chips above « Format »): YouTube 4K (16:9, 4K, 30 i/s, H.264, 40 Mbit/s), YouTube HD
  (1080p, 12), Reels / TikTok / Shorts (9:16, 1080p, 10), Instagram / Facebook (4:5, 1080p, 8) and square (1:1, 8): one click sets
  aspect, resolution, fps, `codec: 'avc'` and a bitrate by hand (`withPlatformPreset`, one undo step; the quality, key frames, sound
  and name stay; `platformPresetOf` lights the chip the settings match exactly). A phone or tablet still caps the size
  (`exportResolutionNote`). **File name template** (`video.nameTemplate`, `''` = the track's name, ≤ `NAME_TEMPLATE_MAX` = 120):
  `expandNameTemplate` with `{project}` `{track}` `{format}` `{resolution}` `{fps}` `{date}` (the recording's date, else today), an
  unknown word left as typed; for a single film (the batches keep « projet – format »). **Time before the first film**:
  `rateFromHistory` reads the last film rendered to the end from the history (the same rate as `filmRate`, per frame and
  megapixel) when none was measured this session, and `estimateRenderSeconds` gives « ≈ … de rendu » in the summary (cold tiles make
  a first film slower: it is an estimate). **Where it goes**: the website's save dialog has an `id`, so Chrome / Edge reopen on the
  last folder; the desktop one is opened on the folder of the last film (`openflyover.lastFilmFolder`) and, after the export,
  « Ouvrir le dossier » shows the file in the system's file manager (`Platform.revealFile`, the opener plugin's
  `reveal_item_in_dir`, permission `opener:allow-reveal-item-in-dir`; `ExportResult.path`). The website cannot reveal a file.
  **Loudness** (`src/film/loudness.ts`, « Volume » in « Encodage », `video.normalizeLoudness` off by
  default and `loudnessLufs` −14 / −16 / −23, → `VideoEncoding.loudnessLufs`, null when off): `ExportController` runs
  `normaliseLoudness` on the mixed soundtrack in place before the encoder gets it: `measureLoudness` is ITU-R BS.1770-4 (the two
  K-weighting biquads at 48 kHz, 400 ms blocks at 75 % overlap, absolute gate −70 LUFS, relative gate −10 LU; null for silence or
  another sample rate), the gain is the target minus it (at most `MAX_GAIN_DB` = +15 dB up) and `limitPeaks` is a look-ahead
  limiter (5 ms: the minimum gain needed over the next 5 ms, averaged over as long, so it never exceeds what a sample needs) on
  a 4× oversampled (Catmull-Rom) peak, ceiling −1 dBTP. Pure functions; tests on a 997 Hz stereo sine (−23 dBFS reads −23.0 LUFS,
  as EBU TECH 3341 gives). The preview is not normalised.
  **AV1** (`video.codec: 'av1'`, MP4): offered only where the device encodes it (`encodableCodecs`)
  and used only when chosen (`AV1_CANDIDATE` is not in `CODEC_CANDIDATES`, so « Automatique » never lands on a slow software
  encoder), bitrate factor 0.5 of H.264; the native ffmpeg path does not assume an AV1 encoder (null, like HEVC). **Image
  sequence** (`src/export/sequenceEncoder.ts`, « Séquence d'images PNG », `ExportRequest.sequence = { folder }`): a
  `VideoEncodeSession` that writes frame `i` as `<name>_00001.png` through `canvas.convertToBlob` into the folder picked on the
  click (`pickFolder`: `showDirectoryPicker` on Chrome / Edge, the folder dialog on the desktop; written flat, no subfolder), no
  sound (`mixFilmAudio` skipped), no held frames (holds 0), `ExportResult.fileName` the pattern `<name>_#####.png`; a cancel keeps
  the images already written (a closed file cannot be removed through a folder). `VideoEncodeSession.codec` is a `SessionCodec`
  (container and codec as strings) so that `png/png` fits. **ProRes** is not offered: WebCodecs cannot encode it (mediabunny only
  muxes it), and the desktop route would be the system's ffmpeg (`prores_ks`, `.mov`) with a new choice of the native encoder.
- Render scale `exportRenderScale` = short side / 1080 (1 outside export): track width and label size, in
  pixels, are multiplied so that a 4K film looks like the 1080p one. Markers do not need it (size proportional to the
  camera distance, hence a constant screen fraction).
- Still image ("Image fixe" button in the « Affiche » mode, `startStill` in `src/poster/export.ts`, type from `settings.poster.type`): same request with `still { progress, type }`, the current playback
  progress, at the video's format × resolution size. Same path as the film (size, pixel ratio 1,
  render scale, `renderSettledFrame`, `composeFrame` with the overlay if it is shown), a single frame rendered at the
  progress shifted by 1e-9 so that the camera is placed by the current style (and not the hand-orbited view), then
  `OffscreenCanvas.convertToBlob` (JPEG quality 0.92; extension based on the type obtained). Name: `<trace> <progression> %`.
  Restoration identical to the film.
- Overview still image (poster): `still.overview` and `still.compose` (see "Poster").
- Motion blur (`settings.lens.shutter`): up to 8 renders per frame across the open shutter (fewer when the view barely
  moves), averaged by the composer (see "Lens").
- **Overlay only** ("Habillage seul (fond transparent)" (overlay only, transparent background) checkbox in the drawer, drawer state, not saved): to lay
  the overlay over your own footage in editing software. `overlayOnly` request: same `buildFrameSchedule` /
  `buildFrameTimes` as the film, hence the same frames, at the same instants, frame for frame; `runOverlayOnly`
  (`ExportController`) does not touch the scene (no size, no `frameloop`, no waiting for tiles, no WebGL image) and does not
  mix the sound. Each frame is redrawn (`composeOverlayFrame`: canvas cleared, transparent, then `DrawOverlay`
  with `loadFrameMedia` before), then encoded as WebM / VP9 with transparency (`createExportEncoder(…, { transparent })`,
  sole candidate `ALPHA_CANDIDATES`; on the Linux desktop, ffmpeg: see "Video export without WebCodecs"): mediabunny (`alpha: 'keep'`) splits color and alpha in a worker and encodes
  the alpha with a second VP9 encoder, stored next to each frame (`BlockAdditional`, `AlphaMode` header). The
  browser therefore only needs an ordinary VP9 encoder, checked by `isConfigSupported` (`pickCodec`); without VP9,
  the drawer says so and disables the button (no fallback to a PNG image sequence: thousands of images in memory, and
  of little use). Same destination as the film (disk or memory, desktop included), same cancel (`abandon`: file
  deleted). Name: `<trace> habillage.webm`. No size estimate (nearly empty frames, well below the target
  bitrate); times not counted in `secondsPerMegapixel`.
- **Batch rendering** (`src/export/batch.ts`, "Plusieurs formats" mode of the drawer): `buildBatchJobs(selection, still
  image format)` gives the checked films (format × resolution, in the order of `VIDEO_ASPECTS` then `VIDEO_RESOLUTIONS`,
  keys `16:9@1080p`), then the still image (playback position, PNG, in the "Vidéo" mode format) and the poster (its
  settings, `startPoster`). Frames per second and quality: those of `settings.video`. Names: `batchBaseName` →
  "<projet> – 16x9-1080p", "<projet> – image 16x9-1080p", "<projet> – affiche" (`effectiveProjectName`).
  `runBatch` runs the jobs one by one (a failure does not stop the next ones; a canceled job, or a cancel
  requested between two jobs, cancels all the remaining ones), each through `exportJob`: the same request and the same
  `ExportController` as a single export (view restored after each one, shared tile cache), waiting for the end through
  `settledExport` (store subscription), then `takeResult` (the store returns the result without revoking its URL: the batch
  keeps each file). "Annuler" in the top bar cancels the current job, hence the whole batch; "Tout annuler" (cancel all)
  does too (`useBatchStore.cancel`). The "Vidéo" drawer neither downloads nor announces anything during a batch: the batch does it.
- Batch destination: with direct writing and a folder picker (`canPickFolder`: desktop, or
  `showDirectoryPicker` in Chrome / Edge), a folder is requested on the "Tout exporter" click (before any `await`);
  each film is written there as it is encoded (`WritableFolder.createFile` → `WritableFile`, extension based on
  `pickCodec`), still images and poster copied at the end (a file with the same name is replaced). Folder refused = no export;
  picker failure or no picker = each file kept in memory and saved as soon as it is ready (download,
  `saveUrl` on the desktop), its URL kept for "Enregistrer à nouveau" (save again) until the next batch.
- **One film per track** ("Un film par trace" source of the "Plusieurs formats" mode, only with a folder picker
  and direct writing): "Choisir le dossier des traces…" (choose the tracks folder) reads a folder (`pickReadableFolder`:
  `showDirectoryPicker` in read mode, or the desktop folder dialog, which adds the folder and its files to the fs
  scope, `read-dir` / `read-file` permissions already granted); `trackFiles` keeps the GPX / FIT files, sorted by name ("Sortie 2"
  before "Sortie 10"). "Tout exporter" asks for the output folder, then `useBatchStore.runTracks` → `runTrackFilms`:
  for each file, `show` imports it alone (`importFile`) and displays it in place of the tracks (`clearTracks` +
  `addTracks`, like opening a project) with the automatic film (`trackFilm`: shots, options and music from the user's
  film; generated stops; speeds, framings, texts, media and points of interest removed), starts the track's landmarks and
  weather and waits for them (`TRACK_DATA_WAIT_MS`, 30 s at most); then the checked
  jobs go through `runBatch` / `exportJob`, request derived from this track's clock (`shownFilm`), names
  "<fichier sans extension> – 16x9-1080p.mp4" (`trackFilmName`). Unreadable file or failed film: track flagged,
  move on to the next one; track without timestamps: film without weather or time, as if loaded by hand. Progress
  "3 / 12 · Sortie 3.gpx · 42 %" (`trackProgressLabel`), "Tout annuler" as for the batch. At the end, whatever happens,
  `restore` puts back the same objects (tracks, settings, frame origin, playback): "Enregistré" remains, and the undo
  history, suspended for the whole run (`History.suspend`), keeps no step from the batch.
- Batch estimate (`estimateBatch`): files, frames to render (each film counted), size of the films whose codec is
  known, and render time = frames × megapixels × `secondsPerMegapixel`, the speed of the last film exported in the
  session (`filmRate`, measured by the store at the end of a film; nothing before the first).
- **Main thread**: once per frame the loop yields (`yieldToEventLoop`: `scheduler.yield()`, else a message port); the
  progress reaches the store at most every 250 ms (`throttledReporter`, last frame forced); the overlay preview
  (`OverlayCanvas`) and the timeline are frozen while an export runs and redrawn at its end.
- **Encoding pipeline**: the encoder takes the canvas synchronously in `addFrame` (`VideoSampleSource` +
  `new VideoSample(canvas)` before any await; ffmpeg path: `getImageData`), so frame i is encoded while frame i+1
  settles and renders (one `addFrame` in flight). `pickCodec` asks `hardwareAcceleration: 'prefer-hardware'` first, else
  `'no-preference'` (`CodecCandidate.hardware`). On the native path the compositor's 2D context is created with
  `willReadFrequently` (`compositorAttributes`).
- **Timings** (`ExportTimings`): `rendered`, `renders` (advance calls), `renderMs` (main thread), `gpuMs` / `gpuRenders`
  (GPU timer queries `EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2`, `gpuTimer.ts`; 0 where unavailable, e.g. Safari / iOS: the
  composition then holds the wait for the GPU), `waitMs`, `composeMs`, `frameMs` (VideoFrame / readback), `encodeWaitMs`
  (back-pressure), `encodeMs` = the last three, `timeouts`, `givenUp`. `timingsSummary` writes them on one line for the
  console `[export]` line, the history « Répartition » and the diagnostic « Résultat » (added to the stored report when
  an export ends, shown in « Dernier export »).
- **Shadow pass count** (#52): `shadowTiles`, `shadowTriangles` and their `shadowOffscreen…` share, summed over the renders. `TerrainShadow`
  counts them after fitting its camera (`lastShadowPass`: the visible tiles three's shadow culling keeps, the off-screen
  casters apart); `ExportController` adds them after each `advance`. `shadowSummary` writes « ombres : N tuiles (M hors
  champ), K triangles (… hors champ) par rendu » in the console `[export]` line and the diagnostic « Ombres » row (not in
  the history). Measure before changing which tiles cast.
- Limits: without direct writing (Firefox, Safari), file kept in memory (~1× its size at the end); the tab must stay open, speed depends on the GPU (to be measured on a
  machine with a GPU, see the manual checks of `docs/handbook/index.html`).
- **Command line** (desktop, `src-tauri/src/cli.rs` + `src/export/cliRender.ts`): `openflyover --rendu <dossier>
  [--sortie <dossier>] [--prereglage <nom>] [--formats 16:9@1080p,…]`. Rust reads the arguments at startup (`parse_cli`,
  tested; unknown option, missing value, options without `--rendu`: message and exit code 2), makes the paths absolute
  (`prepare`: existing tracks folder, output folder created), adds these two folders, and only these, to the fs scope, and
  keeps the request. The application asks for it once at startup (`cli_render`, which consumes it: a reload does not
  restart anything), applies the named preset (`presetSettings`), checks the formats (`cliFormats`; default: the one from
  "Vidéo"), reads the tracks in the folder (`readableFolderAt`), runs the same `runTracks` as the drawer with
  `writableFolderAt(output)`, writes `rendu-en-lot.txt` (`cliReport`: one line per track) and quits through `cli_exit`
  (0: all films done; 1: a failure; 2: impossible request). The window opens (WebGL needs it).
- **Render history** (« Historique des rendus », last section of the export drawer; `src/export/history.ts` pure and
  tested, `historyStore.ts`, `src/ui/ExportHistory.tsx`): `installExportHistory` (called in `main.tsx`, so also in the
  command-line batch) watches the export store; the run starts at `begin` (phase `rendering`, after the save and
  confirmation dialogs) and ends on done / error / canceled, so `wallMs` is the real render time, with the frames,
  the distinct images rendered, the `ExportTimings` split and the encoder kind (`webcodecs` / `native`). Every film is
  recorded, each file of a batch too; still images and posters are not. An entry (`HistoryEntry`) is flat and small:
  name, outcome and short error, output (size, fps, quality, codec, bytes), and `RenderSettings`, the curated summary
  of the settings that change the render time (atmosphere, shadows, sun, clouds mode / quality, lens, imagery and
  terrain source ids, water on / off, camera style); no track, coordinate or project. Stored as
  `{ v: 1, entries }` under `openflyover.exportHistory.v1` in the platform `storage`, newest first, at most
  `HISTORY_LIMIT` (20) entries and `HISTORY_MAX_BYTES`; `parseHistory` never throws and drops corrupted entries or
  fields. « Réutiliser ces réglages » (`reuseSettings` + `applySettings` in one history transaction, toast with
  « Annuler ») puts back the export options and these fields only, ignoring unknown source ids.

## Poster (phase 7)

- **Principle**: a PNG poster of the outing, in one click from the export drawer ("Affiche" mode, next to "Vidéo").
  The 3D view is rendered through the still-image path (`ExportController`) at the size of the layout's view box,
  then drawn with the text onto an `OffscreenCanvas` the size of the poster by `drawPoster`.
- **Settings**: `settings.poster` (`src/poster/settings.ts`): `format` (A4 2,480 × 3,508, A3 3,508 × 4,960, portrait or
  landscape, at 300 dpi; square 2,160 × 2,160), `style` (the seven styles of the overlay, with its labels), `title`
  (empty = project name, `effectiveProjectName`), `subtitle` (followed by the track date), `figures` (distance, elevation gain, duration,
  max. elevation, number of climbs), `weather`, `flat` ("Carte à plat" (flat map), missing from an old project: `false` through
  `withPosterDefaults`, `SETTING_UPGRADES`). Validation `isValidPoster` (`SETTING_CHECKS`). A preset keeps only
  its style (`presetSettings`, like the film reduced to its shots): the title belongs to the project.
- **Content** (`content.ts`, pure): title, "sous-titre · date" (subtitle · date) line (`formatDate`, `i18n/format.ts`), figures that the track records
  (a missing figure is not drawn, its chip is grayed out), 400-point profile (`elevationProfile`), weather line
  (`weatherSummaryLine`, only if the weather of the first track is loaded), credits (`overlayCredits`, the same
  strings as the status bar), read from the stores by `currentPosterContent` (`export.ts`). Several tracks: a series of
  outings is summed (`totalStats`: distances, elevation gains, durations and climbs added, highest max. elevation; a
  sum that one outing does not record is omitted), "3 sorties" (3 outings) and the dates from the first to the last in the
  subtitle, without a single day's weather; a ghost race (`race.enabled`) keeps the figures and the weather of the
  first track (the same route), "3 traces" (3 tracks). In both cases, no profile: up to `POSTER_LIST_MAX` (6)
  tracks, a list (color chip, name, distance, elevation gain, short date "12/07/2026", `trackLine`) takes its
  place; beyond that, only the count and the totals.
- **Layout** (`layout.ts`, pure): 1 u = 1% of the short side. The boxes depend only on the size, the style and the
  rows present, never on a measured text: the size of the view to render is known before any measurement, and a text cannot
  spill over onto its neighbor. Portrait and square: text below the view (one row of figures); landscape (ratio
  > 1.15): 40 u column on the right (two figures per row, credits at the foot of the column). Square: rows at 70%. Arrangements (`POSTER_LAYOUTS`, each style picks one): "Éditorial" (paper page, view inset by 6 u), "Diffusion" and "Néon nuit" (full-bleed view, ink band or column with a trail-marker red bar), "Application" (view across the whole poster, rounded and shadowed paper card on top), "Cinéma" (a thin black band over the view, the lower band carrying the text centred: title in capitals, the figures on one thin line, profile as a line; same on every format), "Journal de bord" (a paper column down the left edge, 30 % of the width, wider for a list of tracks, with the title on two lines, the figures as a list with dotted leaders, the map of the track in the room left — `PosterContent.outline`, `PosterRows.map`, `PosterLayout.map` — the profile, the weather and the credits at the foot; the view takes the rest over the whole height) and "Tableau sportif" (the view over a full-width dark band with an accent line: the title on an accent stripe, the figures big and spread evenly, the profile as an area behind them). `PosterLayout.bands` holds the black bands beyond the panel. The text is fitted to the drawing
  (`fitText`: size reduced down to a floor, then "…"; `fitLines`: credits on two lines at most, fixed size of
  1.15 u before reduction). Track list: 3 u rows, one column (two on the square, filled column after
  column); a single size for all rows (`fitTrackList`: figures in right-aligned columns as
  wide as their longest text, reduced until 40% of the row is left for the name, cut with "…"). Tests: all boxes within the page and its margins, within the panel, with no overlap between
  them or with the view (except the floating card and, in "Tableau sportif", the profile area behind the figures), view ≥ 38% of the poster, for each format × style × set of rows.
- **Drawing** (`draw.ts`): `POSTER_THEMES` themes in the "Carte alpine" design system (paper, ink, trail-marker red, glacier),
  fonts of the overlay styles of the same name (Fraunces, IBM Plex Sans, Plex Sans Condensed), all in `OVERLAY_FONTS`
  (bundled and loaded by `loadOverlayFonts`, tested). View cropped to cover its box (`coverCrop`).
- **View rendering**: `startPoster` loads the fonts, computes the layout and places a still-image request
  `still { overview: true, framing, compose }` the size of the view box (PNG); `framing` (`framingPath`: all
  the tracks as one path) makes the overview frame all the tracks, each drawn in its color by the
  scene. In `ExportController`, `overview` does not change
  the progress (so `FlyoverRig` does not move the camera): before each render the camera is placed on
  `overviewView` (framing of the overview shots, at the box ratio) seen from the south, north up, and the controls target
  follows; the progress marker (`flyover-marker`) moves to a layer that no camera renders; camera, target and
  layer are restored at the end. `compose` replaces the film overlay: it receives the composited image (sky + view) and renders
  the poster, encoded as PNG like a still image; render scale of labels and track = short side of the view
  / 1,080, like a film. The result follows the path of the other exports ("Vidéo" drawer always mounted: download,
  `saveUrl` on the desktop, toast); name "<projet> – affiche.png".
- **Flat map** (`view.ts`): `still.drawView` replaces the 3D render (`runDrawnStill` in `ExportController`: neither
  scene nor camera touched, canceled through `AbortSignal`). Map drawn in 2D, simpler and safer than an
  orthographic camera (tile engine, aerial perspective and haze designed for a perspective camera):
  tiles of the current imagery (`settings.imagerySourceId`, Web Mercator, north up) framed on the box of all
  the tracks with an 8% margin (`planFlatMap`: never zoomed in further than the finest tiles, zoom rounded for
  tiles between × 0.7 and × 1.4, one level less as long as more than 300 are needed, tiles outside coverage omitted), loaded by
  a `createTileFetcher` with 6 requests (HTTP cache and offline packs first), gray where a tile is missing (failure if
  none), then each track in its color on an edging in the style's page color, width of the 3D track
  (`trackStyle.width` for 1,080 px).
- **Preview**: 2D thumbnail (≤ 260 × 220 CSS px) drawn by the same `drawPoster` under a transform, without 3D rendering:
  the last view rendered for these tracks and this view (`previewKey` includes flat / overview / camera, kept scaled
  down to 480 px, `usePosterPreview`), else an approximate flat map at low zoom (`previewMap`, ≤ 360 px, debounced
  400 ms, shared fetcher of 4 requests, 64 tiles), else a sky with the outline of the first track (`miniMapOutline`);
  a hint says the preview is approximate.
- **View, type and progress**: `poster.view` 'overview' | 'camera' (« Vue d'ensemble » / « Vue actuelle de la caméra »:
  `still.camera` keeps the camera's pose, put back before every render, marker hidden); `poster.type` 'image/png' |
  'image/jpeg' (quality 0.92; also for « Image fixe », now in this mode). `renderFlatMap` reports `onProgress(done,
  total)` and returns `{ view, missingTiles }` (`ExportResult.missingTiles`, shown in the panel);
  `exportStore.tiles { done, total }` (flat map: requests; 3D: most pending seen and how many came). The 3D poster
  waits for its tiles like a film frame and fails the same way.
- **Limits**: ghost race markers drawn at the current progress; an A3 view
  in the "Application" style is 17 Mpx in WebGL (GPU size limit not checked). Flat map: no shaded relief,
  no labels except a pin on each start and finish (the 3D one without text, one for a loop, one where racers start
  together); tiles at other zooms than those of the 3D view, so partly downloaded.

## Weather in the scene (phase 7)

- `src/weather/sceneWeather.ts` (pure; `settings.weatherScene { enabled, strength }`, "Météo dans la scène" (weather in the scene) checkbox visible with
  the atmosphere and loaded weather): on every frame, the weather under the marker at the sun date (`sceneConditionsAt`:
  interpolated cloud covers, hourly totals and WMO 45/48 fog re-centered on the middle of their hour then interpolated) gives
  `sunScale`, `skyScale`, `hazeScale`, `hazeHeightM`, `shadowStrength`, `exposureCompensationEv`, `desaturation`, `skyVeil`
  (formulas at the top of `sceneWeatherFrom`, bounded, identity for a clear sky, without data or with the setting off; `strength` blends
  toward identity). With o the cloud opacity (min of the total cover and low + 0.6·mid + 0.25·high), w the rain or snow
  wetness (1 − e^(−mm/h ÷ 2) or 1 − e^(−cm/h), the larger) and f the fog: `sunScale` = (1 − 0.8·o^1.5)·(1 − 0.5·max(w, f)),
  down to 0.1 under a rainy overcast; `skyScale` = (1 − 0.35·o^1.5)·(1 − 0.25·w), about 0.5 at worst;
  `hazeScale` = 1 + 2·low² + 8·rain + 12·snow + 16·f, at most 30 (visibility 60 km ÷ `hazeScale`);
  `shadowStrength` = (1 − 0.9·o^1.5)·(1 − 0.7·max(w, f)). A pure function of progress: the export stays deterministic.
- Application (`AtmosphereLayer`): intensities of the `SunLight` and the `SkyLight`, `shadow.intensity`, compensation added to
  the exposure; under the veil, the SH coefficients of the sky light tend toward their luminance.
- `WeatherEffect` (`src/scene/weatherEffect.ts`, after the aerial perspective, same `EffectPass`): the Takram effect has no
  density setting, hence an exponential height fog (β0 = 3.912·(hazeScale − 1)/60 km at ground level under the marker;
  `hazeScale` of the weather, at most 30, analytic integral along the ray; the manual "Brume" slider, a uniform white
  veil, was removed: older projects and presets that still carry `haze` load without it, unknown keys are ignored), veiled sky, distance pulled toward gray under the veil, desaturation; logarithmic
  depth read as in the Takram effect; uniforms prefixed `weather*`.
- Limits: the light, the haze, the veil and the precipitation come from the weather under the marker only, applied to the whole scene (the cloud cover alone varies over the scene: "Volumetric clouds", cover field).

### Export quality tiers (« Qualité d'export »)

- `video.quality` (« Standard » / « Haute » / « Maximale ») sets the bitrate (`encoder.ts`) and, since the tiers, how much the costly
  effects are computed in the export: `QUALITY_TIERS` in `src/export/qualityTier.ts` (pure, tested), applied **at export time only**:
  the project is never rewritten and the preview is unchanged. Stills and the poster take the tier of their request's quality too.

  | Quality | Clouds export quality (`EXPORT_SAMPLES`) | Motion blur, most renders per frame | Bitrate (`QUALITY_BITS_PER_PIXEL`) |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | Standard | « Rapide » (`low`, 16 slices) | 4 | 0.16 |
  | Haute (default) | « Moyenne » (`medium`, 24 slices) | 8 | 0.28 |
  | Maximale | « Fine » (`high`, 32 slices) | 8 | 0.44 |

- **Precedence**: a value chosen by hand wins over the tier. `settings.clouds.quality` is `'auto' | 'low' | 'medium' | 'high'` and
  defaults to `'auto'` (« Selon la qualité de l'export »): `effectiveRender(quality, clouds)` takes the tier's clouds quality on
  `'auto'`, the hand-chosen one otherwise (both ways: « Rapide » by hand stays « Rapide » at « Maximale », « Fine » by hand stays
  « Fine » at « Standard »). The sub-frame cap has no setting of its own: it comes from the tier and only ever lowers the 8 of
  `SHUTTER_SUBFRAMES`, so a tier never raises anything above the full quality of before. Not a write-through: `'auto'` is what keeps
  the export-time override out of the project (a project saved before keeps its `'medium'`, i.e. the same film as before, shown as
  « Personnalisé »; « Revenir au préréglage » sets `'auto'`).
- **Where it applies**: `CloudsLayer` reads `effectiveRender(request.quality, …)` while an export runs (`useExportStore`, so web, desktop and
  the `--rendu` batch, which all go through the export store, use the same tiers); `ExportController` passes `maxSubFrames` to
  `adaptiveShutterSamples(…, max)` (and `subFrameCount(spanPx, max)`: window = max / (max − 1) of the span, still ≤ `SHUTTER_STEP_PX`
  between renders) and to `shutterSamples`. The count stays a function of the film time and the quality alone: same project + same
  quality → same frames. `subFrameSlices` still splits the clouds' slices exactly among the sub-frames.
- **Badges** (`presetBadge`, `PresetBadge` in `PanelSection.tsx`, `preset` prop of `SettingRow` / `RangeField`): « Imposé par Haute » (periwinkle
  pill after the weight pictogram) on the clouds quality row (Météo) while it is `'auto'`, and on the motion blur slider (Objectif) only
  when the tier lowers its 8 (Standard); « Personnalisé » (chartreuse pill) on the clouds row once chosen by hand, with the row's
  « Revenir au préréglage » button. The « Qualité » row of the export drawer then reads « Haute · Personnalisé » (`isCustomised`), lists
  what the quality drives and repeats the button. Depth of field has no lever of its own (see below), only the repetition at each
  sub-frame, which its pictogram counts.
- **History**: the entry's `RenderSettings.clouds.quality` is the quality the export used, and `tier: { subFrames, cloudsByHand }` records
  the cap and whether the hand chose the clouds (optional field added to version 1: older entries parse as before, an older app
  ignores it). « Réutiliser ces réglages » puts `'auto'` back when `cloudsByHand` is false (an entry without `tier` pinned its quality).
- **Not a lever**: depth of field. The `DepthOfFieldEffect` already runs its near-field targets at half resolution (library default), the
  far field and the circle of confusion at full size, and its bokeh taps are shader constants: no one-line change cuts its cost without
  changing the look, so it is left as is (it gets cheaper with the sub-frame cap). To revisit with GPU timings.

## Volumetric clouds (phase 3)

- `@takram/three-clouds` 0.7.6 (MIT, same family and same versions as `three-atmosphere` / `three-geospatial`).
  `CloudsLayer` (in the `AtmosphereLayer` `EffectComposer`, before `AerialPerspective`, which composites them) is mounted only
  with the atmosphere, in `manuel` or `mer` mode, or in `meteo` mode once the weather of the first track (« À la
  suite »: of a stage) is loaded.
- Setting `settings.clouds { mode: 'meteo' | 'manuel' | 'mer' | 'aucun', coverage, altitudeM, seaTopM, seaRender, quality }`
  (default `meteo`, 0.4, 1,200 m, 2,000 m, `volume`, `auto` = the export quality's tier, `Export quality tiers`; `SETTING_CHECKS`: `isValidClouds`; `SETTING_UPGRADES`:
  `withCloudDefaults` for projects saved before `seaTopM` and `seaRender`), "Nuages" (clouds) block of the "Atmosphère
  et météo" section.
- Pure (`sceneClouds.ts`, tested): `cloudCoversAt` gives the cover of the three layers (Open-Meteo low / mid / high at
  the sun date under the marker, via `sceneConditionsAt`; missing layer = total; manual: low = `coverage`,
  mid 60%, high 40%). `sceneCloudsFrom`: the library has only one `coverage`; the layer that asks for the most
  sets it (table measured on the quantiles of the r / g / b channels of `local_weather.png`: cloud fraction → coverage), the
  others are thinned by their exponent `w^e` (e = ln t / ln tᵢ, t = 1 − 2.5·coverage, 1 ≤ e ≤ 8), and beyond 0.4 (nearly overcast
  sky) by their density instead (scaled by Cᵢ / C). Bases: low at `altitudeM` above the lowest point of the track, mid 2 km higher,
  cirrus at max(7 km, ground + 5.5 km), × exaggeration (thicknesses unchanged). Layer "a" (fog) and cloud haze
  turned off: the haze remains that of `WeatherEffect`. Under visible clouds, the `WeatherEffect` sky veil is
  reduced to 30%.
- **Cover that varies over the scene** (`meteo` mode only; v2.1): `cloudCoverField(series, timeMs, frame)` (pure) asks each
  station of the series for its cloud cover at the sun date (`weatherAt` on a series of that station alone), blends them over a
  32 × 32 grid by inverse distance weighting (power 2, minimum distance 100 m, as between stations in `series.ts`) and turns the
  blended cover of each layer into the `coverage` of three-clouds (`coverageFor`, the table above). The grid is a square of the
  cube-sphere UV space around the scene frame (`uvJacobian` gives UV per metre; the texel centres go back to lon/lat through its
  inverse), wide enough for the farthest station + 10 km (20 to 250 km), fixed in the world: it is not drifted with the wind, the
  weather stays where its stations are. It returns RGBA8 bytes (low, mid, high layer, 0; 255 = `MAX_COVERAGE`), the UV centre
  and 1 / width of the square, and the largest cover of each layer. `zonedCloudsFrom` gives the layers (the bases and
  thicknesses of `sceneCloudsFrom` for those largest covers, but density and exponent 1: no thinning, the field carries the
  coverage). A pure function of (series, film time, place): the export renders the same field as the preview (~1.5 ms for
  12 stations, measured in node). The three-clouds shaders read one global `coverage`, so `patchRegionalCoverage`
  (`scene/cloudShaders.ts`, text patch of `sampleWeather` like the patches of `CloudsLayer`) replaces
  `1.0 - coverage * heightScale` by the coverage of each layer from the texture (`regionalCoverage`, bilinear, clamped to its edge;
  uniforms `regionalFrame`, `regionalAmount`), in the cloud march **and** in the shadow map (both materials, shared uniform
  objects). `regionalAmount` is 0 outside `meteo` mode, so manual, sea of clouds and no clouds render as before. The texture
  is refilled every frame only when its bytes change, and a counter joins `cloudInputs` so that a still preview starts averaging
  again. Without a series or without any cloud value the old uniform path applies (no clouds). A single station gives a uniform
  field. Not the same look as before for a near-overcast sky with layers of different covers: before, the layers needing less
  than the dominant one were thinned by density (the shared global coverage); now each is patchy at its own cover.
- Sea of clouds ("Mer de nuages", `mode: 'mer'`, `seaOfClouds`): a single dense low layer (channel r), mid and high
  layers off, whatever the weather. Its top is `seaTopM`, an altitude above sea level (× exaggeration), the most
  direct reading for a user who knows the altitudes of the route: the summits above it emerge. Choosing the mode
  proposes the top three quarters of the way from the lowest to the highest point of the first track (`seaTopFor`,
  rounded to 50 m; slider « Sommet de la mer de nuages », 300–5,000 m). Thickness 600 m below the top: the base stays
  above the valley floors, so a camera following the track under the sea sees an overcast ceiling instead of a
  white-out, and from above the opaque layer hides the valleys anyway. Coverage 1 (cloudy at mid-height even over
  the empty texels of the weather texture) and exponent 0.5 (texels pulled towards 1): the cloudy threshold of the
  library rises towards the top of the layer for every texel at nearly the same height, so the top is flat at the
  scale of the scene and the shape noise erodes it into billows of 100–200 m, with gentle dips over the empty texels;
  density 0.3 (cumulus 0.2). Same drift with the wind and same export path as the other modes.
  Shape (October 2026, after a real-GPU screenshot: a flat, uniform whitish sheet): coverage 0.95 and exponent 0.75,
  so the dips over the empty texels break the sheet; a crisper threshold (`coverageFilterWidth` 0.3 instead of 0.6),
  a density decreasing from the base to the top (`densityProfile` −0.5·h + 1 instead of 0.75·h + 0.25: dense bottom,
  wispy top), a shape noise of 4 km instead of 3.3 km (`shapePeriodM`, `shapeRepeat` of the effect, cumulus-scale
  billows) and a density of 0.15: the light enters the billows, which shade each other at a low sun, instead of
  a flat white surface. `SceneClouds` carries the optional shape (`CloudShape`) and period; the other modes keep the
  library defaults. The visible tops sit a little below `seaTopM` (the density fades towards the top of the layer).
- Surface sea of clouds (`seaRender: 'surface'`, « Rendu de la mer de nuages » › « Nappe », shown under the top of the
  sea): `CloudSeaSurface`, a mesh of the scene in `AtmosphereLayer` instead of `CloudsLayer` (not mounted then), to
  compare with the volumetric sea on a real GPU. One draw call: a radial grid centred under the camera
  (`buildRadialGrid`: 8 m to 300 km, 256 sectors, rings growing by 1 + 2π/256 so the cells stay square, ~112k
  vertices), lifted in the vertex shader to `seaTopM` × exaggeration minus the relief, along the curvature of the Earth
  (−r²/2R), and displaced into cumulus billows (`seaRelief`, pure and tested, written to GLSL by `seaReliefGlsl` from
  the same constants): rounded domes on a jittered grid (smooth maximum between neighbours) in five octaves from
  2.2 km down to 110 m (75 to 8 m high, unexaggerated) over a 9 km swell, in a domain warped by 380 m; an octave
  covering fewer than `LOD_SAMPLES` = 6 cells (vertex) or pixels (fragment) fades to its mean: noise-free by construction, flat at the
  horizon. Drift: `cloudDrift` of the same wind × film time, as in the volumetric mode.
  Shading (fragment, per pixel): normal by finite differences of the relief, steepened ×2 for the shading only
  (cumulus domes are rounder than a sea can be displaced); sun direction, colour × intensity of the Takram `SunLight`
  and irradiance of the `SkyLight` probe (plus the night fill) set every frame, so golden hour turns it warm; wrap
  lighting (0.25), creases darker than the tops (occlusion from the relief), self-shadowing from the coarse relief
  probed toward the sun at 60, 180 and 450 m, forward scattering (Henyey-Greenstein, g 0.6) through the rims and thin
  tops; seen from below, a grey overcast ceiling. The aerial perspective hazes it like the terrain.
  Soft edges: a 256² grid of terrain altitudes over the terrain area (tracks + `AREA_MARGIN_M`, lon/lat of the
  samples computed once per area), a half-float texture sampled again (debounced, flushed by the export) when tiles
  arrive; the cloud fades over its last 120 m (× exaggeration) above the terrain (`edgeFade`) and is discarded where
  invisible (no depth written). A pure function of the settings, film time and camera: the export draws the same sea.
- Wind: the archive's wind at the start of the outing (constant for the film, 4 m/s westerly breeze by default); in « Manuel »
  and « Mer de nuages », « Vent » (`clouds.windKmh`, 0–100 km/h, 15 by default, 0 = still) from `windDirection` (the weather's, or one of the
  eight compass points), also in « Météo » with `windByHand` (`settingsWind`); × 2 at altitude
  × `CLOUD_TIMELAPSE` (20). Drift = wind × film time (`playback.timeS`, otherwise `clock.timeAtProgress`): offsets of
  the weather texture (Jacobian of the shader's cube-sphere UV, `weatherOffsetFor`) and of the shape textures (ECEF
  displacement × repeat) set directly on every frame, library velocities at zero: nothing accumulates.
- Quality: preview with the `low` preset, full resolution (half until October 2026). While the view changes (camera, sun, cover, layers or
  drift differ from the previous frame: `cloudInputs`), temporal upsampling: one pixel in 16 is marched per frame and
  the others are reprojected, cheap but never averaged: each pixel keeps the single noisy ray marched for it, and on
  the dense sea of clouds the jitter of the march start (up to two steps of 150 m and more) showed as a regular grain
  of 4 × 4 cloud pixels (8 × 8 screen pixels at half resolution: the speckle of the user's real-GPU screenshot) that
  a still view kept for good, whatever the number of frames drawn. Once the inputs are those of the previous frame
  (`previewCloudPass`), every pixel of the pass is marched with the marches of the `low` preset (`STILL_MARCH`: 200
  steps of at least 100 m, 25 for the shadow map) and the frames are averaged (`temporalAlpha` 1, 1/2 … 1/32: the
  first discards the upsampled history), the clouds asking for frames until 32 are averaged; any change goes back to
  upsampling and `PREVIEW_MARCH`. Cost: while the view moves, 1/16 of the canvas pixels marched per frame (1/64 at
  half resolution before); once still, 32 frames marching every pixel, ~0.5–1 s on a real GPU (estimate; the first
  one compiles the variant of the shader without upsampling once); the view stays interactive, any input goes back
  to upsampling. Export (`isExportBusy`): preset `effectiveRender(request.quality, settings.clouds).cloudsQuality` (`settings.clouds.quality`, or the tier of the export quality on `'auto'`), full resolution, no upsampling and no
  shadow map TAA; the effect's `update` is wrapped: 16 / 24 / 32 renders per frame (`EXPORT_SAMPLES`, low / medium /
  high; 2 / 4 / 6 before) with the frame counter set to k (noise slice) and `temporalAlpha` = 1/(k+1) — the first
  discards the history, the following ones average (still camera, zero velocity). An exported frame therefore never
  depends on the previous ones, whatever the number of `settle` renders; only the final render of a (sub-)frame runs
  them (see "Video export"). The export cost of the clouds grows in
  proportion (×6 for "Moyenne"). With the motion blur, the slices are shared among the sub-frames of the shutter
  (see "Lens").
- Grain, measured with SwiftShader (sea of clouds at 1,800 m, noon, still view 681 × 383, no terrain; mean absolute
  difference between neighbouring pixels in a cloud-only crop, 8-bit, 0.6 on a smooth sky): before (upsampled, never
  averaged) 5.9; still view averaged over 32 frames 0.7–0.9 at half resolution, 1.0 at full resolution (sharper); exported frame
  "Moyenne" (medium) 9.7 with 4 renders, 2.4–2.9 with 24 (real shape detail included). Two still views differing only
  by their noise slices: 1.46 apart with the golden-ratio noise, 1.99 with the former one. The grain is the march, not
  SwiftShader: the sky and the terrain of the same frames are smooth (0.6), and the same speckle shows on the user's
  real GPU. A plain average of the export renders (no variance clipping) was tried and dropped: its brightness
  depended on the noise slices (unexplained). Verified: the root cause (upsampling keeps one noisy ray per pixel, the
  resolve shader never averages it) and the numbers above; inferred: the cost on a real GPU.
- Local resources: textures served at `/clouds/` by the `vite.config.ts` plugin (~2.8 MB). The spatio-temporal blue
  noise (STBN) of the Takram examples is not in the package (downloaded from GitHub, NVIDIA license): it is
  replaced by an interleaved gradient noise generated at startup (`cloudNoise.ts`, 128 × 128 × 64; slice z shifted by
  z × the golden ratio, modulo 1, so that consecutive slices stratify the march start of a pixel), also passed to
  `AerialPerspective` (otherwise it downloads it as soon as the clouds provide their shadow map). Nothing is downloaded
  from GitHub.
- Limits: layers at a fixed altitude for the whole film; in the preview, temporal upsampling
  trails when the camera moves fast, and the moving or playing preview keeps the grain of the upsampling (only a still
  view and the export average); high cost on a weak GPU ("Aucun" (none) mode). The edge of the sea of clouds against
  the relief is where the march stops on the depth of the terrain: three-clouds 0.7.6 has no option to soften it
  (no soft-particle or depth-fade parameter); the lower density and the wispy top of the sea soften it somewhat.
- Cloud shadow map (`SHADOW_MAX_FAR_M` = 50 km, `shadow.temporalPass` off): by default the library spreads its
  cascades over the camera's far plane (5,000 km here, `FlyoverCanvas`), so the first of the two 256² cascades of the
  preview covered ~1,000 km; below a sun of ~35° every cloud turned black (SwiftShader, every mode). Limited to
  50 km, the clouds render down to a sun at the horizon, self-shadowed; the reprojection of the shadow map still made
  the clouds beyond its range black at a low sun, so it is off (export already without it; the preview averages the
  jitter of the shadow map with the frames of a still view). Beyond 50 km the clouds get the light of the short march
  towards the sun only. Verified with SwiftShader (golden hour, sea of clouds, with relief): black before, lit and
  self-shadowed after; not yet on a GPU. The resolve also drops a non-finite history or output (`guardResolve`,
  defensive: a single NaN frame would otherwise stay in the history for good).
- **Cloud shadows on the terrain** (v2.1): the terrain is lit by three's lights (`SunLight`), which know nothing of the clouds,
  but the cloud shadow map (Beer shadow map, `effect.atmosphereShadow`) already reaches `AerialPerspective`, which only used it to
  dim its own post-process lighting (`sunLight`, not used here), so its result was never used. `patchCloudShadow`
  (`scene/cloudShaders.ts`, text patch of its fragment shader, applied on the first frame the effect exists by
  `addCloudShadow` in `AtmosphereLayer`) multiplies the colour of a terrain pixel (depth < 1, before the clouds are composited
  over it, so the clouds themselves are not dimmed) by `mix(1, sunTransmittance, strength · sunShare)`: `sunTransmittance` =
  exp(−optical depth) read from the shadow map by the library's own PCF (8 taps, 4 on a phone or tablet, jittered with the
  noise texture), `sunShare` = share of the sun in the irradiance there (`GetSunAndSkyIrradiance` on the local vertical:
  the shader has no normal of the relief, a horizontal surface is assumed), `strength` = the weather's `shadowStrength`
  (the sun is already dimmed under an overcast, so the shadows fade there instead of darkening twice), 0 with the
  « Ombres des nuages » checkbox off (`settings.cloudShadows`, default on, Lumière › Atmosphère; no shadow-map read then), without clouds, in « Nappe » mode (no shadow map) and at night
  (the sun share is 0). The shadow map was already rendered for the clouds themselves: the added cost is the per-pixel
  read. The export pins the `frame` uniform of the aerial perspective (the dither slice of its PCF) so the same frame
  gets the same noise whatever the renders before it. Limits: the shadow map covers 50 km from the camera
  (`SHADOW_MAX_FAR_M`) in two cascades of 256² texels in the preview; cirrus (no `shadow` flag in three-clouds) cast none; the
  shadow darkens everything drawn under it (track, marker, sprites) like the terrain; no relief normal, so the darkening of a
  slope facing the sun is under-estimated; software GL only (SwiftShader), not seen on a real GPU.
- **Night**: the atmosphere lights the clouds with the sun and sky only, so they went black at night. `addNightFill`
  patches the march shader (sky-light line) with a `nightIrradiance` uniform set each frame to the terrain's night fill
  (hemisphere sky colour × `nightFillIntensity`): constant moonlight, no moon phase or position. Occlusion unchanged (a
  cloud hides the marker and the track). Not yet checked on a GPU.

## Reflective water (phase 3)

- **Data**: OpenStreetMap through Overpass (same client, queue and 30-day cache as the landmarks:
  `cachedOverpassQuery`, `runOverpassQuery(…, parse)`), **two requests per track** and only if `settings.water.enabled`.
  The first, the water areas:
  `way` / multipolygons `natural=water`, `waterway=riverbank`, `water=*` in the boxes of the track corridor widened to 8 km,
  `out geom qt` (~600 kB, 3–4 s for the sample; `docs/sources.md`). The request is not canceled on unmount (shared and
  cached; StrictMode runs the effect twice); its response is only ignored if it is stale. A failed request is only
  retried on the next track or setting change. The second (4.0, `waterLinesQuery`) gives the **coastline** (`natural=coastline`
  ways, in the same 8 km corridor) and the **waterway lines** (`waterway=river|canal|stream` ways, in a narrower 4 km corridor,
  `WATER_LINE_MARGIN_M`: streams are many), `out geom qt`. The layer runs both together (`Promise.allSettled`) and shows what
  came; the status chip says « erreur » if either failed. Measured on four tracks (2026-10-22, `docs/sources.md`): 0.55–1.1 MB, 21–39 s
  on a busy public server, 40–76 kB once reduced.
- **Pure reduction** (`water.ts`, tested): relation rings stitched end to end from their ways (direction irrelevant,
  unclosed ring dropped), inner rings attached to the outer ring that contains their first point, member way of a
  returned relation ignored, Douglas–Peucker 4 m, polygons < 2,000 m² discarded, the 300 largest kept, coordinates to 1e-6°;
  `lake` (flat surface) or `river` (`waterway=riverbank`, `water=river|stream|canal…`). Only this compact result is cached.
  **The sea** (`coast.ts`, tested, `parseWaterShapes` → `seaPolygons`): by OSM convention the land is on the LEFT of a coastline
  way and the water on its right. Ways are joined end to end **in their own direction only** (`chainCoastWays`), simplified at
  8 m, coarser while the corridor holds more than 20,000 points (`reduceCoast`). The corridor boxes overlap (their margins), and
  translucent water drawn twice would show: they are cut into disjoint rectangles (`seaCells`, the grid of every box edge). In
  each rectangle the coast is clipped (Liang–Barsky); every piece enters and leaves through the rectangle's edge, and the sea is
  the ring made of the pieces and of the edge walked **clockwise** from each exit to the next entry (water on the right all the
  way round; the corners of the rectangle are added). A closed ring inside a rectangle is an island (counter-clockwise: a hole of
  the sea that contains it) or a lagoon (clockwise: a polygon of its own). A rectangle crossed by no coast is all sea if an island
  stands in it, else decided by the side of the **nearest coast** (`seaSide`: its own side inside a segment, the sum of the two
  normals at a vertex), else nothing. **Fallbacks**: a rectangle whose pieces do not close (data cut off, crossing ways) becomes
  a flat rectangle, because the sea plane is at 0 m and hides under any land above it; and **without a single coastline nothing
  is drawn at all**, so a polder or flat land at 0 m stays land. Result: polygons of kind `sea` (`sea/<cell>#<n>`), e.g. 2
  polygons of 2,358 vertices and 69 holes (islands) on the Brittany coast, 54 ms.
  **The lines** (`parseWaterLines`, `WaterLine { id, kind, width?, points }`): tunnels and culverts left out, simplified at 4 m,
  ways under 30 m dropped, the widest then the longest kept up to 200 km (`MAX_LINE_LENGTH_M`). The cache keeps the kind and the
  `width` tag only (clamped 1–150 m); the width of a kind without a tag (`LINE_WIDTH_M`: river 12 m, canal 8 m, stream 2.5 m) is read
  when the water is drawn (`lineWidthM`), so changing it needs no new request. When the area polygons are known, the part of a line
  inside a polygon (riverbank, lake, sea, holes excepted) is cut out (`runsOutside`: a run keeps its first point inside so the
  ribbon reaches the polygon), so nothing is drawn twice.
- **Pure mesh** (`waterMesh.ts`, tested): polygon cut by a grid of square cells (60 m, at most 40 per side,
  Sutherland–Hodgman of each ring against each cell), each piece triangulated with its holes (`ShapeUtils`, earcut). The
  grid puts vertices inside the water: a river follows its valley and the opacity fades toward the bank (`fade` = distance to the closest
  edge / 40 m, capped at 1). **The sea** has no grid: `buildSeaMesh` triangulates the polygon with its holes as it is (flat water
  needs no vertex inside), with `fade` 1 everywhere: where the relief rises above the plane the land hides the water, so the
  shore is the relief's own. **A line river** (`riverStrip.ts`, tested `buildRiverStrip`): the polyline resampled every 60 m
  (`STRIP_STEP_M`, so the ribbon follows its valley), three vertices per sample (left bank, middle, right bank, banks mitred
  at bends up to twice the half-width), `fade` 1 in the middle and 0 on the banks (`EDGE_FADE`), triangles counter-clockwise like
  the lakes'. The ribbons of a track total at most a few thousand samples.
- **Draping** (`WaterLayer`, in `TerrainLayer`): like the track, `engine.sampleHeight` × exaggeration + 2 m, re-draped
  (debounced, flushed by the export via `registerDrapeFlush`) on each `engine.onChange`. A lake is flat at the 90th percentile of the
  heights of its vertices away from the shore (`fade` ≥ 0.5; the median of all of them when fewer than 4: `lakeLevel` in
  `waterMesh.ts`): a low level let the relief of the lake (DEM noise, tile seams, coarse levels of detail rising towards the
  banks) poke through the water as dark patches. A polygon with no known height waits for its tiles.
  The **sea** is flat at 0 m (the datum of the relief: scene heights are above mean sea level) + `SEA_LIFT_M` 0.5 m, so a beach a
