  little above the datum is not flooded; it needs no tile (`pieceHeights`), the same on every exaggeration (0 × any factor).
  A **ribbon** takes the height of its middle vertex for its three vertices (the water is level across the river); a sample
  whose tile is not loaded takes that of the nearest known one along the line (`stripHeights`), none known: skipped.
  A single merged geometry (one draw call), `raycast` neutralized (track picker, camera), `depthWrite` off, a polygon offset
  (−2, −4) towards the camera.
- **Rendering**: `MeshStandardMaterial` (roughness 0.08: sun glint from the `SunLight`, terrain shadows) extended in
  `onBeforeCompile`: normals from four sine waves (23, 9, 4.1 and 1.7 m) animated by the **film time**
  (`playback.timeS`, otherwise `clock.timeAtProgress`), faded out when their wavelength covers fewer than ~8 pixels (no
  shimmering in the distance); sky reflection = Schlick Fresnel (F0 0.02) × 1.5 × irradiance of the Takram sky probe (`lightProbe`)
  in the reflected direction raised above the horizon / π (fixed glacier color without the atmosphere); opacity = bank fade
  × mix(0.82, 1, Fresnel) × `strength`. A function of the film time only: the export stays deterministic. The sea is told
  from the rest by a vertex attribute (`waterSea`): its waves are 3 × longer and 1.5 × taller.
- **Setting**: `settings.water { enabled, strength }` (true, 1; `SETTING_CHECKS`: 0 ≤ strength ≤ 1), "Eau reflétante (mer,
  lacs et rivières)" (reflective water: sea, lakes and rivers; « Lacs et rivières reflétants » before 4.0, same field, so the
  render history and the old projects are unchanged) checkbox and "Intensité de l'eau" (water intensity) slider in the "Fond de carte" section.
- **Limits**: the reflection is that of the average sky (SH probe), neither of mountains nor of clouds; lakes at DEM level (no OSM
  level); a very steep river keeps 60 m steps; only the 8 km corridor is queried (4 km for the lines); the sea is flat (no tide, no swell, no depth colour) and its shore is
  the OSM coast cut by the relief, so a DEM below 0.5 m just inside the OSM coast is flooded (tidal flats, estuaries up to the
  limit OSM maps as coast); a river mapped in a gap of its own riverbank polygon is drawn over it; a stream is a 2.5 m ribbon,
  hardly visible from high; lines are only the 200 km widest. The "© contributeurs OpenStreetMap" credit (status bar and
  export) reads « Repères et eau » when the water is drawn (`overlayCredits` `water`, `useWaterStore.polygons`: sea polygons and
  lines count with the lakes).

## Ghost race (phase 7)

- Progress remains the distance fraction of the first track; each other track receives a
  marker placed by `raceAt(race, progress)`, a pure function. Setting `settings.race { enabled, sync, camera? }`,
  « En parallèle » in the « Plusieurs traces » block of the track list (from two tracks, see "Several tracks").
- Camera (« Caméra sur », `camera`, absent = `'premiere'`: the first track, as before): `'tete'` the racer ahead
  (`rankRacers`; once it has finished, the first one still racing), a cut when the lead changes; `'ensemble'` the
  first track's flight view moved to the centre of all the racers and pulled back until their spread fits the frame
  (`framedGroup`, × `GROUP_FRAMING_MARGIN` = 1.4, never nearer than the flight view: continuous). Another track chosen
  in the list is made the first one (`flyTrack`). The film's marker stays on the first track
  (`FollowedFlight.markerOnFilm`). « Classement à l'image » toggles the overlay's leaderboard widget.
- Synchronization: `elapsed` (same time elapsed since each start), `clock` (same recorded time; waiting at the start,
  stopped at the finish), `distance` (same fraction of each track). The first two require timestamps on all the
  tracks, otherwise fall back to `distance`.
- Time ↔ distance tables per track (points without a time filled, clock made monotonic), lookups by bisection. Ranking
  gaps compared at equal fraction (exact on the same route); displayed as "+1 min 20", "−350 m". In time modes the gap
  is the difference between the race times at which the racer and the lead reached the furthest point both reached
  (positive = behind); without timestamps, the difference of the distances covered, in metres (negative = behind).
- `RaceMarkers` (after `FlyoverRig` in `TerrainLayer`): same kind of marker as the head (`settings.marker`, see "Track
  and marker") in the track color with an ink edge, same screen size as the main marker. Limit: the stops of the lead track are crossed instantly (playback advances by distance).

## Several tracks (« Plusieurs traces »)

- Block of the track list from two tracks: « La première » (default, nothing changes: the first track is flown, the
  others drawn), « À la suite », « En parallèle » (the ghost race). Stored in `settings.race`, no migration: « En
  parallèle » is `enabled` (as before), « À la suite » the optional `sequence` flag (ignored while `enabled`); one track is
  never a sequence (`playsInSequence`). The transition and the cards between the stages are the film's **junctions**
  (below); `race.stageCards` and `race.stageTransition` of a project saved before them are read once, by
  `withStageSettingsMigrated` (in `sanitizeSettings`), into `film.junctionDefault` and dropped from `race`.
- À la suite: each stage can have its own marker through marker keys at the stage cuts (« Un marqueur par étape »,
  Marqueur section).
- « À la suite » (`src/flyover/sequence.ts`, pure): the film flies a **sequence track** (`buildSequence`: the tracks'
  segments one after the other in list order, id `suite:<ids>`, name `chainName`, never stored), cached on the tracks'
  ids, points and names (`sequenceOf`) so it keeps its identity. Unlike « Enchaîner en un seul parcours », nothing is
  merged: each track stays a stage (`Stage`: `startM` / `endM` along the sequence, `from` / `to` fractions of the
  progress) with its colour, name and stats; the stage order is the list order, or `race.order` (**Reordering**, below).
  `filmTrackOf(tracks, race)` (hook `useFilmTrack`) replaces `tracks[0]` wherever the film is concerned: film source
  and clock (`useFilmSource`, `getFilmSource`: landmarks of every stage along the sequence, `sequenceLandmarks`, a
  landmark near two stages once), FlyoverRig and the export, timeline, track picker, climbs and their labels,
  kilometre labels, landmark, climb, highlight and point-of-interest panels (seeking in metres along the film), region
  highlight, water. Stops, speed sections and camera keys are anchored in metres along the sequence:
  the first stage keeps the first track's metres, so switching mode keeps what was placed on it.
- Clock: `FilmClockFor.cutsM` (the stages' starts) and `stageIds` (their track ids) → `clock.cuts` (`{ atM, timeS }`, like
  the camera keys), read by the overlay (`overlayTime` copies them into `OverlayTime.cutsS`) and the motion blur, and
  `clock.junctions` (below). The « Plans » lane keeps a thin band in each stage's colour.
- **Junctions** (`src/film/junctions.ts`, `film.junctions[]`): the junction after the stage of track `afterTrackId` (derived id
  `junction-<trackId>`, so it survives a reorder), `{ kind, holdS, card? }`, every field optional: what it does not set is
  `film.junctionDefault` `{ kind, holdS, card }` (absent = `DEFAULT_JUNCTION`: `nuit`, a fade to black held 1.5 s where the
  stages are on the same day, cards on).
  `junctionOf` / `junctionCardOf` are the only readers. Kinds (`JunctionKind`): `coupe` (hold 0), `fondu-noir`,
  `fondu-blanc` (`holdS` 0.5–10 s), `nuit` (the night between two days). A junction is a **pause of the film clock** at the
  cut (`extra`: added to the film whatever « Garder la durée », like a night; `junctionPlan` → `FilmClockInput.junctions`):
  `clock.junctions[k]` `{ id, afterTrackId, atM, kind, holdS, startS, holdStartS, holdEndS, endS }` (one per cut, a cut
  has all four times equal), `holdStartS` being the film time the marker reaches the cut, so `cuts[k].timeS`, the time the
  stage changes. At the same place a junction comes before a stop, which comes before a night. `junctionInputsFor` computes
  the plan for a film being edited (the timeline's draft). **Nights**: a night found at a cut (`NIGHT_AT_CUT_M`) is the
  junction's when it asks for it (`night`, « Passer la nuit », its own or the default's, « Jours et nuits » on or off: kind
  `nuit` on the clock, the night pause, no extra hold); without it, or without a night to pass, its « Passage » (`kind`:
  `coupe`, `fondu-noir`, `fondu-blanc`, `PASSAGE_KINDS`) replaces it. There is no automatic switch: what the panel or the
  inspector shows is what plays. « Montrer les nuits » only decides the nights inside a stage. **« Revoir l'étape finie »**
  (`recap`): over a night, the high view during it (`ClockJunction.view`); else a pause of its own, `RECAP_S` (4 s) at
  `atM − RECAP_LEAD_M` (1 m: the marker, figures and drawn line stay on the stage that ends), `extra` like the junction,
  placed on the clock as `ClockJunction.recap`; `recapViewAt` lifts the camera over the stage (`RECAP_RISE_S` 2.5 s) and holds
  it to the cut, where the passage takes the next stage. The junction block of the timeline starts with that pause, and
  `stageFlightS` leaves it out. Edits store the whole transition (`withJunction`: kind, hold, night, recap). Project v6
  (`withNightAndRecapOnTheirOwn`) turns a stored `nuit` into `fondu-noir` + `night`, gives another stored kind
  `night: false`, and makes the day cards' high view « À la suite » the default `recap`; the migrations read frozen values,
  never today's defaults. Project v3 (`withExplicitNightJunctions` in `project/document.ts`) writes the default an older project
  played: `nuit` for a default fade to black with the nights on (it used to turn into the night at each cut), a fade to
  black with the nights off.
  Migration: a transition other than `coupe` holds `DEFAULT_JUNCTION.holdS` (1.5 s), so an old project with a dip grows by
  the holds only; with `coupe` its film is identical.
- **Duration per stage** (`film.stageDurations?: Record<trackId, seconds>`, `STAGE_DURATION_RANGE` 5–600 s; absent = the stage's share
  of `flyoverDurationS` proportional to its distance, i.e. today's film exactly): `stageSpansOf(film, stageIds, cutsM, lengthM)` →
  `FilmClockInput.stages` (`FlightStage` `{ fromM, toM, durationS? }`, none unless a stage of the sequence sets one; `junctionInputsFor`
  carries them with the junctions) → `flightPacing(…, stages)`. A stage that sets a duration flies **exactly** that long: its moving
  time is scaled (`stagedMovingTime`) so that moving time + its own pauses (stops: shortened as ever with « Garder la durée »;
  not the extra pauses, junction holds and nights) = `durationS`; the unset stages share `flyoverDurationS × their length / total
  length`, with « Garder la durée » scaling their moving time to it (without it, as before), so the flight is the sum of the set
  durations and the shares (+ holds). Stops are kept in the stage they are in (a stop on a cut belongs to the next one). The scaling is
  piecewise per grid step (the stage edges are grid nodes), so the speed jumps at a cut, which is a junction hold anyway. Not a
  sequence, or no stage set: the code path is the one of before. The clock needs no new field: `stageFlightS(clock, k)` gives the flight
  time of stage k (the junctions are pauses centred on their cut, so their `holdS` is taken off each side; nights inside are taken off),
  what the stage inspector shows. Keyed by track id, so a reorder (`reorderStages`) needs nothing. UI: « Durée du vol » in the stage
  inspector (slider, « Durée automatique »), the right edge of the stage block (`dragStage` in `film/timeline.ts`, found on the clock of
  the film with the duration changed like `dragJunction`; the body of the block still reorders), arrows ±1 s (Shift ±0.1 s), Delete =
  automatic; « Survol » › « Durée et rythme » says how many stages have their own. Not covered: `flightNights` still decides which nights
  the flight crosses faster than they last from the shared duration.
- **Stage card**: `JunctionCard` `{ enabled, durationS (1–15 s, 5), text?, subtitle?, anchor, size, color?, font?, fields? }`,
  read with `junctionCardOf` = `DEFAULT_STAGE_CARD` ← the **common card** (`junctionDefault.card` shown or not,
  `junctionDefault.cardSettings`, set in « Trace › Plusieurs traces », `commonCardOf` / `withCardSettings`) ← what the card
  stores of its own (`StoredStageCard`, partial, on the junction before its stage, `firstCard` for the first): `withJunctionCard`
  stores only the fields that differ from the common card (`patchedCard`; an empty `text` / `subtitle` / `color` / `font`
  goes back to « none » over a common one), so a card hidden by Delete stores `{ enabled: false }` and still follows the
  common card. `hasOwnCard` (« Réglage propre ») is any field but `enabled`; `withCommonCard` / `withAllCardsCommon` take
  them back; the switch « Une carte au début de chaque étape » (`withDefaultCard`) drops every card's own `enabled`. The same
  for the transitions: `applyToAllJunctions` (« Utiliser pour toutes les transitions ») makes one the common transition and
  sends every junction back to it (`withAllJunctionsCommon`). Project v4 (`withStageCardsOverCommon`) trims the cards older
  projects stored whole. Without `text` the automatic card (name,
  « Étape 2 sur 3 · date », the figures of `fields`), with it the film's own words (`fillTemplate`: `{name} {n} {count} {date}
  {distance} {ascent}`, drawn like a timeline text, `STAGE_TEXT_SCALE`). Window (`stageCardWindow`): from `holdStartS` of the
  junction before it (the start of the flight for the first stage) until `durationS` after its `endS` (the stage's flight
  starts there: the card stays on its first seconds; `dragStageCard` counts the duration from there too), not past the
  start of the next junction.
- **Timeline**: an « Étapes » lane above « Plans » in a sequence: a block per stage (`stage-<trackId>`, track colour, selects and
  seeks) and between two of them the junction block (`junction-<trackId>`, `[startS, endS]` of its pause, at least
  `JUNCTION_MIN_PX` wide); the right edge of a fade stretches `holdS` (`dragJunction`, found on the clock of the film with the
  hold changed, `DragContext.clockOfFilm`, so the film ripples), arrows ±1 s (Shift ±0.1 s), Delete goes back to the default
  transition. The cards are blocks of « Textes » (`card-stage-<trackId>`, `card-stage-first`; their right edge sets
  `durationS`, Delete switches the card off; the first one is not shown while the opening title is). Inspector
  (`FilmInspector`, `JunctionFields.tsx`): transition and « Durée », « Appliquer à toutes les jonctions »
  (`applyToAllJunctions`), the card (« Contenu » automatic or « Mon texte », position, size, colours, duration); the stage:
  colour, « Aller au début ». The panel « Plusieurs traces » (`StageDefaults`) sets the default transition and cards.
  Edits are `editFilm` calls: one undo step, texts attached to a stop follow a changed hold (`followStops`).
- **Reordering the stages** (`src/film/reorder.ts`, pure): the order of the stages « À la suite » is `settings.race.order`
  (optional track ids, absent = the list order; ids that are not tracks any more are ignored, tracks it lacks come last), so a
  reorder is a **settings change**, inside the settings history, and `tracks[]` keeps its order (`moveTrack` and « Survoler
  celle-ci » still reorder `tracks[]` in the other modes, where `orderedTracks` is the identity). `orderedTracks(tracks, race)`
  (`flyover/sequence.ts`) is the only reader of the order: `filmSequenceOf` (so `filmTrackOf`, `useFilmSource`, the overlay,
  `filmFollowOf`), `trackUnderMarker` (the store's track of the stage, by position in the ordered list), the drawn lines
  (`refreshDrawOn`) and the track list go through it; the overlay's per-stage weather is matched by track id
  (`prepareOverlayFilm(…, weatherTrackId)`). Weather, sunrise / sunset, the poster and the batch still read `tracks[0]` (documented
  limit). `reorderStages(film, before, after)` (`before` / `after`: `StageSpan[]` `{ trackId, startM, endM }` of the sequence
  before and after the move) shifts by `newStartM − oldStartM` of its stage every stop, camera / parameter / marker key and speed
  portion (an item on a boundary belongs to the stage that starts there; a portion across a boundary is split in two, same factor,
  the pieces under `MIN_SPEED_SPAN_M` dropped; the second piece is `speed-<n+1>`); texts, media and music keep their film seconds
  (those attached to a stop follow it through `followStops`, run by the caller with the clocks of both orders; the free
  ones are left on their film seconds by `followStops` when the cuts of the two clocks differ); a junction keeps
  its transition only between the two stages it was between, in that order, else it is the default one (a junction after the new
  last stage is dropped), and the card of a stage stays with its stage (`firstCard` included); pois, landmarks and nights need
  nothing (lon/lat, ids, computed from the sequence). The store action `setStageOrder(ids)` (`scene/usePacing.ts`, with
  `reorderTracks(trackId, toIndex)` and `orderTracksByDate()` = `idsByDate`) builds both span lists (`buildSequence` of the new
  order), writes `race.order` (dropped when it equals the list order) and the remapped film in **one** `setState` inside
  `getSettingsHistory().transaction`: one undo step restores the order and every remapped item; the playhead keeps its progress.
  UI: the track list has a handle (`data-handle`: mouse drag after 4 px, touch long press 350 ms with `navigator.vibrate`,
  `touch-action: none` on the handle only, auto-scroll of the panel within 40 px of its edges, insertion line, Escape cancels),
  Alt+↑ / Alt+↓ on a card with an `aria-live` announcement, the ▲ button and « Ordonner par date » (`dropIndexAt` finds the place
  from the centres of the other cards); the « Étapes » lane's stage blocks drag the same way (gesture kind `reorder`, a line across
  the lanes where it lands, Alt+←/→/↑/↓ on a focused block; not in the centred phone layout, where a tap selects).
- Camera (`src/flyover/follow.ts`, `filmFollowOf` → `FilmViewOptions.follow`): the flight camera flies the stage under
  the marker on its own smoothed path at its own progress (`stageAt`), so it never glides across the jump between two
  stages: a cut at the film time the marker reaches it. The shots still frame the whole sequence. A pure function of
  the progress, passed by FlyoverRig and by the export's prefetch (`filmViewAt`): preview and export agree.
- Overlay: `prepareOverlayFilm` / `overlayFilmFrameAt`: the live widgets show the stage under the marker (its distance,
  D+, time, profile, mini-map), the opening and closing cards the name and totals of the sequence. A stage card
  (`stageCardAt(sequence, time, film)`, `StageCard`: the automatic name, « Étape 2 sur 3 · date », distance, D+, or the film's
  own text; its own anchor, size and duration, `STAGE_CARD_S` = 5 s by default, from the flight start for the first, from its
  cut for the others, until that duration after the junction ends, as `stageCardWindow`) drawn even while the overlay is off, the live widgets giving way to it; the first stage's card gives way to the title card when that is on. Dip of the junction
  (`junctionsDipAt`, from `OverlayTime.junctions`, i.e. from the clock: covered at `holdStartS` and until `holdEndS`, `JUNCTION_FADE_S`
  0.8 s each side; a night: only around `holdStartS`) merged with the shot dips; the card is drawn **over** the dip it lives in.
  Both enter the export's held-frame key (dip alpha and card opacity), a pure function of the film time.
- Drawn lines with « trace qui se dessine » (`drawOnDistances(…, sequence)`): stages flown whole, the current one to the
  marker, the next ones not yet. Start and finish labels name every stage's ends.
- **Light across the junctions**: see "Sun at the time of the outing" (`junctionSunDate`): a fade blends the stages' suns over its pause, a
  `nuit` junction is dusk, night and dawn (`nightSunDate`), a cut and the sun keys switch or rule as before. « Lumière »: « Source de la
  lumière » (« Heure de la trace » / « Heure fixe » for all stages), « Jours et nuits » a single switch.
- Sun, clouds and weather: `trackUnderMarker(tracks, race, progress)` gives the track under the marker and the
  progress along it (the stage « À la suite », else the first track at the film progress), resolved once per frame by
  `AtmosphereLayer` and shared with `CloudsLayer` and `CloudSeaSurface` (`MarkerTrack`): sun date from the stage's own
  timestamps (`sunDateAt` on its path), weather in the scene, cloud cover, cloud base and wind from its own series. The
  weather store fetches the later stages too (`syncStageWeather`, `stages` by track id, driven by the weather panel;
  `weatherSeriesOf(state, track)`), and each stage gets its own series in the overlay (`prepareOverlayFilm`). A pure
  function of the progress: clouds and weather change on the cut, nothing is interpolated across it (the sun is, over a fade).
- Limits: time smoothing of the camera must not average across a stage cut (as for a shot that cuts); the weather
  panel, the poster and the « Lumière » sunrise and sunset still show the first track.

## Track chaining

- "Enchaîner en un seul parcours" (chain into a single route; track list, from two tracks): `chainTracks` (pure, `src/import/chain.ts`)
  merges the tracks into one, ordered by start time if all are timestamped, otherwise in list order
  (the list is not reordered). Each track keeps its segments: the jump between the end of day 1 and the start of day 2
  is neither drawn (`TrackLines`, one line per segment) nor counted (`computeStats`, `buildTrackPath`); the marker
  crosses it in one go. Two overlapping recordings are refused (a group outing: ghost race).
- New id, stats and bounds recomputed (`buildTrack`), color, source, activity and time offset of the first one,
  waypoints of all; name = common words ("Tour du Mont-Blanc" for "… J1", "… J2") otherwise "première → dernière" (first → last).
  The duration counts the nights between the days.
- Store: `replaceTracks(list)` (same frame origin, playback at the start); `setTrackColor(id, color)` (list chip, playback not interrupted); `flyTrack(id)` (the track moves to the top: it is the one flown over). Tracks are not in the history
  (like deletion): the "Traces enchaînées" message carries "Annuler", which restores the separate tracks; it
  disappears on the next change to the tracks (`chainLoadedTracks`, `projectActions.ts`).
- Import: when a single import yields timestamped tracks that follow each other (no overlap, less than 24 h apart,
  `followEachOther`), an "Enchaîner ces N traces ?" message offers the action on these tracks only.
- « Un marqueur par jour » (Marqueur section): one marker key per junction (`junctionsM`: the segment starts), set
  afterwards in the inspector.
- Limit: stops, key framings and landmarks anchored in meters on the old first track are not re-anchored.

## Strava import

"Importer depuis Strava" (import from Strava; « + Ajouter » › « Activités Strava… » in the track list, "Importer depuis
Strava" on the home screen; `src/ui/StravaImport.tsx`: `StravaDialog`, `StravaImport`): OAuth connection to Strava, list of the athlete's activities, import of the
ticked ones.

- **No PKCE at Strava** (checked in October 2026, developers.strava.com/docs/authentication): the code exchange and
  the refresh require the `client_secret`. Without a server, the user **brings their own Strava application**
  (strava.com/settings/api, free); its Client ID and Client Secret are typed once and kept in
  `getPlatform().storage` of this browser or this computer (`openflyover.strava.app.v1`), with the tokens
  (`openflyover.strava.tokens.v1`: access, refresh, expiry in ms, athlete name). They are only sent to
  `www.strava.com`. **Key policy**: OpenFlyover still has no key or account of its own; no Strava credential is in
  the repository.
- **CORS**: Strava answers `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` on `/oauth/token` (POST, `x-www-form-urlencoded` body) and on
  `/api/v3` (`Authorization` header allowed): the site and the desktop webview call Strava directly.
- **Authorization** (`src/platform/oauthRedirect.ts`, `authorizeInBrowser`): scope `activity:read_all` (read-only,
  private activities included, whole track including privacy zones; the dialog says so), random `state` checked on
  return (`parseCallback`: state, `access_denied` refusal, code, activity scope granted).
  - Site: Strava opens in a pop-up (opened within the click) and sends it back to `/oauth-callback.html` (static page
    in `public/`) which passes its query to the application through a `BroadcastChannel`, removes the code from the
    history (`history.replaceState`) and closes. The application window does not reload: tracks and settings stay.
    Callback domain to declare at Strava: the site's host (`localhost` in development; Strava always accepts
    `localhost` and `127.0.0.1`).
  - Desktop: `tauri-plugin-oauth` (community plugin, FabianLars) listens once on a free port of `127.0.0.1`,
    `tauri-plugin-opener` opens the Strava page in the system browser, the return URL comes back through the
    `oauth://url` event. Nothing more than `localhost` to declare at Strava. The commands of both plugins are called
    through `invoke` (no npm package). Deep links (`openflyover://`) are ruled out: Strava only accepts its callback
    domain and `localhost` as a return.
- **Tokens**: refreshed one minute before expiry (`needsRefresh`, 6 h tokens). A 401 from the API or a refused refresh
  → `StravaAuthError`, tokens cleared, back to the "Se connecter" (connect) form. Client ID / Secret refused
  (`Application` error) → message, form kept. 429 → "100 par quart d'heure, 1 000 par jour" (100 per 15 minutes,
  1,000 per day). "Déconnecter" (disconnect) clears the tokens; "Oublier ces identifiants" (forget these credentials)
  clears the application.
- **Activities**: `/athlete/activities`, 30 per page ("Plus" (more)), search by name among those loaded (the API has
  none). Import: one `ImportJob` per activity (`runImport` in `projectActions.ts`: colors, messages and chaining offer
  as for files), streams `latlng,time,altitude,heartrate,cadence,watts` (`key_by_type`) → `streamsToTrack` →
  `buildTrack`: one segment, time = `start_date` + `time`, `Track.source = 'strava'`, activity name, activity = FIT
  sport name (`stravaActivityType`: `Ride` → `cycling`, `TrailRun` → `trail_running`…, otherwise the type in
  snake_case), time offset from `utc_offset`. An activity without a GPS position is refused.
- **Strava API terms** (docs/sources.md): the dialog shows the text mark "Powered by Strava" and warns that the data is
  for the user alone; "Déconnecter Strava" clears the tokens. Personal use, data shown only to the connected athlete (nothing is sent elsewhere), limits of
  100 requests / 15 min and 1,000 / day per application (one request per page of activities, one per imported
  activity, plus the refresh).

## Desktop application (phase 6, first increment)

Two targets, one codebase: the static website and the Tauri 2 application (`src-tauri/`), which loads the same `dist/`. What
differs goes through `src/platform/`.

- **Contract** (`platform.ts`): `Platform` = `capabilities { isDesktop, videoEncoder, canStreamToDisk, sharesFiles? }`, `storage` (synchronous
  key / value: `get`, `set` → false when the storage refuses (full, blocked; the value then stays in memory for the
  session and `get` returns it), `remove`, `keys(prefix)`, which lists the keys stored or kept in memory), `openFiles({ filters, multiple })` → `File[]` (empty if the dialog is closed),
  `saveFile(blob, { fileName, filters? })` and `saveUrl(objectUrl, …)` → `{ saved: true, fileName } | { saved: false }`, `saveDocument` (save in place, see "Project (phase 4)"),
  `createWritableFile({ fileName, filters? })` → `WritableFile { fileName, path?, write(data, position), close, discard }` or null
  (dialog closed; `discard` stops and deletes the partial file, no effect after `close`, can be called several times),
  `droppedFiles(dataTransfer)`. Filters are those of native dialogs (`{ name, extensions }` without a dot); the website
  derives the `accept` attribute from them.
- **Selection** (`index.ts`): `getPlatform()` (singleton) calls `selectPlatform(globalThis)`: desktop if Tauri has set
  `isTauri` / `__TAURI_INTERNALS__` on the window, website otherwise. `videoEncoder`: `'webcodecs'` if `VideoEncoder` exists, otherwise `'native'` on the desktop (ffmpeg, see
  "Video export without WebCodecs (Linux)"), otherwise null.
- **Website** (`web.ts`): the previous behavior. `<input type="file">` created on the fly (synchronous up to the click, to keep
  the user gesture; `cancel` removes it), download through a link and an object URL, `localStorage`.
  `createWritableFile`: `showSaveFilePicker` (types derived from the filters, `pickerTypes`) then `createWritable()` (Chrome
  writes to a temporary `.crswap` file moved on close); `discard` = `abort()` then `handle.remove()`
  (Chrome 110+), because the picker has already created the file; `AbortError` (dialog closed) → null.
- **Folder** (`folder.ts`, batch rendering): `pickFolder` → `WritableFolder { name, createFile(fileName) }` or null. Website:
  `showDirectoryPicker({ mode: 'readwrite' })`, then `getFileHandle(name, { create: true })` and the same stream as
  `createWritableFile` (`writableOf`); `discard` = `removeEntry`. Desktop: `open({ directory: true, recursive:
  false })` dialog (the dialog plugin adds the folder to the fs scope, direct files only), then `openWritablePath(path)`
  from `desktop.ts`, shared with `createWritableFile`.
- **Desktop** (`desktop.ts`): `@tauri-apps/plugin-dialog` (`open`, `save`) and `@tauri-apps/plugin-fs` (`readFile`,
  `writeFile`), imported dynamically on first call: the website bundles them in separate chunks that are never loaded.
  A file that is read becomes a `File` (name, type derived from the extension), so `openFiles`, `openProject`, `readPhoto` and the
  imports need nothing special. `createWritableFile`: `save` dialog then plugin-fs `open(path, { write, create, truncate })`;
  each write `seek`s only if its position is not that of the cursor (header patches), then
  `write`s in a loop up to the last byte (one IPC call per 4 MiB chunk, bytes as JSON); `discard` closes and
  `remove`s. Storage stays `localStorage`, which the webview keeps in the application folder.
- **Wired up**: "Ouvrir" and Ctrl+O (`chooseFilesToOpen`), "Enregistrer" (`saveProject`, marked as saved only if
  the file is written), drop on the window (`droppedFiles`), export result on the desktop (`saveExportedFile`, one
  call in `ExportPanel`'s `download`; on the desktop the "Télécharger …" (download) link becomes an "Enregistrer …" button and
  the toast action "Enregistrer…"; a film streamed to disk does not need it), film streamed to disk
  (`createWritableFile`, see "Video export"), "pas d'encodeur" (no encoder) message in `ExportPanel` (`videoEncoderMissingHint`). All
  file choices go through `openFiles`: welcome screen ("Choisir un fichier" → `chooseTracksToImport`, "Ouvrir un
  projet…" → `chooseProjectToOpen`), "Ajouter" in the track list, overlay logo (png, jpg, webp, svg),
  timeline "Média" (jpg, png, webp, mp4, m4v, mov, webm: the types the desktop recognizes, `mimeTypeOf`; GIF,
  AVIF… remain possible by dropping on the website). Storage through `getPlatform().storage`, same keys as before (data
  already saved remains): interface preferences (`App.tsx`, `openflyover.shell.v1`), presets
  (`presets.ts`, `openflyover.presets.v1`), weather cache (`openMeteo.ts`, `openflyover.weather.v1`); the last two
  through a `getItem` / `setItem` adapter to the `KeyValueStore`, and Overpass cache (`overpass.ts`,
  `openflyover.osm.v1.<fingerprint>`): when `set` returns false, it deletes its own entries (`keys(CACHE_PREFIX)`,
  never the other keys) and retries once; otherwise the memory cache avoids repeated requests within the session.
- **Tauri** (`src-tauri/`): `lib.rs` registers the dialog, fs, oauth and opener plugins (the last two for the Strava
  import, and for the links of the app that open in a new tab), the six native encoder commands
  (`video.rs`), the two command-line commands (`cli.rs`), `opened_files` (`opened.rs`) and `system_stats` (`system.rs`), ten in all,
  declared in `build.rs` (`AppManifest::commands`):
  a command missing from this list or from `capabilities/default.json` is refused. `main` window
  1440 × 900 (at least 1024 × 700). `dragDropEnabled: false`: otherwise Tauri intercepts drops and the HTML no longer receives
  `drop`. Permissions (`capabilities/default.json`): `core:window:allow-destroy` and `dialog:allow-message` (closing, see
  "Mes projets"), `dialog:allow-open`, `dialog:allow-save`, `fs:allow-read-file`,
  `fs:allow-write-file`, `fs:allow-open`, `fs:allow-seek`, `fs:allow-write`, `fs:allow-remove` (film streamed
  to disk), `fs:allow-mkdir`, `fs:allow-read-dir`, `fs:allow-exists`, `fs:allow-rename` (offline packs, "Mes projets": a project is written to a `.tmp` file, then renamed), `allow-video-available`, `allow-video-sound`, `allow-video-open`,
  `allow-video-frame`, `allow-video-finish`, `allow-video-cancel` (native encoder), `allow-cli-render`, `allow-cli-exit`
  (command line), `allow-opened-files` (project files opened by the system), `allow-system-stats` (status bar), `oauth:allow-start`,
  `oauth:allow-cancel`, `updater:default` and `process:allow-restart` (see « Updates »), and `opener:allow-open-url` limited to `https://*` (the Strava authorization page, and the links of the app
  that open in a new tab, `src/platform/externalLinks.ts`); two fixed
  scopes, `$APPDATA/tiles` and `$APPDATA/projects` with their contents (`fs:scope`), in addition to the paths the dialog plugin adds for each
  chosen file and the two folders of a command-line render: nothing else is readable. CSP: `connect-src` /
  `img-src` list the tile hosts (`src/terrain/sources.ts`), Open-Meteo (archive and forecast), the two Overpass servers,
  plus `https://www.strava.com` (`connect-src` only, Strava import), `ipc:` and `blob:`; `style-src 'unsafe-inline'` with `dangerousDisableAssetCspModification:
  ["style-src"]` (Tauri would otherwise add a nonce that cancels `unsafe-inline`). **Any new source must also be added
  to the CSP in `tauri.conf.json`.**
- **Updates** (« Réglages » › « Mises à jour », desktop only, on demand only: no check at start-up, no polling, no silent install).
  Contract: two optional `Platform` methods, `checkForUpdate()` → `UpdateCheck` (never throws: `current`, `available { update:
  { version, date, notes } }`, `offline`, `unavailable`, `error { message }`) and `installUpdate(onProgress?)` (download, signature check,
  install, then `relaunch`); absent on the website, where `PreferencesPanel` and the « Sources et licences » line do not show them. `desktop.ts` imports
  `@tauri-apps/plugin-updater` and `@tauri-apps/plugin-process` dynamically (the web bundle never loads them) and keeps the `Update` of the last check
  for `installUpdate`. The pure part (`updater.ts`, tested with the plugins mocked in `updater.test.ts`) maps results and failures to the state of
  `UpdatesSection.tsx`: a rejection `plugin updater not found` (Tauri's answer for a plugin that is not registered) is `unavailable`, a network failure
  `offline`. The section refuses to install during an export or a batch (the restart does not go through `installCloseGuard`), writes the waiting autosave,
  and asks through `Platform.ask` first, naming unsaved changes of the open project. **Configuration is an overlay**: the base `tauri.conf.json` has neither
  `plugins.updater` nor `bundle.createUpdaterArtifacts`; `src-tauri/updater.conf.json` adds them (`createUpdaterArtifacts: true`, the public key,
  the endpoint `https://github.com/YannickRiou/openflyover/releases/latest/download/latest.json`, `windows.installMode: "passive"`) and is merged by
  `desktop.yml` (`--config`) only when the secret `TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY` exists, since a build with `createUpdaterArtifacts` fails without the key.
  **Conditional registration** (`lib.rs`, `setup`, `#[cfg(desktop)]`): `tauri_plugin_updater` and `tauri_plugin_process` are registered only when
  `app.config().plugins.0.contains_key("updater")`; the updater plugin requires `pubkey` in its configuration and would fail to initialise without it, so
  an app built without the overlay starts normally and the button says the updates are not available in this build. The two crates are desktop-only
  dependencies (`Cargo.toml`), `serde_json` is a direct dependency because `generate_context!` writes the `plugins` section as `serde_json` values.
  **Permissions**: `updater:default` (check, download, install, download_and_install) and `process:allow-restart`
  are in `capabilities/default.json` always: they are resolved at build time from the plugin crates, which are always compiled in, and an entry whose
  plugin is not registered is inert (the command rejects with `plugin updater not found`), so no second capability file is needed (checked with
  `cargo check` with and without the overlay). The request to GitHub is made by the Rust plugin, so the CSP (`connect-src`) needs no new host; the
  page only uses `ipc:`. Linux: only the AppImage updates itself. Setup, release and errors: `docs/installers.md`.
- **Verified**: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` passes (configuration, permissions, icons, `generate_context!`),
  with a fake resource compiler (`RC_x86_64_pc_windows_msvc`) since nothing is linked. On Linux, the check
  requires `libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev` and GLib ≥ 2.70 (Ubuntu 22.04 or later); the development machine (Ubuntu 20.04) does not
  have them.
- **Desktop installers** (`.github/workflows/desktop.yml`, instructions in `docs/installers.md`): `tauri-apps/tauri-action`
  on Windows (NSIS + MSI), macOS (`universal-apple-darwin`) and Ubuntu 22.04 (AppImage + deb), manually or on a
  `v*` tag (a `release` job creates one draft release, the three builds upload to it by `releaseId`, a `publish`
  job publishes it once they all succeed). Optional signing through the repository secrets: Windows, PFX imported into the store then
  thumbprint written to `src-tauri/tauri.windows.conf.json` (generated, never versioned; timestamping and SHA-256 in
  `tauri.conf.json`); macOS, Tauri's `APPLE_*` variables exported only if present (empty, they would make
  the build fail), ad hoc signing (`signingIdentity: "-"`) otherwise. Updates: see « Updates » below (optional update key, `TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY`).
  The installers are branded with the « Carte alpine » palette: images and licence pages live in `src-tauri/installer/` (build
  inputs, committed) and are generated by `scripts/installer-assets.mjs` (a real screenshot of the 3D view, `scripts/installer-hero.jpg`, composed in HTML in one
  headless Chromium, written as 24-bit BMP) and `scripts/installer-license.mjs` (MIT licence, reminder of the third-party
  licences grouped by licence from `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`, data-service attributions from `docs/sources.md`; RTF for the
  Windows installers and the DMG, plain text for the Debian copyright file). `tauri.conf.json` references them through
  `bundle.licenseFile`, `bundle.windows.nsis` / `wix`, `bundle.macOS.dmg` and `bundle.linux.deb.files`.
  `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, on pull requests and on master: `lint`, `test` (with coverage on master) and `build` jobs, in parallel (`build` runs `tsc -b`, which type checks both projects, then Vite); `cargo test` only when `src-tauri/` or the workflow changed (`rust` job, against a placeholder `dist/`); on master, a `badge` job publishes the line coverage on the `badges` branch for the README badge. `.github/workflows/pages.yml` rebuilds the website for GitHub Pages on every push to master (`BASE_PATH=/<repository>/`, see `docs/deploying.md`). Release: the same number in `package.json`, `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` and `src-tauri/Cargo.toml`, `npm run notices`, then a `v*` tag (`docs/installers.md`).

### Video export without WebCodecs (Linux)

WebKitGTK does not have `VideoEncoder`; Edge (WebView2) does, and so does WebKit on macOS since Safari 16.4. On the desktop without
WebCodecs, `capabilities.videoEncoder` is `'native'`: the film is encoded by the system `ffmpeg` (`apt install
ffmpeg`), started by Rust commands (`src-tauri/src/video.rs`); nothing to bundle, no process execution exposed to
JavaScript.

- **Commands** (async: they do not block the window), one session per export, in a `Mutex<Videos>`:
  `video_available` (`ffmpeg -version` started and successful), `video_sound` (binary body: the sound as WAV, written to the
  temporary folder → id), `video_open(path, width, height, fps, bitrate, keyframeInterval, audioBitrate, transparent,
  sound)` → id (checked by `Rate::checked`), `video_frame` (binary body:
  the RGBA image, id in the `x-video-session` header), `video_finish(id)` → file size, `video_cancel(id)`.
  Arguments fixed in Rust (`ffmpeg_args`, tested by `cargo test`): `-f rawvideo -pix_fmt rgba -s WxH -r fps -i -`
  [`-i son.wav`] `-c:v libx264 -preset medium -pix_fmt yuv420p`, colors converted and tagged BT.709
  (`-vf scale=out_color_matrix=bt709`, `-colorspace` / `-color_primaries` / `-color_trc bt709`) [`-c:a aac -b:a <audio>`]
  `-movflags +faststart -f mp4 -y chemin`. ffmpeg aims at the export's target bitrate like WebCodecs: `-b:v` target,
  `-maxrate` 1.5×, `-bufsize` 2×, `-g` key frame interval in frames. Name typed as `.webm` or codec VP9 (`is_webm`,
  `nativeCodecFor` on the JavaScript side): `-c:v libvpx-vp9 -deadline good -cpu-used 4 -row-mt 1` with the same rate
  flags, sound `-c:a libopus`, `-f webm` (tried with the ffmpeg of an Ubuntu install: VP9 + Opus read by `ffprobe`,
  44.1 kHz WAV resampled automatically). HEVC is not offered through ffmpeg.
- **Overlay alone** (`transparent`, see "Overlay only"): always WebM / VP9, whatever the name typed (MP4 / H.264 has
  no alpha), the same file as the web export: `-pix_fmt yuva420p` instead of `yuv420p` (in the list of
  `ffmpeg -h encoder=libvpx-vp9`); libvpx-vp9 encodes the alpha as a second stream, which the WebM muxer stores
  beside each frame (`alpha_mode` 1). `-auto-alt-ref` left alone: 2-pass only (same help), and the export is 1-pass.
  Not probed on the GPU (no hardware alpha). Tried with ffmpeg 6.1: synthetic RGBA frames (transparent, opaque and
  half-transparent areas), `ffprobe` shows `alpha_mode=1`, decoded back with `-c:v libvpx-vp9` as `yuva420p`: alpha
  0 / 255 / 129 for 0 / 255 / 128, every transparent pixel at 0.
- **H.264 on the GPU** (`h264_encoder`): before the first MP4 export of a run (outside the lock), ffmpeg encodes a
  tenth of a second of black (`probe_args`: `-f lavfi -i color=c=black:s=256x256:d=0.1 … -f null -`, 5 s at most,
  then killed) with `h264_nvenc`, then `h264_vaapi`; the first that succeeds is kept for the run (`OnceLock`, name
  written to stderr), `libx264` otherwise (not probed). A real encode, not `ffmpeg -encoders`: an ffmpeg built with
  NVENC or VAAPI may have no GPU or driver to run it. NVENC: `-c:v h264_nvenc -preset p5 -rc vbr` with the rate flags,
  `-pix_fmt yuv420p`; VAAPI: `-vaapi_device /dev/dri/renderD128` before the inputs, `-c:v h264_vaapi -rc_mode VBR` with
  the rate flags, filter `scale=out_color_matrix=bt709,format=nv12,hwupload`. A failure during an export with a GPU encoder is reported
  like any other (no retry on the processor in the middle of a file). WebM stays VP9 on the processor.
- **Path**: the path of the file chosen in the "Enregistrer" dialog at the start of the export (`WritableFile.path`, set
  by `openWritablePath`; batch render folder included). `video_open` refuses a relative path or one outside the fs
  scope (where the dialog plugin adds each chosen file). The handle opened by the dialog writes nothing; it is
  closed after `video_finish` (file kept) or deleted by `discard` (cancel, failure). Without a path (in-memory
  fallback), the export fails with a message.
- **Frames**: the export compositor draws each frame into a 2D canvas; `getImageData` → raw RGBA as the binary
  body of `invoke` (no JSON; 8.3 MB at 1080p, negligible next to the render). Back-pressure: `video_frame`
  writes to ffmpeg's standard input and only replies once the pipe has taken the frame; size checked.
- **Sound**: the film mix (`mixFilmAudio`, see "Video export") is converted to interleaved 16-bit WAV (`wavFile`)
  and passed before opening (`video_sound`), as ffmpeg's second input, encoded as AAC 192 kbit/s (Opus in WebM); temporary file
  deleted at the end of the session (success, failure, cancel) or if `video_open` fails.
- **Errors**: ffmpeg runs with `-loglevel error`; if it stops (write refused on its input) or ends in
  failure, the command returns "ffmpeg a échoué :" and its last three error lines, and deletes the partial
  file. `video_cancel` kills ffmpeg and deletes the file; no effect on a session that has already finished.
- **JavaScript side** (`src/export/nativeEncoder.ts`): `createNativeVideoEncoder` has the `VideoEncodeSession` contract
  (`addFrame`, `finish`, `cancel`); `createExportEncoder` picks it when `videoEncoder` is `'native'`, otherwise
  WebCodecs (`ExportController`); `exportCodec` replaces `pickCodec` in the drawer and the batch: MP4 / H.264 (WebM / VP9 for the
  overlay alone) if `video_available` answers yes (asked every time: ffmpeg installed while the application is running is detected), null
  otherwise, and the drawer says "installez ffmpeg" (install ffmpeg) (`videoEncoderMissingHint`). The Tauri invoke function is imported on
  first call: the website loads none of it.
- **Not yet**: no built-in
  fallback without ffmpeg (`openh264` crate + MP4 muxing in Rust, considered: H.264 "baseline", a few MB more).
  Rejected: `ffmpeg` as a sidecar (`bundle.externalBin`, 70 to 100 MB per platform, GPL license with x264); `rav1e` (AV1
  in pure Rust, too slow in 4K, less universal playback).
- **Verified**: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`; JavaScript tests with a fake invoke
  (`nativeEncoder.test.ts`); GPU probe without a GPU (both fail, `libx264` kept). To be checked on a Linux desktop
  (`cargo test`, real export, NVENC / VAAPI): the manual checks of `docs/handbook/index.html`.
- **Machine load** (`src-tauri/src/system.rs`): read-only command `system_stats` → used / total RAM and CPU %, for the
  status bar's performance readout (`sysinfo` 0.36, the last release for `rust-version` 1.77, `system` feature only;
  permission `allow-system-stats` in `capabilities/default.json`).

## Offline packs (phase 6)

"Préparer hors ligne" (prepare offline; Projet tab, "Hors ligne" section, `src/ui/OfflinePanel.tsx`) downloads once the tiles
of a flyover of the loaded tracks; the view and the export then read them without a network.

- **Plan** (`plan.ts`, pure): the tiles the engine loads during the flyover, with its own rule. A tile of
  size G is split when a cell (G / 64) exceeds 3 px: camera closer than r = G / 64 · H / (6 · tan 25°)
  ≈ 6 G for H = 1080 px (×2 in 4K); a split loads the four children. The follow camera stays near the
  track, at h = automatic distance × "distance" setting × sin(tilt) above it: a tile is split if
  √(d² + h²) < r, d = distance from the track to the tile (points densified at 25 m, 1 km grid). Traversal from the
  engine roots (same area: track box + 25 km, 40 km at least, ≤ 16 roots): the two levels below the
  roots over the whole area (the distant landscape), deeper only the children of the tiles that touch the
  corridor (2, 5 or 10 km wide, centered on the track). Outside the corridor the engine keeps the pack's coarser
  level (replacement waits for the four children, hence whole sets of children). Imagery: `planImagerySubtiles` of
  each terrain tile, same zoom offset as the view (cropping beyond the source's max zoom), so never finer than the
  device shows (`packImageryLevel`: « Fin » on a phone or tablet, said in the estimate). Estimate
  = number of tiles × average size measured per source (`policy.ts`). Order of magnitude, straight 20 km track in
  the Alps, Mapterhorn + IGN, offset 1, h = 400 m: 2 km → 10,500 tiles, ~450 MB; 5 km → 22,000, ~930 MB;
  10 km → 28,000, ~1.2 GB (Mapterhorn: ~150 KB per 512 px tile). Beyond 150,000 tiles, refused. Not in the
  plan: overview shots and high orbits (coarse levels, already there), 4K export (one more level in a wide
  corridor).
- **Policy** (`policy.ts`): one decision per source, reasons and links in `docs/sources.md`; a source missing from the
  table is refused. OpenTopoMap and swisstopo (photos and map) are allowed for personal use
  (`personalUse`, warning in the panel) with a low limit. Esri World Imagery is refused (its terms forbid offline
  copies). A refused imagery source does not prevent the pack: it
  then only contains the terrain, and the imagery stays online. Limit per day and per device: Mapterhorn 20,000,
  IGN 50,000 (all layers), swisstopo 10,000, OpenTopoMap 2,000; AWS unlimited.
- **Download** (`download.ts`): 4 requests at a time, `downloadTile` retries (the fetcher's), tiles already
  in the pack skipped, 4xx = "pas de donnée ici" (no data here; Mapterhorn beyond z12 outside fine areas), stop after 20 consecutive network
  failures or if the storage refuses a tile, pause (requests in progress finish), resume, cancel
  (new pack deleted). Daily counter per provider in `storage` (`openflyover.offline.quota.v1`).
- **Packs** (`packs.ts`, `store.ts`): identifier = fingerprint of the plan's URLs, so same tracks + same settings =
  same pack, and restarting it completes an incomplete pack. List in `storage` (`openflyover.offline.v1`: name, sources,
  corridor, tiles, bytes, complete, source URL prefixes). `installOfflineTiles()` (`main.tsx`) registers the
  fetcher's reader: `covers(url)` compares the URL with the packs' URL prefixes (synchronous, no waiting when no
  pack contains the source: same path as before), then `tileCache.get`. An unreadable or missing copy → network.
- **Storage** (`src/platform/tileCache.ts`): website = Cache Storage, one cache per pack `openflyover-tiles-v1:<pack>`,
  the URL as key (deleting a pack = deleting its cache; a tile shared by two packs is stored twice),
  `navigator.storage.persist()` requested on the first tile, `size()` = `navigator.storage.estimate()`; unavailable without
  HTTPS (`tileCache` null, the section says so). Desktop = one file per tile, `<app data>/tiles/<pack>/<tileFileName(url)>`
  (two 53-bit fingerprints), plugin-fs with `baseDir: AppData`; name index read once (`readDir`): a
  missing tile costs no disk call; image type detected from the first bytes.
- **Cost of cache-first**: desktop, nothing for a missing tile (in-memory index); website, one `match` per pack that
  contains the source (not measured here, for lack of a browser; negligible next to a 50 to 300 ms network request).
- **Limits**: offline, a tile outside the pack fails after the retries (the parent stays displayed); the export waits for
  these failures as it does online. The pack follows the track and the settings of the moment: changing the source or the zoom
  offset requires another pack.

## Installable web app and mobile (after v0.1.0)

Website only: none of this runs in the desktop app (`shouldRegisterServiceWorker`: never inside Tauri, never in
development, only in a secure context).

**Browser support** (MDN browser-compat-data 8.1.5, checked 10 October 2026):

| Feature | Safari iOS | Chrome Android | Firefox Android |
|---|---|---|---|
| `VideoEncoder` / `VideoDecoder` (WebCodecs) | 16.4 | 94 | no |
| `AudioEncoder` (sound of the film) | 26 | 94 | no |
| WebGL 2, `EXT_color_buffer_float` | 15 | 58 / 56 | yes |
| `OffscreenCanvas` | 16.4 | yes | yes |
| Manifest `display` | 11.3 | yes | yes |
| Manifest `share_target` | no | 76 | no |
| `showSaveFilePicker` | no | 132 | no |
| `navigator.share` with files (`canShare`) | 14 | 75 | no |
| Service worker | 11.3 | 40 | yes |
| `StorageManager.persist` | 15.2 | yes | yes |

iOS publishes no per-tab WebGL memory budget; Safari kills and reloads a page that holds too many textures (WebKit
bug 300782, October 2025): the device budget below is conservative, to be measured on real phones.

- **Manifest** (`public/manifest.webmanifest`): « OpenFlyover », `display: standalone`, theme `#1b2832` (top bar ink),
  background `#f6f3ec` (paper), icons rendered from `public/favicon.svg` (`icons/`: 192 and 512 px, a 512 px maskable
  one with a full-bleed background and the drawing at 72 %, a 180 px `apple-touch-icon`). Every URL is relative
  (`start_url`, `scope`, `id`: `./`), so the same file works under `/openflyover/` and at a root (tested,
  `webApp.test.ts`). `index.html` links it with `theme-color` and `apple-touch-icon` (Vite adds the base).
- **Service worker** (`src/platform/serviceWorker.js`, plain JS, emitted as `sw.js` by the `serviceWorker()` plugin of
  `vite.config.ts`, which writes in the precache list and two hashes; scope = the base):
  - precached at install, in `openflyover-shell-<hash of the build>`: the page (`./`), every build file (JS, CSS) and
    every public file except `oauth-callback.html` (fonts, sample, icons, manifest): ~3.7 MB, 50-odd files. The whole
    app works offline, the 3D scene and the export included;
  - cached on first use, in `openflyover-sky-<hash of their content>`: the sky and cloud textures (`atmosphere/`,
    `clouds/`, ~12 MB), downloaded only when the atmosphere is first shown, not at install over a mobile connection;
    the cache name follows their content, so it survives app updates (the textures change with the Takram packages
    only);
  - never: another origin (tiles, Open-Meteo, Overpass, Strava: their requests are not even seen by the worker; tiles
    have their own cache, the offline packs, under their terms) nor the Strava callback page;
  - navigation to the app: the precached page (any query), so it opens offline; the precache answers before the
    network, `ignoreVary` (servers send `Vary: Origin`, module scripts carry an `Origin`);
  - update: a new build changes `sw.js` (its hash); the browser looks for it at each navigation, and an open app every
    hour and when shown again, at most once per 10 min, never while hidden (`watchForUpdates`, `registration.update()`:
    an installed app may stay open for days); it installs it in the background, the app shows
    « Nouvelle version disponible » › « Recharger » (`webApp.ts`: `SKIP_WAITING`, reload on `controllerchange`); on
    activation the worker deletes the older `openflyover-shell-` / `openflyover-sky-` caches only, never the offline
    packs (`openflyover-tiles-v1:`) nor « Mes projets » (`openflyover-projects-v1`).
  Verified in headless Chromium (Pixel 5 emulation, `vite preview` under `/openflyover/` and at `/`): manifest without
  errors, no installability error (`Page.getInstallabilityErrors`), worker active, second load with the server stopped
  served from the cache. Update while open (Pixel 5 emulation, `sw.js` changed on the server): no request
  when shown again within the gap, one past it (clock moved forward), then the toast and « Recharger » reloads on the
  new worker.
- **iOS install hint**: iOS has no install prompt; once per device, in a Safari tab (`navigator.standalone` false), a
  message says « Pour installer OpenFlyover : touchez Partager, puis « Sur l'écran d'accueil ». » (toast, « Compris »).
- **Files in**: Android (installed app): `share_target` (POST, multipart, field `tracks`, `.gpx`, `.fit` and the MIME
  types apps use, `application/octet-stream` included: Garmin and Komoot share FIT / GPX under it). The worker answers
  the POST to `share-target`: files kept in the `openflyover-share` cache, then a 303 to `./?partage`; the page
  (`takeSharedFiles`) reads them, empties the cache, removes the query and opens them as « Ouvrir » does. A name
  without extension gets one from the content (`sharedFileName`: ".FIT" at byte 8, `<gpx`). iOS has no share target:
  the file picker. On iOS, the track picker has no `accept` filter (`pickerAccept`): Safari greys out `.gpx` / `.fit`
  files whose type it does not know; the import checks the extension. Verified in headless Chromium: a multipart form
  posted to `share-target` (file without extension) opens the app with the track.
- **Files out**: where `showSaveFilePicker` is missing on a touch screen (iOS, Android before Chrome 132),
  `capabilities.sharesFiles`: `saveFile` / `saveUrl` hand the file to `navigator.share({ files })` when `canShare`
  takes it (iOS: « Enregistrer la vidéo » into Photos, « Enregistrer dans Fichiers »); the sheet closed = nothing
  saved; a file the sheet does not take (Chrome Android refuses `.json`) or a refusal (no user gesture left) =
  the download as before. The share sheet needs the click: called from a button (« Enregistrer », « Enregistrer à
  nouveau… », the toast action), never at the end of an export. Label: `shareSaveLabel()` (« Partager /
  Enregistrer »). Computers keep the download. Sound: without `AudioEncoder` (iOS before 26) the film is exported
  without sound and the result says « sans le son : ce navigateur ne sait pas l'encoder » (existing path). No
  WebCodecs (Firefox Android): `videoEncoderMissingHint` points to Chrome (Android) or Safari 16.4+ on a touch screen.
- **Persistence**: `navigator.storage.persist()` is requested once, on the first offline tile or the first « Mes
  projets » save (`tileCache.ts`, `projectLibrary.ts`), on every browser: iOS 15.2+ may then keep them; an app added
  to the home screen is exempt from Safari's 7-day eviction of script-written storage.

### Device budget (`src/core/deviceBudget.ts`)

Read once (`deviceBudget()`), where each limit lives. Phone = touch screen as the main pointer (`(pointer: coarse)`)
and short side < 600 CSS px; tablet = touch screen, larger; computer = everything else, values strictly unchanged
(tested). `navigator.deviceMemory` ≤ 2 GB (Chrome only) lowers a touch device further.

| Limit | Where | Computer | Tablet | Phone | ≤ 2 GB |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| pixel ratio cap / while playing or touching | `FlyoverCanvas` (`dpr`, `MovingPixelRatio`) | 2 / 2 | 2 / 1.5 | 2 / 1.5 | 1.5 / 1 |
| decoded tile bitmaps (LRU) | `createTileFetcher` | 600 | 200 | 200 | 120 |
| height grids (256 px equivalents) | `DEFAULT_TUNING.heightCacheEntries` | 400 | 160 | 160 | 100 |
| terrain tiles loaded or loading (LOD gives way past it) / loading at once | `DEFAULT_TUNING.maxResidentTiles` / `maxConcurrentLoads` | unbounded / 24 | 160 / 12 | 120 / 6 | 90 / as the device |
| finest imagery (zoom offset) | `engineOptionsFromSettings`, offline packs (`packImageryLevel`) | « Très fin » (2) | « Fin » (1) | « Fin » (1) | « Fin » |
| preview clouds: resolution, still frames | `CloudsLayer`, `CLOUD_SETTLE_FRAMES` | 1, 32 | 0.5, 16 | 0.5, 16 | 0.5, 16 |
| sea of clouds by default | `DEFAULT_CLOUDS.seaRender` | volume | « Nappe » | « Nappe » | « Nappe » |
| export short side | `videoSize` | 2160 (4K) | 1440 | 1080 | as the device |
| shadow map (texels per side) | `AtmosphereLayer` | 4096 | 2048 | 2048 | 1024 |
| terrain LOD height during an export | `exportLodHeight` (`TerrainLayer`, `ExportController`) | the film's | the film's | min(film, 720) | as the device |
| video bitrate ceiling, film kept in memory | `videoBitrate` / `deviceBitrateCap` | none | 12 Mbit/s | 8 Mbit/s | as the device |
| « in memory » size warning | `warnsInMemory` | 1.5 GB | 250 MB | 250 MB | 250 MB |
| default export resolution | `DEFAULT_VIDEO_SETTINGS` | 1080p | 1080p | 720p | as the device |

« Nappe » is one draw call, no ray march: far cheaper than the volumetric sea. Lens effects stay off by default (as on
a computer). A larger export class comes out at the cap (`videoSize`), with `exportResolutionNote` to show next to the
choice. "Touching" = fingers moving the 3D view (`watchTouchGesture`, `scene/touchGesture.ts`: a finger past 6 px, a tap
does not count), until 400 ms after the last one lifts (the controls' damping; a new touch in between keeps the lower
ratio, so one drop and one return per gesture); the mouse never changes it. Verified in headless Chromium: iPhone 15
emulation (DPR 3) draws at 2, 1.5 while playing, 2 again on pause; 1.5 during a one-finger orbit and a pinch, 2 again
after release, one change each way, a tap changes nothing; a desktop viewport at DPR 2 stays at 2, mouse drag
included. Not measured: memory and frame time on a real phone.

#### Mobile memory budget (iPhone 12, 4 GB: the tab is killed by iOS, not reloaded by the app)

No code reloads the page: iOS ends a WebContent process that uses too much memory (Jetsam) and the browser reloads the
tab; the automatic reopening of the last project (see "Library") hides it. The figures are estimates, not measured.

| What | Before | Now on a phone |
|---|---|---|
| terrain shadow map | 4096², ~128 MB, redrawn every frame | 2048², ~32 MB (1024², ~8 MB with ≤ 2 GB) |
| MSAA of the default framebuffer | ~75 MB at 1080p, never read under the composer (`multisampling={0}` + SMAA) | none when the context is created with the atmosphere on (`canvasAntialias`); without the atmosphere the scene is drawn straight to it, MSAA stays |
| tiles resident during an export | LOD evaluated for the film's height (1080, or 1920 in 9:16) | for 720 px at most: the preview's tiles (`exportLodHeight`; the texture of a tile is then up to 1.5× coarser than "3 px" at 1080p) |
| tiles kept loaded during an export | wall-clock retention (2 s view, 30 s prefetch), no cap: +1.2 tiles per film frame measured on an iPhone 12 (68 → 244 in 100 frames), tab killed | counted in film frames (6 / 30), prefetch +5, +10, +20, hard cap of 120 loaded or loading tiles (160 tablet, 90 with ≤ 2 GB): ~310 MB of tiles at most |
| tiles a single view needs | the selection was never evicted: a low portrait view looking at the horizon held 160–224 tiles (iPhone 12, « descente »), tab killed | the error target grows by ×1.1 steps until the view fits the cap (`lodFactor`): distant tiles coarser, the ground under the camera unchanged |
| tiles loading at once | 24 (each with its fetches, decoded bitmaps, DEM pixels and canvas): ~94 pending at once on an iPhone 12 | 6 (12 tablet, `maxTileLoads`) |
| film kept in RAM (iOS: no direct disk write) | 1080p30 « Haute » ~16 Mbit/s = 2 MB/s, peak 2.5–3× the film at the end (`BufferTarget`'s growing buffer, `slice`, `Blob`) | ≤ 8 Mbit/s = 1 MB/s; « Débit vidéo » shows the real figure and « plafonné » |
| imagery canvases (512² to 1024²+ each, ~1–4 MB of JS memory per resident tile) | kept as long as the tile lived | released as soon as the texture is uploaded (`texture.onUpdate`: 0×0, reference dropped); a new WebGL context redraws them (`restoreEngineImagery`) |
| warning on the size estimate | 1.5 GB | 250 MB |
| autosave of a project with a 40 MB clip | every change: whole document serialised as one string (the clip as base64, ~55 MB), UTF-8 copy for the size, `Response` copy: ~4–6× the project, 3 s after each change | `serializeProjectBlob`: the JSON of each medium is made once per table entry (`WeakMap`) and kept as a Blob the next documents reuse; size from `Blob.size`, `Response(blob)` streams to the cache; only the header and the tracks are serialised again |
| reopening a project with a 40 MB clip at start-up | the document read as a string (~55 MB), a UTF-8 copy for the `File`, a second string at `file.text()`, the parsed object: ~3–4× the project | `load` returns a `Blob`: the `File` shares it, one string is made at `file.text()` and freed after `JSON.parse`: ~2× the project at peak |
| export default | 1080p | 720p (`DEFAULT_VIDEO_SETTINGS`; a project already saved keeps its own) |

Imagery canvases are released once uploaded (`releaseCanvas`, `terrain/imagery.ts`: 0×0, `texture.image = null`, `userData.released`). After `webglcontextrestored`, `TerrainLayer` calls `restoreEngineImagery(engine)`: each tile whose texture was released goes grey (and counts as « missing » for an export frame), is drawn again by `loadImageryTexture` through the fetcher's cache, and gets its new texture; a failed redraw stays grey until the next restore. Tiles never drawn keep their canvas until their first draw.

A phone that exports also holds the **screen on** (`export/guard.ts`: Screen Wake Lock, asked again when the page returns
to the foreground, released at the end, on cancel and on error; no API = no lock). While an export is busy a flag is
kept in the tab's `sessionStorage`; if the next start finds it, the export never ended and a toast says so
(« Le dernier export a été interrompu… »), with a « Détails » button showing the **breadcrumb**: the last `pagehide` /
`freeze` / `webglcontextlost` with `renderer.info.memory` textures and geometries and the loaded tile count. iOS kills a
tab short of memory without any page event (the counters also give the tiles still loading or queued, « N en attente »: a plateau of « tuiles » below the cap is what the tile budget aims at), so the export also writes its own steps (start, video size applied, encoder
ready with its codec and bitrate, the first 3 rendered frames then 1 in 10 from 50 to 200, where an iPhone 12 died, and
1 in 25 elsewhere: `isExportStep`; finalisation); the last `MAX_STEPS` (25) are kept as a list, each with the JS heap
where `performance.memory` exists (Chrome), and each export starts with a fresh list. While a film renders, the export
adds its own counters to every breadcrumb (`setExportProbe`, page events included): loaded and pending tiles read live
from the engine, the bitmaps of the fetcher's LRU and their approximate MB (`cachedBytes`, RGBA of each), the decoded
height grids (`engine.caches()`), the frames « en vol » (handed to the VideoEncoder minus packets out, counted with
mediabunny's `onEncodedSample` / `onEncodedPacket`) and its `encodeQueueSize` (« file », read through mediabunny's
internal encoder wrapper, left out if it changes), the re-drapes run by the export's `flushDrapes` since the previous
step; a step line also gives the time since the one before (« +11,0 s »). « Export de test sans encodage » (checkbox
under « Diagnostic », Réglages tab; the tab's sessionStorage only, `noEncodingTest`, never in the project) runs a film
export with no encoder: every frame is rendered and composed, none is handed over, the file is discarded and the export
ends on the error « Export de test terminé sans fichier (n images rendues) »; the report's Export section then says
« Encodage : désactivé (test) ». It tells, on a phone, the encoder's memory from the render's (the manual checks of docs/handbook/index.html).
The **diagnostic report** (`export/diagnostic.ts`, pure and tested: `buildReport`, `formatReport`) is taken at the same
busy transition, under `openflyover.diagnostic`: app version (`__APP_VERSION__`, vite.config.ts), device and its budget,
GPU (`setGpuProbe`, registered by the scene like the breadcrumb probe, read once), the export request (size, quality,
`effectiveRender`, capped `videoBitrate`, memory or disk, frames, estimated size) and the settings (`summariseSettings` +
`settingsDetails`, plus labels, landmarks, marker, track glow, grading, tracks and points, media of the film by kind and
size, overlay widgets). No coordinate and no track, place, file or project name. « Détails » of the toast and
« Diagnostic › Afficher le rapport » (Réglages tab, `currentReport`: the report made now plus the last export and its
steps) open `DiagnosticDialog` (« Copier le rapport », the text selected when the clipboard refuses). Both entries stay
under ~16 KB of sessionStorage (values cut at 200 characters, steps at 80).

The muxer's copies at the end of an in-memory export are gone: the encoder writes through a chunked `StreamTarget` whose chunks
become `Blob` parts as they arrive (`memoryStream`), and the film is the `Blob` of those parts, never one concatenated buffer
(the old `BufferTarget` held its working buffer, a `slice` copy and the `Blob` copy at the same time, about 3× the file). The
MP4 layout is unchanged (`fastStart: 'in-memory'`, index at the start). Still to confirm on a real iPhone: the peak while the
muxer's samples move into the parts.

Not done, on purpose: the canvas is created once: a phone that starts with the atmosphere off keeps the MSAA, one that
starts with it on and turns it off later draws the plain scene without it.
